# meshio++ roadmap

Status at time of writing: **v20.2.0**, ABI 46. What ships is listed where it is documented rather than counted here, where counts went stale: the formats in [supported formats](./formats.md) and the [conformance matrix](./conformance.md), the operations in [architecture](./architecture.md#the-operations-layer), the language surfaces in the [C API](./c_api.md), [Fortran](./fortran.md), [Julia](./julia.md), [R](./r.md), [Octave / MATLAB](./octave.md) and [WebAssembly](./wasm.md) pages.

This document lists what is *not* built. Nothing here duplicates shipped functionality; where a feature partially exists, the shipped half is named and the gap is stated explicitly. Release history lives in [`CHANGELOG.md`](https://github.com/loumalouomega/meshioplusplus/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md), not here.

Effort key: **S** = days, **M** = a couple of weeks, **L** = a month or more, **XL** = a project in its own right. An item names a **probe** — the failing test that proves the gap — wherever one is cheap.

## How this file works

- **Sections are ordered, and the order is the recommendation.** Each section states what belongs in it; an item that sounds exciting does not move up for that reason. An empty section is removed, not kept as a placeholder.
- **A closed item is removed, not struck through.** Its history is the `CHANGELOG.md` entry and the feature's own `doc/` page; a partly closed item is narrowed to what remains (the `AGENTS.md` change checklist rule).
- **Every level of the outline is numbered**, so a gap can be cited in an issue, a commit or a release note: a group of work is `section.item` (a `###`/`####` heading, or a bold label line), the work under it `section.item.subitem`, and a bullet under that `section.item.subitem.subsub`. Lists that record a decision rather than open work — *Considered, not queued*, a section's *Landing an item* steps and the [non-goals](#non-goals-and-decisions-taken) — stay unnumbered, because a number here means work to do. The numbering follows the sections, so a new section inserted at the top renumbers the rest, as the rule above says, and so does a section removed once its last item closes (Performance, October 2026: what was §4 onward moved up one; and again when reader and writer parity closed, the same month: operations, ecosystem reach and the full WebAssembly build became §3 to §5). The long-run section closed the same month as the last of its spikes landed ([findings](./spikes.md)); being last, it renumbered nothing. Mesh generation (§6) closed the same month, also last: its native items landed, its measured-and-parked notes moved to [mesh generation](./mesh_cad.md#measured-and-parked) with their numbers and its references to [mesh generation's further reading](./mesh_cad.md#further-reading), its decisions to the [non-goals](#non-goals-and-decisions-taken), and its remaining dependencies to the sections they wait on (2.3, 3.3.1, 3.4), then 2.3, 3.2.1 and 3.3. The full WebAssembly build (§5) closed the same month, also last: its constrained primitive fitting landed (`fit_cad`'s `constrain_primitives`), what it measured and left of skin to CAD moved to [fit CAD's measured and parked notes](./fit_cad.md#measured-and-parked), its decisions to the [non-goals](#non-goals-and-decisions-taken), and MMG's port to the browser to the section it waits on (3.3.8, then 3.2.8). Generation (§3.1) closed the same month: its primitive constructors landed on every surface, with full-Lagrange `order=2` and the VTK Lagrange orders, the hand-rolled test helpers it left behind and why moved to [parametric primitives](./primitives.md#test-helpers-left-hand-rolled), and its *Deliberately not* list to the [non-goals](#non-goals-and-decisions-taken); what was 3.2 to 3.4 became 3.1 to 3.3. Selection (§3.1) closed the same month, when [`select`](./select.md) (3.1.3) landed on every surface beside the [`find_intersections`](./find_intersections.md) and [`thickness`](./thickness.md) it was listed with; what was 3.2 and 3.3 became 3.1 and 3.2. Volume mesh improvement (§3.2) closed the same month, when `optimize_volume` gained its worst-cell objective (3.2.1: guarded relocation, edge and multi-face removal, worst-cell smoothing), measured on Netgen's output in [optimize_volume](./optimize_volume.md#measured); being last, it renumbered nothing. The operations section (§3) closed with it, when MMG reached the full WebAssembly artifact (3.1.8: [MMG in the full artifact](./wasm.md#mmg-in-the-full-artifact), statically and without ELAS, its size measured there): ecosystem reach became §3, its items 4.1 to 4.7 now 3.1 to 3.7. In November 2026 an audit of the library against its own decisions, its performance and its ParaView plugin turned up two defects in the plugin, which opened *Correctness debts* as §1, so format reach, quality and ecosystem reach became §2 to §4 (what was 1.2.11 is now 2.2.11); and it appended Performance, the ParaView plugin and New capabilities as §5 to §7, the last taking the operators format reach's *Considered, not queued* had listed and the decisions recent work reopened. In November 2026 a review of the published feature list of ScanRuler, an AGPL-3.0 scan-inspection and reverse-engineering tool, appended *Scan metrology and reverse engineering* as §8 (ideas only; [non-goals](#non-goals-and-decisions-taken)); being last, it renumbered nothing. New capabilities (§7) closed the same month, when its last item, the hexahedra of creased scans (7.9.2), was measured and parked in [octree_mesh](./octree_mesh.md#measured-and-parked) as a limit of the output after neither a smoothed skin nor a boundary let off it removed the cells, and 7.9.1 turned `model_tolerances` and `virtual_faces` on in v19.0.0: scan metrology became §7, its items 8.1 to 8.9 now 7.1 to 7.9. In November 2026 the first measure of duplication by place (`tools/dup_report.py`, while SonarCloud's first analysis was pending) appended *Code duplication* as §8, and SonarCloud's first analysis, the same day, *SonarCloud findings* as §9; being last, they renumbered nothing. The shared-utilities pass the same month closed 8.1, 8.5.2, 8.7.3, 8.8.1 to 8.8.6, 8.9.2 and 8.9.4: what was 8.2 to 8.11 became 8.1 to 8.10, their subitems renumbered within them. The binding pass the same month closed 8.1.2 to 8.1.4, 8.2.3, 8.3.1, 8.6.1, 8.8.1 and 8.8.2 and narrowed the rest: 8.3.2 became 8.3.1, 8.6.2 became 8.6 and 8.8.3 became 8.8.1; the sections kept their numbers. The third pass closed the rest but three, which became 8.1 (OpenCASCADE, what was 8.7.1), 8.2 (the subset and clean gather, what was left of 8.8.1) and 8.3 (installed headers, what was 8.9). The SonarCloud pass the same month worked through 9.1 to 9.4 as the first analysis had listed them and left the three things only the next scan settles, now 9.1 to 9.3. Scan metrology (§7) closed the same month, when its last items landed: the profile solids (7.7.4) through a CAD provider and the viewer's measuring mode (7.9.1), after the fits, elements and GD&T, alignment, deviation, thickness, sections to sketches, the reverse-engineering loop, scan editing and the MCP prompts; booleans with fitted solids (7.8.7) went to the [non-goals](#non-goals-and-decisions-taken), and its *Considered, not queued* list stays in the git history with the section. Code duplication and SonarCloud findings became §7 and §8, the latter's items 9.1 to 9.3 now 8.1 to 8.3. Since then a heading other pages link to carries an explicit anchor without its number (`{#awaiting-a-licensed-run}`, `{#quality-of-implementation}`), so a renumbering no longer breaks those links: link to the anchor, and cite the number in the text. The ParaView pass the same month closed *Correctness debts* (§1) and the ParaView plugin (§6): its payload layer closed 1.2, and 1.1 turned out to be backwards, the permutation meshio++ applied to linear wedges on the way to VTK being the defect (removed library-wide, ABI 44); the plugin's items 6.1 to 6.11 landed, 6.3.2 as a library change to the VTK writers. Format reach, quality, ecosystem reach and performance became §1 to §4 (what was 2.2.11 is now 1.2.11, 3.4 is 2.4, 4.1 is 3.1, 5.1 is 4.1), code duplication and SonarCloud findings §5 and §6 (8.1 to 8.3 now 6.1 to 6.3). A review of the whole library the same month, of its formats and operations against what its own pages say they leave out, of its tooling against what the workflows and the build actually run, and of the [non-goals](#non-goals-and-decisions-taken) against what has shipped since each was decided, extended the sections in place (1.3 and 1.4, 2.6 to 2.17, 3.11 to 3.15, 4.12 to 4.15) and appended *Data model* as §7 and *Operations* as §8; being last, they renumbered nothing. It reopened five decisions, each narrowed where it is recorded and queued under the number named there, corrected the item numbers earlier renumberings had left behind in cross-references (which the test now listed as 3.16.2 would have caught), and marked every item it estimated from a `doc/` page alone *verify first*. A second pass the same month put code anchors on every open item, so that taking one up needs no planning pass ([landing checklists](#landing-checklists)), and corrected against the code what the first had written from the pages: among others that CGNS polyhedra need no cgnslib, that the THREADS backend shipped in v19.0.0, that the R package cannot build on CRAN as it stands, and that the MMG and Netgen add-on wheels carry no LGPL text. It found that Ansys `.rst` records flagged both sparse forms (then 1.1.2) are decoded silently as one of them, which reopened *Correctness debts* as §1: format reach, quality, ecosystem reach, performance, code duplication, SonarCloud findings, data model and operations became §2 to §9, every item keeping its place within its section (what was 2.6 is 3.6, 8.1 is 9.1), except that the two remaining verification items of 1.1 became 2.1.1 and 2.1.2 and the chatbot's four parts became 4.4.1 to 4.4.5. The first slice of format reach closed the same month: the cgnslib reader reads every zone (what was 2.3.1.1) and OpenFOAM's compressed cases are read and written (what was 2.3.5.1), so 2.3.1.2 to 2.3.1.7 became 2.3.1.1 to 2.3.1.6 and 2.3.5.2 and 2.3.5.3 became 2.3.5.1 and 2.3.5.2, with 2.3.5.3 the compressed write's remaining surfaces. An audit by role the same month, of helpers that do one job under different spellings, which neither duplication detector can match, and of the instruction baseline against the code of its heaviest rows, reopened code duplication (§6) with 6.1 to 6.8 and extended performance (§5) with 5.1.5 and 5.17 to 5.22, the last a study of flat hash and sorted-vector containers against the standard ones; nothing was renumbered, and 5.3 to 5.15 stay unused because the [non-goals](#non-goals-and-decisions-taken) cite them. The same month two sections that are not library features were appended at the maintainer's request, *Educational animation with manim* as §13 and *The paper* as §14; being last, they renumbered nothing, and they read a third-party package (ManimCommunity, for §13, whose API is marked † where it was not run) and third parties' submission rules, so their anchors outside this tree are marked where they were not opened. §13 was first read against a ManimGL fork that only served to hand the code over, and was rewritten the same month against ManimCommunity; its item numbers were renumbered in the rewrite (the fork's mobject items became 13.3.1 to 13.3.6 here). Also in November 2026 a review of VTK's mesh-aware math (Kitware's *Math on Meshes* post, the mesh-aware functions of VTK's Python `numpy_interface`, `vtkCellDerivatives`, `vtkMatrixMathFilter`, `vtkCellQuality` and the CellGrid filters) against what meshio++ ships appended *Math on meshes* as §15; being last, it renumbered nothing. Its first item to land, the cell-measure quality metrics (15.4), closed the same month and renumbered the items after it within §15 (what was 15.5 to 15.11 became 15.4 to 15.10). The flow quantities of the gradient closed with helicity, and the unary tensor algebra of 15.2 closed as well, the same month: 15.1 (flow quantities) is gone, and what was 15.2 to 15.10 became 15.1 to 15.9. The elementwise functions and the tuple functions of `data_calc` (what were 15.2.1 and 15.2.2) closed later the same month and renumbered that item's steps (what was 15.2.4 is 15.2.2). Roadmap 15.1, the separate `tensor_math` operation, was closed by extending `data_calc` with `matmul`, `outer` and `inv` instead, since the calculator already evaluated `dot`, `cross`, `transpose` and `mag`; what was 15.2 to 15.9 became 15.1 to 15.8, and the calculator is now 15.1. Its coordinates step (what was 15.1.1) closed the same month, so its mesh-aware calls are 15.1.1. The Verdict metrics (what was 15.2) closed later the same month; what was 15.3 to 15.8 became 15.2 to 15.7. Its mesh-aware calls (what was 15.1.1) closed after it, which finished the `data_calc` item (15.1): what was 15.2 to 15.7 became 15.1 to 15.6. Streamlines (what was 15.4) landed in the core, Python, both CLIs and the MCP server, and keeps only its flat bindings, pipeline step and notebook; the time statistics and derivatives (what was 15.5) closed as Python-only layers, and the per-region statistics (what was 15.5) closed last as a Python-only `data_statistics` beside `data_info`, which stays unchanged; being last, it renumbered nothing. The operator export and the field smoothing landed in the core as `mesh_operator` and `smooth_field` and narrowed 15.2 to its tetrahedra (15.2.1), flat bindings (15.2.2) and the DEC spike (15.2.3), without renumbering anything. Mass properties (9.4) landed on every surface and left §9 without renumbering: 9.5 to 9.10 keep their numbers because other pages cite them. Later in November 2026 an independent review against live SonarCloud metrics, `tools/dup_report.py`, the tier S/XL benchmarks and unprofiled hot paths extended the existing sections in place (1.2, 2.3.10, 2.4.9, 3.11.5, 3.24, 4.19, 4.20, 5.23 to 5.28, 6.1 to 6.4, 7.1 refreshed and 7.5, 9.11 to 9.16, 15.5 and 15.6), renumbering nothing.
- **Defect-shaped items go in a *Correctness debts* section at the top, regardless of size** — behaviour that loses data, mis-orients cells, does not terminate on valid input or fails silently is not a feature request, even when the fix and the feature are the same work. The section is removed when its last item closes, which renumbers the rest, and the next defect found opens it again as §1.
- **An item estimated from a doc, a `.d.ts` or a changelog alone says so** ("verify first") and names its probe; the code has repeatedly been more or less capable than its description.
- **[Non-goals](#non-goals-and-decisions-taken) record decisions already taken**, with their reasons, so they are not re-proposed as gaps.
- **Every open item is written to be picked up without a planning pass** (November 2026). It states what exists today with the place in the code that proves the gap, what to reuse, ordered steps numbered so one can be closed alone, tests and fixtures, and a *done when* probe; a design-pass item lists the decisions to take and the option the code favours. Line numbers are those of `master` in November 2026 and drift: each is given beside a function, option or message name, which is what to search for. A claim an item could not check in the code says *verify first*.
- **A priority is optional and only for sections that need to be ordered against each other** (§10 to §14 do not use one; their order is the sequencing's). Where an item carries one: **P0** blocks correctness or a release, **P1** the next release, **P2** planned, **P3** opportunistic or a spike. It never replaces the effort letter.
- **GPL and AGPL implementations (Gmsh's sources, ScanRuler) inform an item only through their documented behaviour**; no code is read from them or ported ([non-goals](#non-goals-and-decisions-taken)).

### Landing checklists {#landing-checklists}

Two lists the items cite instead of repeating. They are what the most recent format (`dxf`) and operation (`silhouette`, `fit_curves`) commits touched; `AGENTS.md` holds the rules behind them. Sources and tests are globbed by `CMakeLists.txt`, so neither needs a CMake edit.

**Checklist F: a format, or a new direction or construct of one.**

1. Core: `src/cpp/include/meshioplusplus/formats/<fmt>.hpp` and `src/cpp/src/formats/<fmt>.cpp`; header counts and tokens checked with `detail/parse_guard.hpp`; numbers through `detail/fast_number.hpp` and streams through `detail/classic_stream.hpp`; parameters through `WriteOptions` and `ReadOptions`.
2. Registry: one row in `registry_rows()` of `src/cpp/src/registry.cpp` and the extension in its defaults table; for an optional dependency, the row inside the feature `#ifdef` and the library's name in `registry_compiled_out`.
3. Python binding: `core_def_read`/`core_def_write` in `bindings/python/_core.cpp` (and a `__has_<lib>__` flag for an optional dependency).
4. Python twin: `src/python/meshioplusplus/<fmt>/__init__.py` (`register_format`, shims through `_shims.plain_shims` or `core_declined`) and `_<fmt>.py`; export in the package `__init__.py` (`__all__` and the `TYPE_CHECKING` imports); `python tools/gen_format_table.py`.
5. Tests: `tests/cpp/test_<fmt>.cpp`, `tests/python/test_<fmt>.py` (both engines, `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_STRICT_CORE=1` for the core), a `SPEC` entry in `tests/python/conformance_spec.py` then `python tools/gen_conformance_table.py`, a seed in `tests/fuzz/seed_main.cpp`, and each reader crash fix's input under `tests/fuzz/regressions/<fmt>/`.
6. Fixtures: `tests/python/meshes/<fmt>/` with a README, the `.gitattributes` rule, and for third-party files the `CITATION.cff` paragraph and a `CHANGELOG.md` line; never a GPL fixture.
7. Docs: `doc/formats/<fmt>.md` (*Reading*, *Writing*, what is not read), `doc/formats.md`, the sidebar in `doc/.vitepress/config.mts`, and the format lists of `doc/c_api.md`, `doc/wasm.md` and the other binding pages when the registry changes; the MCP server's format capabilities (`mcp/_tools.py`, `tests/python/test_mcp.py`, `doc/mcp.md`).
8. Generated and release files: `tools/amalgamate.sh`; an [ABI review](./abi_reviews.md) row for a new or changed installed header; `CHANGELOG.md`; `README.md`; a notebook cell in `example/python/` for a user-facing capability; narrow or remove the item here.

**Checklist O: an operation.**

1. Core: `src/cpp/include/meshioplusplus/operations/<op>.hpp` (`<Op>Options` with `m`-prefixed members, `<Op>Result {Mesh mMesh; …; std::vector<std::string> mWarnings;}`, `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_API <Op>Result <op>(const Mesh&, const <Op>Options&)`) and `src/cpp/src/operations/<op>.cpp`, through the uniform mesh API, `parallel_for` for independent loops, shared helpers under `src/cpp/src/detail/` recorded in [common utilities](./common_utilities.md).
2. Python: `m.def("<op>", …)` in `bindings/python/_core.cpp`; `src/python/meshioplusplus/_<op>.py` (the core call, `core_op_declined` before a numpy twin, or `_core_op` for a core-only operation); three places in the package `__init__.py` (`TYPE_CHECKING`, `_LAZY_ATTRS`, `__all__`).
3. C: `mio_<op>_opts` (size in its comment, a reserved tail), `mio_<op>_opts_init`, `mio_<op>_report`, `mio_<op>`, `mio_<op>_warnings` in `bindings/c/include/meshioplusplus/meshioplusplus.h` and `bindings/c/c_api.cpp` (the `capi_guarded_*` helpers and a `static_assert` on the struct size).
4. The flat bindings: Fortran (`bindings/fortran/meshioplusplus.f90`: the `public` list, the `bind(c)` type, the wrapper); Julia (`src/libmio.jl` struct mirror, `src/operations.jl`, the export list in `src/MeshioPlusPlus.jl`); R (`R/meshioplusplus.R`, `src/ops.c`, `src/init.c`, `NAMESPACE`, `man/mio_<op>.Rd`); Octave (`inst/+mio/<op>.m`, `src/ops_core.c`; `tests/python/test_octave_binding_parity.py` holds it to R); WASM (`bindings/wasm/js_bindings.cpp`, `src/wasm/src/index.mjs` twice, `src/wasm/index.d.ts`; `tests/python/test_wasm_parity.py` holds it to the C header).
5. CLIs: `src/python/meshioplusplus/_cli/_<op>.py` with a `_Verb` row in `_cli/_main.py`; `cmd_<op>` and a `kCliVerbs` row in `src/cpp/cli/main.cpp`; both checked by `tests/python/test_cli_json.py`.
6. Pipeline, where the operation fits its contract: the step and its keys in `tools/pipeline_steps.json`, `python tools/gen_pipeline_table.py`, dispatch in `src/cpp/src/operations/pipeline.cpp` and `_pipeline.py` (`tests/python/test_pipeline_spec.py`).
7. MCP: `tool_<op>` in `mcp/_tools.py`, its `@_forward` wrapper in `mcp/_server.py`, `tests/python/test_mcp.py`, the count in `doc/mcp.md`.
8. Tests: `tests/cpp/test_<op>.cpp`, `tests/cpp/test_c_api.cpp`, a `MIO_ABI_LAYOUT` line in `tests/cpp/test_abi_layout.cpp`, `tests/python/test_<op>.py`, and the binding suites (`tests/fortran`, the Julia `runtests.jl`, R's `testthat`, Octave's `tests/`, `tests/wasm/smoke.mjs`).
9. Measurement: a `row("<op>", …)` in `src/cpp/benchmark/bench_ops.cpp`, the determinism check (`tools/bench_ops.sh … --hash`), and `benchmark/instructions_S.csv` re-recorded with the reason.
10. Docs and release files: `doc/<op>.md`, the sidebar, the operations table of `doc/architecture.md`, `doc/{cli,c_api,fortran,julia,r,octave,wasm,mcp,pipeline}.md`, the ABI row, the single header, a notebook in `example/python/` with graphical output, `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`; narrow or remove the item here.

## The map

## 1. Correctness debts {#correctness-debts}

*Admission: behaviour that loses data, mis-orients cells, does not terminate on valid input or fails silently ([how this file works](#how-this-file-works)). Reopened in November 2026 by the pass that put code anchors on every item, which found the first item below while checking what was then 1.1.2.*

### 1.1 Ansys `.rst` records flagged both bit-sparse and windowed-sparse are refused, not yet decoded

Three files of Ansys' DPF example data hold records with both flags set; their layout is neither form's. Both engines refuse such a record by name (`ReadError` naming the record pointer and the flags; [Ansys `.rst`](./formats/ansys_rst.md#not-read)); decoding it is what remains. **M**

- **State today.** `src/cpp/src/formats/ansys_rst.cpp`: `RstFile::Record` refuses the both-flags case right after the zlib check, and `src/python/meshioplusplus/ansys_rst/_ansys_rst.py` (`_Reader.record`) does the same; `test_both_sparse_flags_refused` and `TEST(AnsysRst, RefusesBothSparseFlags)` pin it, with a fuzz seed in `tests/fuzz/regressions/ansys_rst/both-sparse-flags`. pymapdl-reader misreads these records too.
- **Steps.**
  - **1.1.2 Decode it.** Find the layout: locate the three DPF example files (`ansys-dpf-core`'s `examples` module downloads them; record their names and hashes), dump each both-flag record's header and length beside the dense values DPF returns for the same result set, and test the two candidate layouts (a windowed run inside each set bit, or a bit mask over windows). Needs a DPF Server (2.2.3's installation). When one layout reproduces DPF's values on all three, implement it in both engines .
- **Done when.** A file with such a record reads to DPF's values in both engines; `pytest tests/python/test_ansys_rst.py -k both_sparse` then asserts values rather than the refusal (`MESHIOPLUSPLUS_STRICT_CORE=1` for the core).

---

## 2. Format reach {#format-reach}

*Admission: a format a simulation or physics-ML workflow actually exchanges; a shipped format's missing half is not a roadmap item, because every registered format reads and writes ([decisions](#non-goals-and-decisions-taken)). "Exchanges" means a file that crosses a tool boundary in a real pipeline (mesher → solver, solver → post-processor, solver → training set), not a format that merely exists. Every item names the consumer on the other side of the file.*

Sizes: **S** ≈ days, **M** ≈ two weeks, **L** ≈ a month or more, including tests, docs page and CLI wiring. They assume a new format reuses the shared components rather than growing its own: the card tokenizer (`detail/keyword_card.hpp`), the [node-ordering registry](./node_ordering.md), the Fortran record reader (`detail/fortran_records.hpp`) and the directory sniffing that sequence globs share.

Link legend: unmarked links were opened or returned by a search while this section was written (September 2026); links marked † are quoted from memory and must be checked before relying on them. Vendor documentation portals move often — when a link is dead, search the document title.

---

Every format this section queued is now read: the native FEM interchange, solver input and result-file readers of v16.5.0 to v16.12.0 (Tecplot, Femap, MFEM, Patran, Abaqus `.fil`, Radioss, Z88, libMesh, Marc, Ansys `.rst`, LS-DYNA and OP2), DOLFINx's ADIOS2 `.bp` ([`vtx`](./formats/vtx.md)) and Tecplot SZL through TecIO ([`szplt`](./formats/szplt.md)) of v16.13.0, and the [vendor-runtime routes](./vendor_routes.md) for the files only a vendor's software reads (Abaqus `.odb`, Marc `.t16`, Femap `.modfem`, Ansys results beyond the native reader). Since v16.17.0 the shared components are used wherever a format's layout fits them, the input decks are written too (Radioss's starter deck, checked with the OpenRadioss starter, and Marc's), Femap writes a series as one output set per step, and every format read but not written names its reason on the [conformance page](./conformance.md#formats-missing-a-direction), which a test keeps in step with the registry; the missing half of each has since been written, so every registered format reads and writes. What they still lack is a check against a run of the vendor tool, listed under [Awaiting a licensed run](#awaiting-a-licensed-run): take each as soon as a licence, or a file the tool wrote, is in hand.

### Considered, not queued

- **Graphics, CAD, VFX or lab-specific rather than simulation interchange:** X3D, VRML, Alembic, Silo, FBX, COLLADA, AMF, BYU, Rhino `.3dm`, Parasolid, OpenCASCADE `.brep`. DXF, once listed here, was reopened for sketches and outlines measured from a scan and ships as a drawing format ([DXF](./formats/dxf.md)); 3MF, E57, LAS/LAZ, OpenVDB and OBJ materials, listed here until November 2026, were reopened the same way, because the operations that produce or consume them have shipped since (2.4.5 to 2.4.8).
- **Building and city models, and GIS vector layers:** IFC, CityGML and CityJSON, GeoJSON, Shapefile, GeoPackage. Their consumers want semantics (storeys, attributes, layers) a `Mesh` does not carry, and GDAL and IfcOpenShell serve them; [WKT](./formats/wkt.md) stays the one geometry exchange. Revisit with 9.10.2 if georeferenced meshes become a workflow.
- **Medical image volumes:** NIfTI, NRRD and DICOM are voxel images, not meshes; an array from nibabel or SimpleITK reaches [`isosurface`](./isosurface.md) through `grid` today. The surface formats of the same pipelines are 2.4.4.
- **Closed CFD databases:** STAR-CCM+ `.ccm` (libccmio is not redistributable) and `.sim`; FUN3D and FAST grids beyond what [UGRID](./formats/ugrid.md) already reads.
- **Closed FEM databases with no route worth shipping:** Altair `.h3d` (undisclosed format, SDK not redistributable — Altair's own advice for OpenRadioss is ANIM → VTK, see the [OpenRadioss discussion](https://github.com/orgs/OpenRadioss/discussions/552)); HyperMesh `.hm`; Abaqus `.sim`.
- **No native format to implement:** deal.II (reads and writes UCD, VTK, Gmsh, Abaqus, Exodus — all reachable today); MOOSE (Exodus).
- **Nastran OP4 matrices and punch `.pch`:** matrix and card dumps, not mesh/field exchange; pyNastran serves them.
- **Non-MSC Nastran HDF5 dialects:** incompatible schemas; revisit with a file in hand.
- **Partitioning niche:** gmsh `$PartitionedEntities` (Kratos partitions through MDPA).
- **CalculiX `.dat` print files:** `frd.read_dat` stays a Python helper that returns a list of table dictionaries; it is not a `Mesh`, it has no writer, and `.dat` already belongs to Tecplot and Marc ([CalculiX results](./formats/frd.md)).
- **Operators, not formats** (listed only because they came up in the same review): mesh duals other than the tetrahedral median dual (now [`tet_to_poly`](./tet_poly.md)). ICP registration, the cylindrical and spherical transforms and the shell completions, listed here with them, shipped as [`register_rigid`](./registration.md), [`convert_coordinates`](./coordinates.md) and `meshioplusplus completion`.

Revisit any of them when a consumer asks with a file in hand — a real deck or result file, the tool version that wrote it, and the tool that needs the converted output.

### 2.1 Open verification items before coding

#### 2.1.1 Every link and call marked † or taken from a reference

- **Where they are.** In this file: 2.4.1 (the Fluent CFF readers), 2.4.6 (LASzip, laz-perf, libE57Format), 2.4.7 (NanoVDB), 9.1 (Manifold), 9.7 (METIS, Scotch). Outside it: twelve DOIs of [mesh generation's further reading](./mesh_cad.md#further-reading) (`doc/mesh_cad.md` lines 322–363, legend at line 320) and one accessibility claim in `doc/tui.md` line 174. Not marked but of the same kind: `femap.feFileWriteNeutral(` in `contrib/femap/export_neutral.py` line 32 and the `$!READDATASET` line of `contrib/tecplot/szplt_to_plt.mcr` line 8, both written from the vendor references and never run.
- **Steps.** Open each link and remove its † or replace the link; for a licence claim, quote the licence file's SPDX identifier and the version it was read at. The two vendor calls close with 2.2.4 and 2.2.5.
- **Done when.** `grep -rn "†" doc` returns only the legends. **S**

#### 2.1.2 MFEM NURBS meshes with non-conforming patches

- **State today.** `MFEM NURBS NC-patch mesh` headers are refused: `src/cpp/src/formats/mfem.cpp` lines 2030–2031 (`lex.Fail("'…' meshes are not supported")`, after the accepted `MFEM NURBS mesh v1.0/v1.1` branch at line 2022) and `src/python/meshioplusplus/mfem/_mfem.py` lines 831–832; tested at `tests/python/test_mfem.py` line 532 and `tests/cpp/test_mfem.cpp` line 396; stated in [MFEM](./formats/mfem.md). The NURBS parser is `mf_parse_nurbs`/`MfNurbs` (`mfem.cpp` lines 1057–1403), its twin `mfem/_nurbs.py`.
- **Scope if a user asks.** Read the `patch_topology` section with hanging knots, carry it in `NurbsInfo` (a new optional member: an [ABI](./abi.md) Tier A change), sample each patch as today (the non-conformity is between patches, so the sampled cells get hanging nodes: mark them as [`refine`](./refine.md) does, `refine:hanging`), and refuse `iga_refine` on such a model as it already refuses per-direction refinement of a conforming one.
- **Done when.** MFEM's `data/*-nc*.mesh` NURBS samples read in both engines. No user has asked: do not start without one. **M**

### 2.2 Awaiting a licensed run {#awaiting-a-licensed-run}

Checks that need the vendor tool itself, or a file it wrote that no public source has. Each reader works from the vendor's documentation and every public file found (September 2026); what remains is one probe per line. The procedure is the same for all of them, so it is stated once.

**Recording a run.**

1. Run the probe the item names, on the vendor version it names or a later one.
2. If the run produces a file worth keeping and its licence allows redistribution: put it under `tests/python/meshes/<format>/` with a `README.md` in the form of `tests/python/meshes/szplt/README.md` (a `<!--pytest-codeblocks:skipfile-->` first line, who wrote the file with which tool and version, a table of files, the licence), add the directory's rule to `.gitattributes` (`filter=lfs` for large binaries as for `nastran_op2/*.op2`, or `binary -filter` to keep small ones out of LFS as for `frd` and `szplt`), and add a paragraph to the comment block at the top of `CITATION.cff` (source, copyright, licence, "redistributed unmodified") and a line to `CHANGELOG.md`. A file that cannot be redistributed is described on the page, with its hash, and a reference `.npz` of what the vendor tool reads from it is frozen by a `tools/gen_<format>_reference.py` in the pattern of `tools/gen_vtx_reference.py`.
3. Turn the stand-in test into a real one beside it (the table names both), skipped when the fixture is absent, never deleted: the stand-in still guards the script's calls.
4. Write the vendor version and the date into the page section the table names; where the table says *add*, create a `## Checked against` section as [Abaqus `.odb`](./routes/abaqus_odb.md) has.
5. Fix what the run finds in both engines, with the minimized input as a fuzz regression if it was a crash, then remove the item here and its clause in the [non-goals](#non-goals-and-decisions-taken)' *facts of the format* if the run turned a fact into a feature.

The [vendor-runtime routes](./vendor_routes.md) of v16.13.0 are tested against stand-ins of the vendor APIs only (`tests/python/test_contrib_routes.py`, run by the `optional-readers` job of `ci.yml`). Each needs one recorded run; what to install for it:

- **2.2.1 Abaqus `.odb`.** Abaqus 2020 or later (its `abaqus python`, whose bundled numpy the script needs; 2023 and before run Python 2.7, 2024 and later Python 3). Run `contrib/abaqus_odb/odb_to_vtu.py` on a job with shells, C3D20R and an assembly-level set, and compare its nodal `U` and centroidal `S` against the same job's `.fil` read natively ([route](./routes/abaqus_odb.md)).
- **2.2.2 Marc `.t16`.** Marc/Mentat 2020 or later with PyPost (`py_post`, run with Mentat's Python). Run `contrib/marc_t16/t16_to_vtu.py` on a job that writes both `.t16` and `.t19`, and compare against the `.t19` read natively; confirm that post file position 0 holds the model ([route](./routes/marc_t16.md)).
- **2.2.3 Ansys results via PyDPF.** A DPF Server (Ansys 2021 R1 or later, or the standalone DPF Server 2024 R2 or later) and `pip install ansys-dpf-core pyvista meshioplusplus[h5py]`. Run `contrib/ansys_dpf/rst_to_vtkhdf.py` on a structural `.rst` and compare against the native `.rst` reader; then on a `/FCOMP,RST,1` file, which the native reader refuses ([route](./routes/ansys_dpf.md)).
- **2.2.4 Femap `.modfem`.** Femap 2301 or later on Windows, and `pip install pywin32`. Run `contrib/femap/export_neutral.py`, check `feFileWriteNeutral`'s argument list against that version's API help, and read the neutral file back ([route](./routes/femap_modfem.md)).
- **2.2.5 Tecplot `.szplt`.** Tecplot 360 2023 or later, and `pip install pytecplot`. Save a data set as `.szplt` and as `.plt` from Tecplot itself and read both (the TecIO-backed reader against the native one); run `contrib/tecplot/szplt_to_plt.py` and the macro `contrib/tecplot/szplt_to_plt.mcr`; and point `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_TECIO_LIBRARY` at Tecplot's own shared `libtecio` for the ctypes reader ([route](./routes/tecplot_szplt.md)). Also open in Tecplot 360 a file meshio++ wrote with single-precision, 16-bit and byte variables and one with partitioned zones; and, with a TecIO newer than 2018.3 (which refuses to write polygon and polyhedral zones in an SZL file), write such a zone, read it back, and check the written face map against Tecplot's reading, which would turn the refusal of those zones into a reader and a writer.
- **2.2.19 Catalyst 2 and Ascent.** ParaView 5.11 or later with Catalyst 2, and Ascent 0.9 or later, each with `meshioplusplus` in its Python. Run one of ParaView's Catalyst examples through `contrib/catalyst/blueprint_series.py` (`catalyst_execute` on its `grid` channel) into a `.vtkhdf` series and read it back with `read_sequence`; run an Ascent Python extract of the script, and the gated `conduit.blueprint.mesh.verify` test of `test_conduit.py` with that Conduit ([route](./routes/catalyst.md)).

The readers:

- **2.2.6 Tecplot.** A `.plt` written by `preplot` from the ASCII fixtures (`#!TDV112`), read identically to its `.dat`, and the `.plt` meshio++ now writes opened in Tecplot or checked by `preplot`; binary files of versions 100–105 and 109–111, of which no sample was found (the VisIt suite covers 71, 75, 106–108).
- **2.2.7 Femap.** Element (404) and group (408) records written by Femap 10 and by 12 or later (the public 2401 files hold none); the slot layout of the 13-node pyramid (topology 19); a file meshio++ writes, a multi-set series included, imported into Femap.
- **2.2.8 Patran.** A neutral file exported by ANSA or HyperMesh.
- **2.2.9 Abaqus `.fil`.** A binary run with shells and C3D20R, checked against its `.dat` printout (the truss run TenBarArea is checked); a binary file of a single-precision build (4-byte words), of which no layout description or sample was found, which decides whether the reader and the writer take that width; and a file meshio++ writes, opened in Abaqus.
- **2.2.10 Radioss.** Animation files of the layouts before OpenRadioss (magic numbers `0x5426` to `0x542B`): neither OpenRadioss's tools nor its readers document them. The 16-bit encoding of the nodal normals (the writer leaves them zero: `anim_to_vtk` ignores them and the encoding is undocumented), and a run of OpenRadioss's `anim_to_vtk` on a file meshio++ wrote, time-history lists included.
- **2.2.11 Marc.** A run of Marc itself, including on a deck or a `.t19` post file (a remeshing series included) meshio++ writes (`SIZING`'s fields, one type per `ELEMENTS` line, and the shell and plane-strain default types follow Volume C and Mentat's decks); post files before revision 9 (Marc K7 and earlier, Volume D 2000's PLDUMP layout); Volume A's edge and face numbering, to map edge and face sets (kept as `(cell, number)` field data) onto facets, which also decides whether side regions can be written to decks and post files; the binary `.t16`, read or written natively, which needs Volume D's PLDUMP description of it or a sample file (today it is read only through Marc's PyPost, by the [route](./routes/marc_t16.md)).
- **2.2.12 Ansys `.rst`.** A file written with `/FCOMP,RST,1` or higher (zlib records): `detail/zlib_inflate` is ready, the record framing needs a sample. A harmonic (complex) result file, which no public sample or open reader covers (pymapdl-reader takes cyclic mode pairs only and only DPF reads complex sets), so complex results are neither read nor written; and a file meshio++ writes with element rotations and nodal coordinate systems, read by DPF (pymapdl-reader applies the three nodal rotations in the opposite order, so it agrees only on single-axis angles).
- **2.2.13 LS-DYNA.** A family from LS-DYNA with adaptive remeshing (`NADAPT`), the `d3thdt` time-history database, the cubic and bubble solids (21P, 15T, 20T, 40P, 64), airbag particles and rigid roads (read and written from the manual and lasso-python only: the airbag variable names of a real file, and rigid roads with the full rigid-body velocities, `NDIM = 9`, which the writer leaves out), a real `d3part`, thick shells, and the whole reader against LS-PrePost, with the families and `binout` files (links included) meshio++ writes opened there; whether LS-DYNA writes a ten-node solid's extra nodes once or twice.
- **2.2.14 Nastran OP2.** Random element tables, complex SORT2 nodal tables, CBUSH and CGAP forces, CBUSH real stresses, and NX's solid types 300–303 (read from pyNastran's layouts; no sample). Files meshio++ writes, opened in Femap, Patran or an NX/Simcenter post-processor: the writer produces the MSC dialect only, and the element tables it writes beyond pyNastran's reach (the SORT1 PSD, CRM and ATO random tables, the complex layouts pyNastran does not read) follow the reader's layouts.
- **2.2.15 CalculiX `frd`.** `cgx -b` (CalculiX GraphiX) reading a binary file meshio++ writes; `ccx2paraview` parses the ASCII layout only, so no open tool checks the binary variant beyond meshio++'s own reader and the binary files `ccx` wrote.
- **2.2.16 FEBio `.xplt`.** FEBio Studio or `febio4` opening a file meshio++ wrote: the remeshed series (a mesh section per remeshed step), surfaces with per-surface-node variables, and the compressed states.
- **2.2.17 Nastran HDF5.** MSC's own post-processors (Patran, Simcenter) reading a file meshio++ wrote: its tables follow the column sets of the six MSC Nastran 2020 files, but the `CBUSH`, `CGAP`, `CMASS` and `CONM1` element tables and the `MAT5`, `MAT8`, `MAT9`, `PBARL`, `PBEAML` and `PCOMPG` tables have no sample, so they are not written; a file with them would add them.
- **2.2.18 DOLFINx VTX.** ParaView's `ADIOS2VTXReader` (`tools/gen_vtx_reference.py` drives it) and DOLFINx reading a file meshio++ wrote with several cell types or parts (one block per type and part, with `meshio.shared_points`): both read one cell type per file, so this is meshio++'s own extension of the layout; and a `VTXMeshPolicy.reuse` file (ParaView 6.1 aborts on every step after the first of DOLFINx's own).

Where each run lands (sources are under `src/cpp/src/formats/` with a twin under `src/python/meshioplusplus/<format>/`; tests under `tests/python/` and `tests/cpp/`):

| Item | Script or reader | Version recorded in | Stand-in or existing test |

### 2.3 Depth in formats already read {#format-depth}

Constructs a registered format's reader refuses, skips or flattens. Each was located in the code (November 2026), not in a file: **verify each first** against a file the named tool wrote, and add the fixture with the fix. They are neither a missing direction nor an unwritten feature of something read (the [parity decision](#non-goals-and-decisions-taken) covers both), except where an item says so; they are parts of the format nothing in meshio++ reads yet. Each lands in both engines by [checklist F](#landing-checklists), with its *Reading* and *Writing* sections and its conformance declaration; sources are `src/cpp/src/formats/<format>.cpp` and `src/python/meshioplusplus/<format>/`.

#### 2.3.1 CGNS

A CFD mesh arrives without its boundary conditions, its zone interfaces or its structured zones. Consumers: Fluent, STAR-CCM+, Pointwise, SU2 and every CFD post-processor. **L** in all; the sub-items are independent.

- **State today.** Two core readers: the raw HDF5 path `cgns.cpp` (reads every zone, reads and writes `NGON_n`/`NFACE_n` since v9.21.0) and the cgnslib path `cgns_mll.cpp` (every zone too, in zone-name order, since 2.3.1's first step closed), tried first when `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_WITH_CGNSLIB=ON` (`cgns.cpp` lines 2141–2158), needed only for ADF containers and the CGNS 3.x inline-length NGON layout (refused by name at `cgns.cpp` lines 389–393). The h5py twin is `cgns/_cgns.py` and has no NGON (`PARTIAL_ENGINE_EXEMPT["cgns"]` in `tests/python/conformance_spec.py`). Conformance (`SPEC["cgns"]`): cell data missing, regions `cell` and `point`.
- **Reuse.** `h5::` helpers of `src/cpp/include/meshioplusplus/detail/hdf5_util.hpp` (`open_group`, `group_links`, `read_dataset`, `read_attr_string`); the file-local `cgns_find_child_by_label`, `cgns_read_c1`, `cgns_write_node_attrs`, `cgns_write_c1_node`, `cgns_read_iterative_data` (line 1424) and `cgns_read_regions` (line 885); `detail::vtk_structured_cells` (`src/cpp/src/detail/vtk_xml_pieces.hpp` line 20) for the lattice expansion; `Region(name, RegionKind::Side, dim, tag, entries(n,2))`.
- **Steps.**
  - **2.3.1.2 What the structured read left open.** Write `BC_t`/`Family_t` and the interfaces back (the BCs, interfaces and structured zones are read; only `ZoneSubRegion_t` is written), read `FaceCenter`/`EdgeCenter` solutions (8.4) and the face BCs of a structured zone (lattice facets), offer `weld` over a 1-to-1 interface, and give the flat bindings a `base` argument (`read_cgns_base` is C++ and Python only). Side regions are still not written (the warning at `cgns.cpp` and `_cgns.py`). **M**
- **Tests and fixtures.** `tests/python/test_cgns.py`, `tests/cpp/test_cgns.cpp`, `tests/cpp/test_hdf5_formats.cpp`; fixtures in `tests/python/meshes/cgns/`. The CGNS example set (`cgns.github.io` "Example files", public domain†) has multi-zone structured files with BCs: take two, with the README of step 2 of [recording a run](#awaiting-a-licensed-run).
- **Done when.** `yf17.cgns` and `bump.cgns`-class examples read with their BC regions in both engines, and `meshioplusplus info` lists them. `cgnscheck` (CGNS's own checker†) reports no error on a file meshio++ wrote. The layout written for polyhedra is CGNS 4.x's `ElementStartOffset` form; the 3.x inline-length layout stays refused by name, as today.

#### 2.3.2 Exodus II

Global variables, set variables, polyhedral blocks and assemblies. Consumers: MOOSE (postprocessor values are global variables; its polyhedral output is `NFACED`), Sierra, Cubit, ParaView's IOSS reader. **M**

- **State today.** Global variables, set variables (`exodus:nset:`/`exodus:sset:`), `NSIDED` and `NFACED` blocks are read and written by both engines; `mesh.info` is written back as `info_records` by the Python engine only (the core's mesh has no `info` slot, so the shim bypasses it for a mesh that carries one). Assemblies are not read.
- **Reuse.** The file-local netCDF helpers `read_var`, `var_dims`, `read_names`, `read_att_text`, `read_ints`, `exo_time_values` (`exodus.cpp`), which 2.4.3 moves to a shared header; `ExodusTimeSeriesWriter` (`exodus.hpp`); the [polyhedron kernel](./polyhedra.md) for `NFACED`.
- **Steps.**
  - **2.3.2.4 Assemblies.** Exodus 8 `assembly` groups name a tree of blocks and sets: *verify first* how they are stored in a file (the variables and groups SEACAS writes) and read them into the hierarchy convention 8.5 decides; until then keep the tree in `ExodusInfo` so the writer returns it. **S**
  - **2.3.2.5 QA records on write.** The info records are written back; the `qa_records` (code name, version, date, time) of the producing code are not, because the reader appends them to `mesh.info` and a round trip would grow it. Give them their own side slot first. **S**
- **Tests and fixtures.** `tests/python/test_exodus.py`, `test_exodus_writer_parity.py`, `tests/cpp/test_netcdf_formats.cpp`. Fixtures: generate with `netCDF4` in `tests/python/exodus_fixture.py` for 2.3.2.1 and 2.3.2.3; a MOOSE polyhedral output or a file from SEACAS' test data (BSD†) for 2.3.2.2.
- **Done when.** `ncdump -h` of a file written back lists the same global and set variable names as the source. `exodiff`† (SEACAS) reports no difference between a file and its round trip, with the QA records excluded.

#### 2.3.3 EnSight Gold

Transient geometry, structured parts, several parts on write. Consumers: EnSight and ParaView reading what Fluent, STAR-CCM+, CONVERGE and OpenFOAM's `foamToEnsight` export. **M**

- **State today.** `ensight.cpp` / `ensight/_ensight.py`. Written and read in both engines: several parts (`ensight:part`), transient geometry with `change_coords_only`, and structured `block` parts (expanded; the writer writes them unstructured until 8.6). Still open: the `measured:` line and `per measured node` variables are ignored silently, complex variables are not read, only the first time set is honoured (lines 516–528). (The `TIME` section is the [series writer](./sequences.md)'s.)
- **Steps.**
  - **2.3.3.5 Measured and complex variables.** Measured data is a point cloud with its own files (`measured:` in the case file, a `particle coordinates` geometry): read as a second vertex block when a `measured:` line is present, which needs the point data and cell data of the mesh padded for the extra points. The binary layout of the particle file is not documented anywhere this project has to hand (*verify first* against a file EnSight wrote). Complex variables wait on 8.1's naming. **S**
- **Tests and fixtures.** `tests/python/test_ensight.py`, `tests/cpp/test_ensight.cpp`; fixtures in `tests/python/meshes/ensight/`. Generate transient and `block` cases from ParaView's `EnSightWriter` or VTK's test data (BSD) to check against a real writer's files.
- **Done when.** A two-part transient case from ParaView reads at every step and writes back with both parts (checked here on hand-built cases only).

#### 2.3.5 OpenFOAM

Sets, particle clouds, and compressed writes beyond Python and C++. **S–M**

- **State today.** `openfoam.cpp` (4028 lines) / `openfoam/_openfoam.py` (single-region `polyMesh` only, `PARTIAL_ENGINE_EXEMPT["openfoam"]`). Both readers take `writeCompression on` cases (`points.gz`, …) and `write_openfoam` writes them through `OpenFoamWriteOptions::mCompress` and Python's `compression="gzip"` ([compressed cases](./formats/openfoam.md#compressed-cases)); `polyMesh/sets` is read and written (`set:<name>` regions); nothing names `lagrangian`. Tests build cases inline (`tests/python/test_openfoam.py`, `tests/cpp/test_openfoam.cpp`); there is no fixture directory.
- **Steps.**
  - **2.3.5.2 Clouds, the rest.** Cartesian `coordinates` clouds are read (`cloud=`); barycentric `positions` need the mesh's tetrahedra to place a particle (9.3), binary fields are skipped, and the flat bindings and CLIs have no cloud read. **S**
  - **2.3.5.3 Compressed writes on the flat surfaces: done through the existing codec selector** (`WriteOptions::mCodec` = `zlib` gzips the case; no ABI change). A `--compression gzip` spelling in the CLIs is the remaining nicety. **XS**
  - **2.3.5.4 The collated `processors<N>/` layout.** *Verify first* (nothing in `openfoam.cpp` or the page names `decomposedBlockData`): newer OpenFOAM writes one `processors<N>/` directory holding a single collated file per field instead of `processorN/` directories; parse its block framing and feed the blocks to `reconstruct_decomposed` (`openfoam.cpp`), which already reads the `*ProcAddressing` lists. The reader already honours the `arch` header's label and scalar widths (`FoamFile` parsing near `openfoam.cpp` line 165). **M**
- **Done when.** A tutorial case's `sets` and a `lagrangian` cloud read in the core, and a `blockMesh` + `simpleFoam` case written by OpenFOAM with `writeCompression on` reads equal to its uncompressed twin (the synthetic gzipped cases of `OpenFoamCompressed` in `tests/cpp/test_openfoam.cpp` stand in until one is committed). An optional `checkMesh` job (ESI and Foundation containers) is clean on a case meshio++ wrote.

#### 2.3.6 Gmsh

- **State today.** `$ElementNodeData`, `$InterpolationScheme` and `$GhostElements` fall into the generic unknown-section skip with no warning: `gmsh.cpp` lines 1431–1432 (4.x body) and 1912–1913 (2.2), `gmsh/_gmsh22.py` lines 74–75, `_gmsh41.py` lines 91–100, `_gmsh40.py` line 83.
- **Steps.**
  - **2.3.6.1 Interpret what is kept.** `$ElementNodeData` is read and written (`gmsh:element_node:<name>`, in the file's node order); decide with 8.3 whether it moves to that convention. **S–M, after 8.3's design pass**
  - **2.3.6.2 `$GhostElements`: verify.** Read as `cell_data["partition:ghost"]` in 4.x ASCII files (both engines). Still open: confirm the line layout against a Gmsh-written partitioned file, and MSH 2.2's negative partition ids. **S**
  - **2.3.6.3 The rest of MSH 4.1.** `$PartitionedEntities`, `$Parametrizations` and `$InterpolationScheme` are kept verbatim in ASCII files only; keep them in binary files, and read the parametric node coordinates (`u, v, w` after the point in a 4.1 node record), which are still discarded. The Gmsh 5.0 development manual's `$Polytopes`, `$Edges` and `$Faces` are a watch item until a release defines them†. **S–M**
- **Done when.** Gmsh's `tutorials/view*.msh`-class file with element-node data round-trips in both engines.

#### 2.3.7 The structured VTK family

- **State today.** `read_vtkhdf` refuses every `Type` but `UnstructuredGrid`, `PolyData` and the two composites (`vtkhdf.cpp` lines 1636–1638; `vtkhdf_is_leaf` lines 163–165; `vtkhdf/_vtkhdf.py` line 830). `.pvts`, `.pvtr` and `.pvti` have a Python-only reader. `vti`, `vtr` and `vts` fail conformance with `"no cell type round-trips"` (`conformance_spec.py`, `SPEC["vti"]`, `["vtr"]`, `["vts"]`), because the canonical mesh is not a lattice; `vtr` needs a uniform one (`vtr.cpp` lines 50–52).
- **Reuse.** `detail::vtk_xml_read_pieces` and `vtk_structured_cells` (`src/cpp/src/detail/vtk_xml_pieces.hpp`); `detail::lattice_from_mesh` and `LatticeSpec` (`detail/grid_lattice.hpp` lines 101–205); the parallel-index pattern of `pvtu.cpp` (`pvtu_info` line 70, `pvtu_parse_index` line 484) with `pidx::merge_pieces` (`src/cpp/src/formats/pindex_common.hpp` line 272), and in Python `_parallel_xml.make_parallel_format` and `_pvtk_index.py`.
- **Steps.**
  - **2.3.7.1 VTKHDF structured grids.** `ImageData` is read (static, both engines); still open: its transient form, the writer, and `RectilinearGrid` and `StructuredGrid` where the VTKHDF specification defines them at the pinned VTK version (*verify first*: they were proposals in 2025). The array layout of `PointData` was assumed flat or `(nz, ny, nx)` and should be checked against a file VTK wrote. **S–M**
  - **2.3.7.2 `OverlappingAMR`.** Levels of boxes with a refinement ratio: read as one hexahedral block per level with `vtkhdf:amr:level` cell data and the coarse cells under finer boxes blanked into a `uint8` mask; no hanging-node closure. `HyperTreeGrid` stays refused: it is a tree encoding with no cell list. **M**
  - **2.3.7.3 `.pvts`, `.pvtr`, `.pvti`: remaining work.** The Python readers place pieces by `Extent` (`pvti`/`pvtr`/`pvts`, read-only; [pvts](formats/pvts.md)). Still open: the C++ reader (a `pvtu_info`-style row set), and splitting writers by `partition:part` when the parts are boxes. **S**
  - **2.3.7.4 The round trip** is 8.6's: with its annotation, a mesh read from `vts` writes back as `vts`, and the three conformance declarations change from `error` to a note.
  - **2.3.7.5 Composites with time, and a gate.** [VTKHDF](./formats/vtkhdf.md) flattens `MultiBlockDataSet` and has no time on composites, and refuses `Table` and `HyperTreeGrid`: write a temporal composite as the format's `Steps` group per block, check the `Version` attribute against what the content needs (it already writes the oldest that covers it), and add a `pvpython` CI job† that opens every written variant in a VTK of the pinned version. **M**
- **Done when.** VTK's `Testing/Data` structured `.vtkhdf` and `.pvti` samples read to the same point and cell counts as `vtkXMLReader` reports.

#### 2.3.8 Decomposed files, read whole without MPI

Reopened from the MPI [non-goal](#non-goals-and-decisions-taken), which stays as it is for the library: gathering the pieces of a finished run is file reading. **M**

- **State today.** Four formats reassemble, each with its own code: OpenFOAM (`reconstruct_decomposed`, `openfoam.cpp` line 863, by the `*ProcAddressing` lists), `pvtu` (`pidx::merge_pieces`: no global ids, ghosts dropped, pieces labelled as `piece_i` regions rather than `partition:part`), Elmer (`elm_read_parts`, `elmer.cpp` line 541: nodes deduplicated by global id through a hash map, `partition:part` set) and MFEM (`mf_read_parallel`, `mfem.cpp` line 3372). Nothing names Nemesis, `node_num_map` or `elem_num_map`; the Exodus readers ignore the number maps. `MergeOptions` (`operations/merge.hpp` lines 66–70) welds by tolerance only.
- **Steps.**
  - **2.3.8.1 One helper.** `detail/merge_by_global_id.{hpp,cpp}`: pieces with a global node id per local node and optionally a global cell id, to one mesh with shared nodes merged, cells in global order when ids are given and `cell_data["partition:part"]` (`kPvtuPartKey`, `formats/pvtu.hpp` line 80). Move Elmer's and MFEM's loops onto it with digests unchanged (`tools/bench_ops.sh --hash`), record it in [common utilities](./common_utilities.md), and give `pvtu` the `partition:part` array beside its regions. **S–M**
  - **2.3.8.2 Nemesis.** A base name with `.e.<N>.<p>` siblings (zero-padded `p`): sniff the family as the directory sniffing of sequence globs does, read each with `read_exodus`, take `node_num_map` and `elem_num_map` as the global ids, and merge node sets, side sets and variables. A `read` of any one member reads the family unless `piece=` selects one. Writing a family from `partition:part` follows, as OpenFOAM's `decomposed=True`. **M**
  - **2.3.8.3 CGNS and MED one file per rank.** Only with a file in hand: neither has one convention for it.
- **Done when.** A four-rank set from SEACAS `decomp` reads equal, after sorting by global id, to the serial file it was cut from.

#### 2.3.9 Smaller losses, one fix each

Each is the parity decision's "unwritten feature" if the format can hold it; where it cannot, the page says so and the item closes. **S each**

- **2.3.9.1 glTF morph targets.** `write_gltf` writes one static mesh, and the reader skips morph targets (`gltf_read.cpp` lines 799–800) and animations (lines 832–836). Write a series of point displacements as `targets` (`POSITION` deltas) with an `animation` sampler on the `weights`, so a deformation series is one `.glb` a browser plays; read them back as steps. A series sink as in 2.3.4. **M**
- **2.3.9.5 Medit normals in `.meshb`.** The ASCII `Normals` and `NormalAtVertices` are read and written (`medit:normal`); the binary `GmfNormals` and `GmfNormalAtVertices` sections are still skipped (the engines also disagree on the skip itself, which is 3.4.12). **S**
- **2.3.9.7 LS-DYNA `*ELEMENT_SOLID_H20`.** Skipped as unsupported (`lsdyna.cpp` lines 419–422, `lsdyna/_lsdyna.py` lines 266–267); the two-line layout reads at most ten nodes. Read 20 nodes to `hexahedron20` through the [node-ordering registry](./node_ordering.md).
- **2.3.9.8 glTF extensions and compression.** Neither `EXT_mesh_features`, Draco (`KHR_draco_mesh_compression`) nor meshopt (`EXT_meshopt_compression`) is named anywhere: read both compressions (a decoder dependency, optional and feature-gated, as zlib is) and write neither until a consumer asks; map `EXT_mesh_features` feature IDs to `point_data`/`cell_data` and the property table to `field_data`. Specifications†: the Khronos glTF registry. **M**
- **2.3.9.9 OpenUSD completeness: remaining.** `primvars` by interpolation and `timeSamples` as steps were already read and written; face `GeomSubset`s are now regions (surface meshes, both directions). Still open: `faceVarying` primvars (no meshio++ counterpart) and a volume mesh's regions on its skin. **S**

#### 2.3.10 MOAB H5M sets, element tags and DAGMC conventions

Neutronics, fusion and multiphysics workflows (OpenMC / DAGMC, MCNP, Shift, MOAB, NekRS-MOAB) exchange `.h5m` files whose material volumes, boundary surfaces and per-cell tallies live in MOAB entity sets and element tags; meshio++ drops all of them on read and skips them on write. **M**

- **State today.** `src/cpp/src/formats/h5m.cpp` (295 lines) and `src/python/meshioplusplus/h5m/_h5m.py`: `read_h5m` reads only seven linear types (`Edge2`, `Tri3`, `Quad4`, `Tet4`, `Pyramid5`, `Prism6`, `Hex8`, lines 40–45) and `tstt/nodes/tags`, ignoring `tstt/elements/*/tags` (cell data) and `tstt/sets` (line 165). `write_h5m` writes only `line`, `triangle` and `tetra` (lines 231–233), writes no cell data (line 282) and emits an empty `tstt/sets` group (lines 286–287).
- **Reuse.** `h5::` helpers (`detail/hdf5_util.hpp`), `write_tag_dataset` (`h5m.cpp` line 97), and the [node-ordering registry](./node_ordering.md) for quadratic MOAB types (`Tri6`, `Quad8`/`9`, `Tet10`, `Hex20`/`27`, `Prism15`, `Pyramid13`).
- **Steps.**
  - **2.3.10.1 Element types and cell tags.** Read and write `tstt/elements/<type>/tags` as `cell_data` (fixing the Python writer's broken cell-data path noted at `h5m.cpp` line 282), add `quad`, `hexahedron`, `wedge`, `pyramid` and the quadratic MOAB types to `write_h5m` and `h5m_to_meshio`. **S**
  - **2.3.10.2 Entity sets and DAGMC metadata.** Read and write `tstt/sets/{list,contents,parents,children}` and set tags (`MATERIAL_SET`, `NEUMANN_SET`, `DIRICHLET_SET`, `GEOM_DIMENSION`, `CATEGORY`, `NAME`, `GLOBAL_ID`) as named `Region`s (using `start_id` ranges to map MOAB entity handles back to 0-based global cell/point indices), preserving DAGMC volume/surface hierarchy through 8.5 when available. *Verify first* against a real MOAB/DAGMC `.h5m` from OpenMC's public test suite†. **M**
- **Done when.** A DAGMC `.h5m` volume/surface model round-trips its material/boundary sets and element tags in both engines, and MOAB's `mbsize`† reports the same entity, tag and set counts on the written file.

### 2.4 New formats {#new-formats}

Each names its consumer, as the admission asks. None was estimated from a file: **verify first** with one in hand, and with the licence of whatever reader the layout is taken from. Each lands by [checklist F](#landing-checklists).

*November 2026 pass.* Landed in both engines: `detect` (formerly 2.4.9, every surface), PLOT3D, the shared netCDF helper, UGRID NetCDF, ADCIRC, Selafin, GIFTI, FreeSurfer, the ZIP container and 3MF, native LAS, glTF and OBJ textures with `bake_texture`, and legacy Fluent `.cas`/`.dat`, each checked against an independent library where one could be installed (xugrid, nibabel, lib3mf, laspy). What remains below needs something this pass did not have: a decoder library (LAZ: LASzip or laz-perf; E57: libE57Format; NanoVDB: its header and a licence check), a vendor-written file (Fluent CFF, ADCIRC, TELEMAC, Delft3D-FM, NASA TMR), or a viewer to open the result (PrusaSlicer, Blender).

#### 2.4.1 Fluent case and data

- **Why.** The `.msh` mesh is read ([`ansys`](./formats/ansys.md)), but not a `.cas`/`.dat` pair nor the HDF5 `.cas.h5`/`.dat.h5` (CFF) Fluent 2020 and later write by default: a Fluent user cannot bring a solution to meshio++ without exporting it first. Consumer: Fluent → ParaView, OpenFOAM or a training set.
- **Reuse.** `ansys.cpp`: `struct FluentReader` (line 133: bracket depth, hex header groups, binary bodies), `read_ansys` (line 347: section index split into the binary-width prefix `20`/`30` and the core id; cores 10, 12 and 13 are nodes, cells and faces; 39 and 45 zone names), `fluent_hex` (284), `fluent_face_rows` (324); the twin's `_Reader` (`ansys/_ansys.py` line 53). HDF5 through `detail/hdf5_util.hpp`.
- **Steps.**
  - **2.4.1.1 Legacy `.cas`: done.** Read as a `.msh` in both engines, zone types as `fluent:zone_type:<name>` ([ansys](./formats/ansys.md#cases-and-data-files)).
  - **2.4.1.2 Legacy `.dat`: verify.** Sections 300, 2300 and 3300 become cell data by cell and boundary-face id (`data=`, or the `.dat` beside a `.cas`) in both engines, from hand-built files. Still open: read VTK's `room.cas`/`room.dat` to the ranges `vtkFLUENTReader` reports, and the full sub-section name table. **S**
  - **2.4.1.3 CFF `.cas.h5`/`.dat.h5`.** Undocumented; the open descriptions are VTK's `vtkFLUENTCFFReader` (BSD-3†) and PyFluent's file readers (MIT†): read the layout from them, not their code paths verbatim, and credit both in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` only if code is taken. `/meshes/1/{nodes,faces,cells}` and `/results/1/phase-1/{cells,faces}/<field>/1`. A new format name (`fluent_h5`), gated on HDF5 as `cgns` is. **M**
  - **2.4.1.4 Writing.** The parity decision asks for it: a case the reader returns is written back as legacy `.cas`/`.dat` (the `.msh` writer plus the data sections); CFF write only if Fluent is at hand to open it, otherwise a `READ_ONLY` conformance entry with that reason and an item in 2.2.
- **Fixtures.** Fluent tutorial cases are not redistributable; VTK's `Testing/Data` has small `.cas`/`.dat` and `.cas.h5` files (BSD): take those with their README.
- **Done when.** VTK's `room.cas`/`room.dat` and `room.cas.h5`/`room.dat.h5` read to the cell counts and field ranges `vtkFLUENTReader` and `vtkFLUENTCFFReader` report. **M** (legacy), **M** (CFF)

#### 2.4.2 PLOT3D: verify

Read and written in both engines ([PLOT3D](./formats/plot3d.md)): the variant inferred from the size, `.q` and `.f` files, unformatted double-precision writing. Still open: read a NASA TMR multi-block `.p3dfmt` grid to its documented block dimensions and write it back byte-equal. **XS**

#### 2.4.3 Coastal and ocean meshes

- **Why.** Triangles and mixed polygons with nodal fields and a timeline, all of which the model holds. Consumer: a hydrodynamic run → ParaView, QGIS's mesh layer or a surrogate's training set.
- **Steps.**
  - **2.4.3.1 A shared netCDF helper: done.** `detail/netcdf_util.{hpp,cpp}` (`detail::nc`) holds the helpers Exodus had file-local, Exodus reads through them with its messages unchanged, and the next netCDF format reuses them ([common utilities](./common_utilities.md)).
  - **2.4.3.2 UGRID-conventions NetCDF: remaining.** `ugrid_nc` reads and writes 1-D and 2-D topologies in both engines ([UGRID NetCDF](./formats/ugrid_nc.md), checked against xugrid). Still open: a series writer (one file, a time dimension), volume topologies, the CRS name in `field_data` (9.10.2), and a Delft3D-FM `_map.nc` read. **S**
  - **2.4.3.3 ADCIRC: verify.** `adcirc` reads `fort.14` (boundaries as tagged point regions with their order kept) and `fort.63`/`fort.64` results as steps, and writes the grid, in both engines ([ADCIRC](./formats/adcirc.md)). Still open: read an ADCIRC example case, the NetCDF output forms (through 2.4.3.2 where they follow CF), and the barrier columns. **XS**
  - **2.4.3.4 TELEMAC Selafin: verify.** `selafin` reads single and double precision, 2-D and 3-D, with steps, and writes 2-D one-step files, in both engines ([Selafin](./formats/selafin.md)). Still open: a TELEMAC-written `r2d_*.slf` read, a series writer, and units into 9.10.1. **XS**
- **Fixtures.** The UGRID conventions' own examples; ADCIRC's public example problems; TELEMAC's examples (GPL data: do not copy, generate small files with the writer once it exists and check against a TELEMAC-written one locally).
- **Done when.** A Delft3D-FM `_map.nc`, an ADCIRC quarter-annular case and a TELEMAC `r2d_*.slf` each read with their times in both engines. **M** for the three

#### 2.4.4 Neuroimaging surfaces: done

[GIFTI](./formats/gifti.md) and [FreeSurfer](./formats/freesurfer.md) surfaces are read and written in both engines, with curv and annot overlays read; nibabel reads what meshio++ writes and the reverse. Writing curv and annot files is the one piece left, for when a user asks.

#### 2.4.5 3MF: verify

The ZIP container (`detail/zip_archive`, 2.4.5.1) and the core specification (2.4.5.2) are in both engines ([3MF](./formats/3mf.md)), checked against lib3mf both ways. Still open: open a written file in PrusaSlicer, and read the consortium's core sample files. **XS**

#### 2.4.6 E57 and LAS/LAZ

Reopened: scan registration, [surface reconstruction](./reconstruct_surface.md), deviation and the fitting operations shipped, and their input today is `pcd`, `ply` or `xyz`, while a terrestrial scanner writes E57 and an airborne one LAS. **M**

- **Reuse.** `pcd.cpp`'s shape for a point cloud: `pcd_build_mesh` (line 605) builds one `vertex` block (line 686), unpacks colour and keeps sensor metadata in `field_data`; conformance note "every node is kept as a vertex" (`SPEC["pcd"]`).
- **Steps.**
  - **2.4.6.1 LAS, native: done.** `las` reads 1.2 to 1.4 and point formats 0 to 10 and writes 1.4 formats 6 to 8 in both engines ([LAS](./formats/las.md)), checked against laspy. Extended VLRs are not kept yet.
  - **2.4.6.2 LAZ.** Compressed LAS through LASzip or laz-perf (Apache-2.0†), an optional dependency in the TecIO pattern: a CMake option `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_WITH_LAZ`, the feature macro, the registry row inside the `#ifdef`, `registry_compiled_out` returning the library's name so the refusal names it (`registry.cpp` lines 1236–1259), `__has_laz__` in `_core.cpp`, the twin through `laspy[lazrs]`, and a leg in the `optional-readers` job. **S–M**
  - **2.4.6.3 E57.** XML header plus compressed binary sections; through libE57Format (BSL-1.0†) with the same gating (`MESHIOPLUSPLUS_WITH_E57`), the twin through `pye57`. One vertex block per `data3D` scan with its pose applied, or kept apart with `scan=`; intensity and colour to point data; images are not read. **M**
- **Fixtures.** ASPRS sample LAS files and libE57Format's test data, each with its licence checked.
- **Done when.** `meshioplusplus info scan.las` and `scan.e57` list the points and fields, and `register_rigid` runs on two E57 scans.

#### 2.4.7 OpenVDB, through NanoVDB

Reopened: [`compute_sdf`](./sdf.md), [`voxelize`](./voxelize.md) and [`octree`](./octree.md) produce the sparse level sets and fog volumes it stores, and physics-ML pipelines and Blender consume them. **M, design pass first**

- **State today.** `compute_sdf` returns a hexahedral lattice mesh with `sdf:distance` point data and the lattice in `field_data` (`sdf:origin`, `sdf:spacing`, `sdf:dims`; `operations/sdf.hpp` lines 252–256), dense or as a 1-irregular octree (`SdfStructure`); `LatticeSpec` and `lattice_from_mesh` are in `detail/grid_lattice.hpp`.
- **Decisions to take first.** (1) Whether NanoVDB's header (verify its licence, Apache-2.0 or MPL-2.0†, and that it is self-contained) reads and writes `.nvdb` only, which is what its viewers and Blender's importer need to be checked for, or whether `.vdb` proper is required, which means the OpenVDB library and its dependencies (then a provider add-on, not the core). (2) The shape on the meshio++ side: recommended, an export of a lattice array (a narrow band of `sdf:distance`, or `voxelize`'s occupancy) and an import to a lattice mesh, registered as a format `nvdb` whose writer refuses a mesh that is not a lattice by name, as `vti` does.
- **Done when.** Blender or `nanovdb_viewer` opens a narrow-band level set written from `compute_sdf`'s output.

#### 2.4.8 Materials and textures: remaining

A texture travels as `field_data["texture:png"]` through glTF (embedded as `baseColorTexture`, read back from the first image) and OBJ (`usemtl` groups as regions, `.mtl` `Kd` and `map_Kd`), and [`bake_texture`](./bake_texture.md) makes one from a field (core, Python, both CLIs, MCP), all in both engines. Still open: `bake_texture` in the C API, the flat bindings and the pipeline (checklist O steps 3, 4 and 6), and a `gltf-validator` and Blender check of a baked `.glb`. **S**

#### 2.4.9 High-order spectral-element and flux-reconstruction formats (PyFR and Nek5000/NekRS)

High-order CFD communities run PyFR and Nek5000/NekRS and convert meshes and solutions to and from Gmsh, CGNS, Exodus, ParaView and physics-ML training sets; meshio++ already has arbitrary-order Lagrange cells, `detail/hdf5_util.hpp`, `detail/byteswap.hpp` and multi-step sequence readers, so both fit naturally. *Verify first* against current release files from both solvers' public examples (BSD-3†). **M each**

- **Reuse.** `h5::` helpers (`detail/hdf5_util.hpp`) for PyFR; `detail/byteswap.hpp`, `detail/parse_guard.hpp` and `formats/lagrange_common.hpp` (3.4.3 moves it to `detail/reference_element.hpp`) for high-order node ordering and Gauss–Lobatto–Legendre (GLL) to Lagrange mapping; `RegionKind::Side` for boundary patches.
- **Steps.**
  - **2.4.9.1 PyFR `.pyfrm` and `.pyfrs`.** Pure HDF5 layout†: mesh datasets `spt_<type>_p<n>` (shape points per element type and partition), `con_p<n>` connectivity, `bcon_<name>_p<n>` boundary face pairs as `RegionKind::Side`, and `.pyfrs` solution datasets (`soln_<type>_p<n>`) attached as nodal/element fields or read as a sequence. Gated on `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_WITH_HDF5` as `cgns` and `h5m` are; Python twin through `h5py`. **S–M**
  - **2.4.9.2 Nek5000 / NekRS `.re2` and `.fld`.** Binary `.re2` mesh (`#v002`/`#v004` header, endianness tag `6.54321`, xyz element corners, curved-side records, boundary condition tags as side regions) and `.fld`/`.f00001` spectral-element field files (`#std` header, per-element GLL point coordinates and velocity/pressure/temperature fields, readable as a transient sequence; curved spectral elements mapped onto VTK Lagrange hexahedra/quads or evaluated through 15.3). No external library needed. **M**
- **Fixtures.** Small generated examples from PyFR's `examples/` (BSD-3†) and Nek5000/NekRS's `examples/` (BSD-3†), checked against `pyfr export`† and VisIt/ParaView's `vtkNek5000Reader`†.
- **Done when.** A curved PyFR `.pyfrm`/`.pyfrs` pair and a Nek5000 `.re2`/`.f00001` pair convert to `.vtu` and back in both engines (`MESHIOPLUSPLUS_STRICT_CORE=1`) with matching cell counts, boundary regions and field ranges.


---

## 3. Quality of implementation {#quality-of-implementation}

*Admission: work that makes every other item safer to land. None of it is a feature, so none of it competes for the same attention — it can run in parallel with everything.*

The sanitizer leg, the reader fuzzing, the format conformance matrix, the property tests, the benchmark harness, the ParaView plugin test and the fallback narrowing all shipped in v16.14.0 ([fuzzing and sanitizers](./fuzzing.md), [format conformance](./conformance.md), [benchmarks](./benchmarks.md)). What remains, each item with the files it touches; the build is one `CMakeLists.txt` (2,289 lines) and CI one `ci.yml`, so most items name lines in those two.

### 3.1 OSS-Fuzz submission

Calendar-bound, like the registries in [§4](#ecosystem-reach). **S**

- **State today.** The maintainer's Google-account contact is confirmed, the submission files pass OSS-Fuzz's presubmit checks, and the helper-container image and external-engine ASan build pass. The files are `tools/fuzz/oss-fuzz/{Dockerfile,build.sh,project.yaml,package_seeds.py}`: the image clones `master` with the Eigen and JSON submodules; `build.sh` configures with `-DMESHIOPLUSPLUS_FUZZING_ENGINE="$LIB_FUZZING_ENGINE"`, HDF5, netCDF, gidpost and bzip2 off and the SEQ backend, copies `meshioplusplus_fuzz_read` once per format (from `meshioplusplus_fuzz_replay -list-formats` minus `tools/fuzz/not_fuzzed.txt`), builds the operation fuzz targets (`meshioplusplus_fuzz_mesh_cad_surface`, `meshioplusplus_fuzz_triangulate_polygon_2d`, `meshioplusplus_fuzz_adapt_volume`), and packs both reader and `op_*` seeds; `project.yaml` asks for address and undefined with libFuzzer. [Fuzzing](./fuzzing.md#oss-fuzz) holds the helper commands, not a checklist; `tests/python/test_fuzz_tools.py` tests the tools; no workflow touches the directory.
- **Steps.**
  - **3.1.1** In a checkout of `google/oss-fuzz` with the four files under `projects/meshioplusplus/`: `python3 infra/helper.py build_image meshioplusplus`, then for `address` and `undefined`: `build_fuzzers --clean --sanitizer <s>`, `check_build --sanitizer <s>`, `run_fuzzer --sanitizer <s> meshioplusplus meshioplusplus_fuzz_read_vtu -- -max_total_time=30`.
  - **3.1.2** `build_fuzzers --clean --sanitizer coverage`, unpack one seed corpus, `coverage --no-corpus-download --no-serve --fuzz-target=meshioplusplus_fuzz_read_vtu`, and read the report for readers with near-zero coverage (a missing seed or dictionary: add it to `tools/fuzz/seed_corpus.py` or `tools/fuzz/dicts/`).
  - **3.1.4** `SECURITY.md` exists (3.14): OSS-Fuzz files reports against it. Then open the pull request in `google/oss-fuzz`, answer review, and turn the command block of the page into the record of what ran and when.
- **Done when.** The project builds on oss-fuzz.com and its first reports arrive.

### 3.3 The meshing corpora: sources and first numbers

The CAD corpus ([benchmarks](./benchmarks.md#cad-corpus), `tools/cad_corpus.py`) and the Thingi10K sample `tetrahedralize` is measured on (`tools/tet_corpus.py`) are weekly jobs; three pieces need a run that can reach the sources, which this repository's development sandboxes could not. **S**

- **State today.** `cad_corpus.py` has `fetch` (`--manifest --dest --record`), `run`, `compare`, `baseline`, `table --check` and `summary`; its manifest `tools/cad_corpus/manifest.json` takes `git` and `https` sources; `fetch` writes the hash of a model that has none to `--record` and refuses a mismatch; `fetch_https` (lines 176–189) extracts `zip` only. `cad_corpus.yml` runs Mondays and uploads `corpus-hashes.json` in the `cad-corpus` artifact. `tet_corpus.py` has `fetch`, `run`, `summary`; `tools/tet_corpus/manifest.json` has `"models": {}`, a seeded sample of 300 and the Hugging Face Thingi10K v1.5.0 tarball; `tet_corpus.yml` runs Tuesdays and uploads `tet-corpus-hashes.json`. Tests: `tests/python/test_cad_corpus.py`, `test_tet_corpus.py`.
- **Steps.**
  - **3.3.1 NIST's MBE PMI test models** (a US-government work): add an `https` source for the CTC and FTC STEP archives with no hashes, let the scheduled run record them, and commit the recorded `sha256` values from the artifact.
  - **3.3.2 ABC** (Koch et al. 2019; MIT dataset, models under Onshape's public-document terms, which permit fetch-at-run-time): its chunks are 7-Zip archives. Add `archive: "7z"` to `fetch_https` through `py7zr` (LGPL, a CI-only tool dependency, imported inside the branch with a named error) or the runner's `7z` executable; a seeded sample of one chunk; a unit test with a tiny `.7z` built in the test.
  - **3.3.3 Thingi10K hashes and first numbers:** take `tet-corpus-hashes.json` from the scheduled artifact into the manifest's `models`, and add a `## Measured on Thingi10K` section to [tetrahedralize](./tetrahedralize.md), which has no place for them (its *Measured* section is at line 45), filled from `tet_corpus.py summary`, with a freshness test like `test_the_published_table_is_fresh`.
  - **3.3.4 What the CAD corpus still fails on** is its measure for the mesher, not a task of this item: 399 of 16,503 faces at a size of the diagonal over 40 (degenerate patches 270, trims leaving their patch 69, other 60), all on KR600, the RC buggy and the Ventilator.
- **Done when.** Both manifests carry hashes for every model and the two pages their tables.

The `master` scan ([static analysis](./static_analysis.md)) ranks files by duplicated-lines density; an item closes when its copies call the shared kernels, whose history is in [shared geometry and FEM kernels](./geometry_kernels.md). The copies that need no kernel are [§6](#code-duplication). **M–L**

- **The shared targets** (all core-private under `src/cpp/src/detail/` unless noted): `math_utils.hpp` (`MathUtils::Sub`, `Add`, `Scale`, `Dot`, `Cross`, `Norm`, `Distance`, `UnitByDivision` with a zero guard, `AngleDeg`, `SolveSymmetric3`); `geometry_utils.hpp` (`TriangleNormal`, `TriangleTwiceArea`, `TriangleArea`, `TriangleAnglesDeg`, `SignedTwiceArea2D`); `bounding_box_utils.hpp` (`BoundingBox3`, `Expand`, `FromPoints` seeded with the first point, `FromCoords`, `Extent`, `Diagonal`); the installed `include/meshioplusplus/detail/geometry.hpp` (`Vec3`, `vec3_*`, `triple_product`, `det3`); `fem_kernels.hpp` (`tet_volume6`, `tet_volume`, `det_unit3`, `corner_jacobian_ratio`, `kHexCornerEdges`, `kWedgeCornerEdges`); the installed `detail/cell_faces.hpp`, `cell_edges.hpp` (surface types only) and `cell_subdivision.hpp` (`cell_refine_edges`, which holds the volume edges); `bspline_ndu.hpp`; `always_inline.hpp` (`MIO_ALWAYS_INLINE`).

A copy whose arithmetic differs from the kernel changes the last bits of its results, so it moves only when that change is shown not to happen or is intended and recorded. Each new or extended utility gets its `tests/cpp/test_*_utils.cpp` case and its row in [common utilities](./common_utilities.md).

  - Hand-written cross products: the sites that build a `Vec3`-typed value (`curved_quad.cpp`, `curved_triangle.cpp`, `feature_fit.cpp`, `param_disc.cpp`, `mesh_cad_reparam.cpp`, six in `mesh_cad.cpp`, `step_wireframe.cpp`, `dxf.cpp`) call `detail::vec3_cross`, and one in `step_brep.cpp` calls `MathUtils::Cross`.
