### 3.5 Clang-Tidy beyond includes

Grow the checked set in stages, each first an advisory report and then a gate on new findings. **M–L**

- **State today.** `.clang-tidy` is `Checks: '-*,misc-include-cleaner'` with a `HeaderFilterRegex` over `src/cpp/(include|src)|bindings/c` and no naming options. `tools/tidy_report.py` (tested by `tests/python/test_tidy_report.py`) runs any `--checks` expression over a Clang compilation database, emits deduplicated JSON (`schema_version` 1) and compares against a `--baseline FILE` by `(file, check, message)` (line-number-independent); `tools/performance_tidy.py` and `tools/performance-tidy.sh` are thin wrappers defaulting to `-*,performance-*`. No baseline file is committed yet: the counts live in [benchmarks](./benchmarks.md#clang-tidy-performance-audit) (520, 283 and 284 on MESHIO, NATIVE and KRATOS after the first batch). `tools/include-cleanup.sh` and the `include-cleanup` job (`ci.yml` lines 1360–1383) are the gate pattern. `clang-tidy-diff` ships with the runner's clang-tidy. [3.23](#clice) weighs clice's `lint` as a faster way to run the same checks.
- **Steps.**
  - **3.5.1 Per-family baselines.** `tools/tidy_report.py` (`--checks`, `--baseline FILE`), the thin `performance_tidy.py` wrapper and `tests/python/test_tidy_report.py` are landed; remaining: commit a baseline JSON per family under `tools/tidy/`.
  - **3.5.2 `bugprone-*`** first (the family most likely to find defects), then `cert-*` and `misc-*` beyond includes: report, triage into fixed, `NOLINT(<check>): <reason>` and disabled-with-reason in `.clang-tidy`, then gate.
  - **3.5.3 A chosen subset of `cppcoreguidelines-*`** (`init-variables`, `slicing`, `narrowing-conversions` on the readers, `pro-type-member-init`); not the ownership or bounds families, which fight the `NDArray` views.
  - **3.5.4 `modernize-*` and `readability-identifier-naming`** configured for the house style (`CamelCase` types and methods, `m` prefix members, `r`/`p` prefix parameters, `lower_case` free functions and locals), with the Python and JS API string names untouched.
  - **3.5.5 The gate.** A `tidy-diff` step after `include-cleanup`: `git diff -U0 origin/master... | clang-tidy-diff -p1 -path <build>` over the enabled set, failing on any finding in changed lines; the full report runs on `master` beside SonarCloud.
  - **3.5.6 Triage the overlap** with SonarCloud's C++ rules and 3.10 once (a table in [static analysis](./static_analysis.md): rule family, owning tool), and close the `performance-*` inventory.
- **ABI.** A fix in an installed header follows the [ABI policy](./abi.md): an inline-body change is Tier B.
- **Done when.** `.clang-tidy` enables the four families' chosen checks and the diff gate is a required check.

### 3.6 Compiler warnings

The C++ build sets no warning flag on any compiler, so what every compiler finds for free is never seen. **M–L**

- **State today.** No `-Wall`, `-Wextra`, `/W4` or `COMPILE_WARNING_AS_ERROR` in `CMakeLists.txt` or `cmake/`; the only `-Wall -Wextra` is `bindings/octave/meshioplusplus/tools/dev_build.sh` line 39. The core's flags accumulate in `_mio_core_copts` (lines 746–752) and are applied **PUBLIC** by `meshioplusplus_configure_core_target()` (lines 1412–1455), so they reach the exported `INTERFACE_COMPILE_OPTIONS` of the installed targets: `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_COVERAGE` (819–827) and `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_SANITIZE` (843–879) use that list on purpose, a warnings option must not. Third-party code compiled into the core's own source list: `pugixml.cpp` (line 708) and the gidpost C sources (716); the Eigen, JSON and Polyscope submodules are under `src/cpp/third_party/`.
- **Steps.**
  - **3.6.1 The option (landed).** `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_WARNINGS` and `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_WARNINGS_AS_ERRORS` in `CMakeLists.txt`, with `meshioplusplus_target_warnings(target)` applied PRIVATE at the end of the file to `meshioplusplus_core_obj`, the core variants, `_core`, the C API, the CLI, the tests and the benchmarks; `pugixml.cpp` and the gidpost sources are compiled with `-w`, and third-party include directories (`pugixml`, `eigen`, `nlohmann/json`, `gidpost`, `polyscope`) are marked `SYSTEM`. `--warnings` and `--warnings-as-errors` in `build/configure.sh` and `.bat`; `tests/consumer/CMakeLists.txt` fails when an installed target exports a `-W` option. Remaining: confirm the consumer check on an `install-cpp` run in CI.
  - **3.6.2 The count.** GCC 13 on `master` (SEQ, tests and C API, HDF5 and netCDF off): 258 distinct warnings, none in vendored code: `-Wdangling-reference` 97 (16 in `src/cpp`, 81 in `tests/cpp`), `-Wdangling-else` 68 (all in `tests/cpp`), `-Warray-bounds` 38 (37 reported inside libstdc++'s heap and algorithm headers, 1 in `src/cpp`), `-Wunused-function` 10, `-Wmaybe-uninitialized` 9, `-Wunused-variable` 7, `-Wmissing-field-initializers` 7, `-Wunused-parameter` 6, `-Wrange-loop-construct` 5, `-Wcomment` 5, `-Wunused-but-set-variable` 2, `-Wparentheses` 2. The `warnings` job of `ci.yml` (advisory, GCC and Clang) records the count in its step summary and uploads the log; record the Clang and MSVC counts here from its first run.
  - **3.6.3 To zero, then `-Werror`.** First cleanup batch landed (`RegistryRow` default initializers, `neuroglancer.cpp` stray `#pragma once`, `grid_lattice.cpp` unused `nz`, `edge_sampler.cpp` unused `rEnds`); remaining: fix the remaining families, readers first, then `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_WARNINGS_AS_ERRORS=ON` in one `cpp-tests` leg (GCC), `cpp-sanitize` (Clang) and one Windows leg.
  - **3.6.4 The warnings that find defects in parsing code**, one at a time, each to zero before the next: `-Wshadow`, `-Wconversion` and `-Wsign-conversion` on `src/cpp/src/formats/` and `detail/parse_guard.hpp` first (a narrowed header count is how a reader overflows), `-Wold-style-cast`, `-Wnull-dereference`, `-Wimplicit-fallthrough`, `-Wformat=2`.
  - **3.6.5 Warning-free headers for consumers.** `tests/consumer` and the `amalgamation` job's smoke compile build with `-Wall -Wextra -Werror` (and `/W4 /WX`), so a user's strict build does not break on meshio++'s installed headers or the single header.
- **Done when.** The three `-Werror` legs are green and the consumer test compiles the headers at that level.

### 3.7 Typed Python

A user's mypy or pyright sees every call as `Any`, and an editor completes nothing from the extension. **M–L**

- **State today.** `src/python/meshioplusplus/py.typed` (checked in `tests/wheel_base_smoke.py`), `[tool.mypy]` in `pyproject.toml`, `[testenv:typecheck]` in `tox.ini`, strict annotations on `_fallback.py`, `_shims.py`, `_files.py` and `_vtk_codecs.py`, and `tests/python/typing/usage.py` (passing `mypy --follow-imports=silent --strict`) are landed; `flake8-bugbear` is now installed in `.pre-commit-config.yaml` and `tox.ini`. No `_core.pyi` stub yet, and annotated definitions in `__init__.py`, `_mesh.py` and `_helpers.py` remain partial (`_helpers.py` 19 of 28, `_mesh.py` 7 of 22). `__all__` is a static list (`src/python/meshioplusplus/__init__.py` line 631, about 357 names) resolved lazily through `_LAZY_ATTRS` (376) and a module `__getattr__` (992–1013), with the imports mirrored under `TYPE_CHECKING`, so a checker can follow them. `_core` is one pybind11 module (`bindings/python/_core.cpp`, 10,482 lines). The MCP tool functions (`mcp/_tools.py`) have no annotations; the typed wrappers are in `mcp/_server.py`.
- **Steps.**
  - **3.7.1 Stubs for `_core`.** Generate `src/python/meshioplusplus/_core.pyi` with pybind11-stubgen from a built extension (`tools/gen_core_stubs.sh`), commit it, and add a freshness check to the `test` job in the manner of `tools/amalgamate.sh --check`; fix the pybind signatures that render as raw C++ types (missing `py::arg` names, unregistered types) until stubgen reports none.

### 3.9 More sanitizers and hardened runs

- **State today.** `cpp-sanitize` (`ci.yml`) runs ASan and UBSan with Clang on the SEQ backend without Python, and `cpp-tsan` (advisory) runs ThreadSanitizer with Clang on the `THREADS` pool (`tools/sanitizers/tsan.supp`). `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_SANITIZE` takes any list; `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_SANITIZE_PYTHON=ON` lifts the Python build guard in `CMakeLists.txt` for LD_PRELOAD debugging sessions. The parallel backends are in `include/meshioplusplus/parallel.hpp`; the THREADS pool, new in v19.0.0, is `src/cpp/src/parallel_threads.cpp`. Parallel tests: suites `Parallel`, `ParallelThreads`, `LibraryLock`, and `tests/cpp/thread_sweep.hpp` in 20 test files.
- **Steps.**
  - **3.9.1 ThreadSanitizer.** `tools/sanitizers/tsan.supp` and the advisory `cpp-tsan` CI matrix (`THREADS` and `TBB` backends, `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_NUM_THREADS=4`, excluding `ParallelThreads.ForkedChildRebuildsThePool` which TSan's runtime rejects after multi-threaded fork) are landed and GCC TSan verified zero races locally on `ParallelThreads` and `LibraryLock`; remaining: promote both legs to required after green CI runs, and optionally add OpenMP with an instrumented `libomp`. **M**
  - **3.9.2 The Python extension under ASan.** `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_SANITIZE_PYTHON=ON` is landed in `CMakeLists.txt`; remaining: add a CI job that builds `_core` with ASan and runs the fast `tests/python` subset with `LD_PRELOAD=$(clang -print-file-name=libclang_rt.asan-x86_64.so)` and `ASAN_OPTIONS=detect_leaks=0` targeting `bindings/python/np_conversions.hpp`. **M**
  - **3.9.3 Hardened standard libraries (landed).** `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_HARDENED_STL` defines `_GLIBCXX_ASSERTIONS` and libc++'s extensive hardening mode PRIVATE on the same targets as the warnings, and the `cpp-hardened` job of `ci.yml` builds and runs the C++ tests on one libstdc++ leg (required) and one libc++ leg (advisory, HDF5 and netCDF off, since the distribution's builds link libstdc++). The libc++ leg's first run found that the core did not build with libc++ 18, Ubuntu 24.04's: `std::atomic_ref` (libc++ 19) in `operations/octree.cpp`, now `std::vector<std::atomic<char>>`. It builds with `-k 0`, so the next run lists every remaining incompatibility; fix them, then make the leg required. Any abort the hardened tests find is a defect to list in §1. **S**
  - **3.9.4 MemorySanitizer** on `meshioplusplus_fuzz_read` for the formats that need no external library (everything outside `tools/fuzz/library_formats.txt`), with an MSan-instrumented libc++; advisory. **M**
- **Done when.** `cpp-tsan` is a required check on THREADS and TBB.

### 3.11 Coverage and test depth

- **3.11.1 Coverage on pull requests.** The `coverage` job runs on pull requests and `.codecov.yml` sets a patch status (80%, threshold 5%). The first pull-request run overran its 90 minutes: the FitCad numeric suites take minutes each at `-O0` with gcov, so a pull request now runs the instrumented C++ suite in parallel with a private `TMPDIR` per test process and without the slow numeric families (`FitCad`, `OctreeMesh`, `MeshCad`, `PatchNetwork`, `HexDualSchemes`, two single tests: about 5% of the cases; the other jobs run them optimized), and runs in their place the small `Quick*` versions of those tests (`tests/cpp/test_quick_*.cpp`: skins of a few hundred triangles, about 5 minutes instrumented in all) and the small suites the name prefixes also caught, and `master` and release tags run the same reduced selection (the full instrumented run overran its 240-minute budget). A pull-request run takes about two and a half hours end to end (C++ 25 to 45 minutes; Python build plus pytest under `--cov` about 110, so that step has 180); if that is too slow for review, shard the Python step or run pytest-xdist with coverage's parallel mode. Remaining: confirm that the reduced run fits on every event, and keep the `coverage-reports` artifact SonarCloud consumes (7.2). **S**
- **3.11.3 A mutation-testing spike.** On the shared kernels (`math_utils.hpp`, `geometry_utils.hpp`, `fem_kernels.hpp`, `detail/parse_guard.hpp`) with Mull against `meshioplusplus_tests`, and on `_helpers.py` and one format twin with mutmut: the surviving mutants name covered lines no assertion checks. Record the score and the survivors in [static analysis](./static_analysis.md), fix the tests, then decide whether a monthly job is worth its minutes. **S–M**
- **3.11.4 Examples that run.** `.github/workflows/notebooks.yml` executes the 74 notebooks of `example/python` weekly into a scratch directory and fails on an exception in any cell. The job sorts each failure: a notebook that needs a build option or library the CI wheel lacks (CutCells, OpenCASCADE, an optional package; `tools/classify_notebook_error.py`) is listed as skipped in the job summary, anything else fails the job. Its first classified run (74 notebooks) skipped four for an optional build or package (CutCells, OpenCASCADE, ADIOS2, scipy) and failed 11: seven read Git LFS pointer files because the job fetched only `example/data` (it now runs `tools/fetch-lfs.sh`, like `ci.yml`), one needs QuadriFlow or Instant Meshes on `PATH` (now classified as skipped), `scipy` is now installed, and two (a `cannot reshape array of size 0` and a runner error with no message) are read again after the LFS fix. The job now installs the optional builds the notebooks need (OpenCASCADE, CutCells, QuadriFlow, ADIOS2, scipy and the plotting stack; the `cannot reshape` was a Nastran HDF5 writer bug, fixed), allows 30 minutes per cell, and lists the slowest notebooks in its summary: on the next runs all 74 notebooks ran, none skipped and none failed, in about an hour. Remaining: the C++, Julia, R and Octave notebooks, and `pytest --codeblocks README.md doc/` in the `test` job, which needs the blocks that use optional packages or files marked `<!--pytest-codeblocks:skip-->` after a first run shows which. **S–M**
- **3.11.5 Low-coverage production hotspots ranked by SonarCloud.** Live SonarCloud's `uncovered_lines` ranking (November 2026, overall 87.9%) shows two distinct gaps: first, `sonar-project.properties` scans `bindings/r/meshioplusplus/tests/testthat/test-meshioplusplus.R` as production source (1,948 uncovered lines at 0.0%; fixed in 7.5); second, six real production files hold the bulk of uncovered core and twin code: `src/cpp/src/operations/pipeline.cpp` (70.3%, 1,059 uncovered lines — v2 error/edge branches), `src/cpp/src/detail/external_mesher.cpp` (6.4%, 440 uncovered lines), `src/python/meshioplusplus/vtkhdf/_vtkhdf.py` (61.1%, 312 uncovered), `src/python/meshioplusplus/abaqus/_abaqus.py` (20.7%, 241 uncovered — bypassed whenever `_core` is built), `src/python/meshioplusplus/vtx/_vtx.py` (10.5%, 230 uncovered) and `src/cpp/src/operations/measure.cpp` (70.4%, 230 uncovered). Mock-driver C++ tests (`tests/cpp/test_external_mesher.cpp`) and extended validation tests in `tests/cpp/test_measure.cpp` and `tests/cpp/test_pipeline.cpp` are landed; remaining: confirm >80% line coverage in SonarCloud's `component_tree` and directly exercise the Python twins (`_vtkhdf.py`, `_abaqus.py`, `_vtx.py`) where `_core` masks them. **Done when** each of the six files exceeds 80% line coverage in SonarCloud's `component_tree`. **M**

### 3.12 Fuzzing beyond readers

After 3.1, so new targets run where the old ones do. **M**

- **State today.** `tests/fuzz/fuzz_read.cpp` reads every registry format (through a scratch file: the registry is path-only, `registry.hpp` has no in-memory read or write); `fuzz_mesh_cad.cpp` holds the two operation targets, dispatched by `MIO_FUZZ_TARGET` or the binary name and reporting through `fuzz_op_finding`; `hdf5_mutator.cpp` is the structure-aware HDF5 mutator. Targets are defined at `CMakeLists.txt` lines 2144–2192 and regressions become `fuzz_regression_<dir>` tests at 2193–2219 (`op_*` directories replay through `meshioplusplus_fuzz_ops_replay`). `fuzz.yml` runs weekly and on pull requests that touch the fuzz paths.
- **Steps.**
  - **3.12.1 A round-trip target** in `tests/fuzz/fuzz_roundtrip.cpp`: read the input as format F; if it reads, write it to a scratch path with `registry_writers().at(F)`, read that, and call `fuzz_op_finding` when the second read throws or differs from the first beyond the format's declared losses (compare counts, cell types and array names and shapes first; values only for the formats `conformance_spec.py` declares exact). Finds writer crashes and reader/writer disagreements no reader target can.
  - **3.12.2 A differential replay**, not a libFuzzer target: `tools/fuzz/diff_engines.py` replays each text format's corpus through `_core` (`MESHIOPLUSPLUS_STRICT_CORE=1`) and the Python twin and reports inputs where one reads and the other raises, or the meshes differ. Weekly, in `fuzz.yml`, after the campaign, on its corpus.
  - **3.12.3 The C API** as `tests/fuzz/fuzz_c_api.cpp`: a byte-driven sequence of `mio_mesh_*` builders, getters after mutation (borrow windows), option structs with random reserved tails, and `mio_read` on the remaining bytes; any crash or a non-status failure is a finding.
  - **3.12.4 Operations on untrusted meshes:** `clean`, `repair`, `convert_cells`, `decimate`, `extract_surface`, `tetrahedralize`'s native backend, each taking the mesh from the reader target's corpus for a compact format (`off`, `medit`) so the fuzzer mutates meshes, with the allowed exceptions listed per operation as `fuzz_mesh_cad.cpp` does.
  - **3.12.5 Parsers of text the user writes:** the pipeline JSON (`pipe_document_from_json`) and the native CLI's argument vector.
  - Register each in `CMakeLists.txt`, `tools/fuzz/run_campaign.sh`, the OSS-Fuzz `build.sh` and [fuzzing](./fuzzing.md).
- **Done when.** The weekly campaign runs the new targets and their first findings are fixed with regressions under `tests/fuzz/regressions/`.

### 3.13 A mechanical ABI and API diff

- **State today.** `tools/check-abi-version.sh` fails when an installed header changed since the last tag without a version bump or an [ABI review](./abi_reviews.md) row naming it; `tests/cpp/test_abi_layout.cpp` pins 195 types, on Linux x86-64 with libstdc++ only; a link-time sentinel covers version mismatch. Nothing sees an exported symbol that changed or disappeared, or a type nobody listed. The shared libraries are `meshioplusplus` (C API, `SOVERSION 0`, `CMakeLists.txt` lines 1928–1936) and `meshioplusplus_core_<backend>` (`SOVERSION` = the ABI version, shared with `BUILD_SHARED_LIBS` or `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_PYTHON_SHARED_CORE`).

  - **3.13.2 `abidiff` on the shared core, advisory**, with a suppression file for `detail::` and with the report attached to the pull request: a changed type with no ABI bump is what the review row should have caught. **S**
  - **3.13.3 A Python API diff (landed).** `tools/gen_api_dump.py` writes `tools/api/python_api.json` (374 names: 254 functions, 101 submodules, 16 classes, 3 constants, each with its signature and a class's public methods), `tests/python/test_api_dump.py` holds it fresh in CI's `test` job whenever the extension is built, and the `lint` job runs `tools/check_api_changes.py` on pull requests: a removed name, a removed or renamed parameter, a new required parameter, a changed default or a removed method needs a `**Breaking:**` line in `CHANGELOG.md`. Remaining: the MCP parity test reading the same file and signatures for the two classes pybind11 gives none (they record `null`). **S**
- **Done when.** Removing a C function in a test branch fails `cpp-install`.

### 3.15 The platform matrix

- **State today.** `ubuntu-24.04`'s GCC 13, an unversioned apt Clang, Apple Clang, MSVC; gcc-toolset-13 in the manylinux_2_34 images; CPython 3.10 to 3.14 with the GIL; `CIBW_SKIP: "*-musllinux* *_i686 *-win32"` (`wheels.yml` lines 108–109). No minimum compiler is stated anywhere but the Spack recipe's `conflicts` below GCC 10; wheel comments say `<format>` needs GCC 13.

(b) Audit the state the bindings share: the leaked class caches `sMesh`, `sRegion`, `sRows`, `sFaces` (`np_conversions.hpp` lines 177–200, set "under the GIL"), `_ALL_LOADED` and the three `_Registry` dicts in `_helpers.py` (lines 61 and 113–115), `_strict` in `_fallback.py`, the module `__getattr__` writing `globals()`, the unlocked caches in `nastran_op2/_write.py` (1795), `nastran_h5/_write.py` (98) and `_tessellation.py` (907), `_LIB` in `szplt/_szplt.py`. (c) Declare the module (`PYBIND11_MODULE(_core, m, py::mod_gil_not_used())`, `_core.cpp` line 1998) only when 3.9.1's ThreadSanitizer run of a threaded pytest subset is clean. (d) Add `cp313t-* cp314t-*` to `CIBW_BUILD` (the patterns `cp313-*` do not match them) and a `3.14t` entry to the `test` matrix. Without the wheel a free-threaded interpreter re-enables the GIL on import, with a warning. **M–L**

Every binary reader and writer states a byte order through `detail/byteswap.hpp`; nothing has run them where the host's differs. Expect findings: list them in §1 as they appear. **S–M**
  - **3.15.4 Stated compilers.** The advisory `compiler-matrix` job of `ci.yml` builds the core with GCC 11 to 14 and Clang 15 to 18 on ubuntu-24.04. First run: GCC 11 failed on one line of `formats/gmsh.cpp` (fixed); Clang 15 cannot parse libstdc++ 14's `<ranges>`/`<format>` (Ubuntu 24.04's default) and has no `--gcc-install-dir` to pick libstdc++ 13, so it is out of the matrix (it needs a container image with an older libstdc++) and the Clang legs, 16 to 18, build with `--gcc-install-dir` pointing at GCC 13 (removing GCC 14's packages instead broke the link); read the next run for the lowest compiler that builds. Remaining: read the first run, state the lowest passing compiler in [installation](./installation.md) and `README.md`, make that leg required, and keep the newest as an advisory leg. **S**
  - **3.15.5 PyPy.** Not queued: pybind11 supports it, no user has asked.
- **Done when.** `pip install meshioplusplus` on `python3.14t` and on Alpine installs a wheel, and the s390x job is green.

### 3.18 Compatibility with upstream meshio's own tests

*Verify first.* Nothing in `tests/python` runs upstream meshio's suite against meshio++, so the claim of being a drop-in for `import meshio` rests on meshio++'s own tests. **S–M**

- **Steps.**
  - **3.18.1 A shim run.** A scheduled job in `ci.yml` (weekly, advisory): install the last upstream `meshio` sdist's `tests/` into a scratch directory, alias `import meshio` to `meshioplusplus` through a `conftest.py` that sets `sys.modules["meshio"]`, run it, and write the failures to a committed `tests/python/upstream_meshio_known.txt`.
  - **3.18.2 Classify each failure** as a bug here (fix it, or add it to §1 if it loses data), a documented divergence (a line on the format page and in the [README](https://github.com/loumalouomega/meshioplusplus/blob/main/README.md)'s comparison), or an upstream defect.
- **Done when.** The job passes with the known-failures file as its only allowance, and every entry in it cites a page.

### 3.19 Index and identifier widths

`tests/python/id_widths_spec.py` writes an int64 point array and an int64 cell array of `2**31 + 5` and up to every writable format that has a conformance row, reads it back, and classifies the outcome; the same probe runs on the id arrays (`abaqus:id`, `global:node_id`, `libmesh:id`, `mdpa:id`, `med:num`, `radioss:node_id`) of the formats that take ids through the mesh model. `tools/gen_id_widths_table.py` renders both as [integer widths](./id_widths.md), and `tests/python/test_id_widths.py` fails on a silent wrap, on a refusal that does not name the format and on any drift from the declared tables. The probe found and fixed five defects: GIFTI wrapped 64-bit integers to 32 bits (a value of `2**31 + 5` came back as `-2147483643`), the Python Netgen writer tried to write one name-table line per id up to `2**31 + 5` where the native writer's 10,000,000-entry budget refuses it, ADCIRC's native reader cut a `long long` time step to `int`, the Radioss animation writers wrapped ids to their 32-bit file integers, and a large `libmesh:id` made both libMesh writers allocate a node table of that many rows. Each is now a `WriteError` or `ReadError` naming the format, in both engines. The formats that store integers as single-precision floats or a few digits of text (`cae`, `ensight`, `frd`, `gid`, `gltf`, `marc_t19`, `radioss_anim`, `xplt`) warn once per array above `2**24`, through `detail::warn_integer_precision` and its Python twin. Clang's `-Wshorten-64-to-32` over `src/cpp/src/formats` found one narrowing (the ADCIRC step): the readers cast explicitly everywhere else. **S**

- **Remaining.**
  - **3.19.4 The wider audit.** The `narrowing` job of `ci.yml` (advisory, Clang) lists every `-Wshorten-64-to-32` in the core, not only the formats; triage its first count, fixing each finding or turning it into a check that throws naming the format and the value. The formats whose ids cannot be set through the mesh model (Nastran, Exodus' own numbering, MSH 4.1, CGNS, VTKHDF) are documented from their pages in [integer widths](./id_widths.md), not probed.
- **Done when.** The `narrowing` job's first count is triaged to zero unexplained findings.

### 3.21 Two master CI failures to look into {#master-ci-failures}

Noticed on 14 November 2026 while watching PR #234; both are `master` push runs of `ci.yml`, and neither was diagnosed (GitHub no longer served their logs).

- **Coverage (Python + C++) hung for six hours** on the merge of PR #233 (`4f5e80dd`, run 43375855195): its step "Build and run the C++ tests (instrumented)" started at 06:08 UTC and was cancelled at 12:03 by GitHub's job limit, so the Python half of the job never ran. Find out whether one instrumented C++ test deadlocks or the build only crawls; add a per-test and a step `timeout-minutes` so a hang fails in minutes with the test's name, and watch the job on the next runs.
- **MCP server (`mcp>=2,<3`) stopped in its install step** on the same run (job 131722706593): "Install meshio++ with the mcp and dashboard extras" began at 06:57 and the job failed at 07:48 with no later step run. Check whether `pip install` of the `mcp` and `dashboard` extras can hang (a resolver backtrack on the `mcp>=2,<3` window, or an index stall), and give the step a timeout.
- The run before it (the v20.1.0 merge, `ebfdcc67`, 11 November) failed 35 jobs, every one in an install step (apt, R, Playwright, GL runtimes): a package-mirror outage, nothing to fix beyond retries.
- **Capped, not diagnosed.** The `coverage` job's two long steps have `timeout-minutes: 90` and the `mcp` install step 20, so a hang fails in minutes instead of six hours. The cause of either hang is still unknown; watch the next `master` runs.
- **Done when.** Two consecutive `master` runs finish with both jobs green, or the hang is explained and capped by a timeout.

### 3.22 The `kahip` 3.27 wheel crashes on import {#kahip-327}

The `kahip` PyPI wheel now resolves to 3.27 (noticed on 14 November 2026; I have not checked its release date), and `import kahip` dies with `Illegal instruction` (SIGILL, inside the wheel's extension module, before any meshio++ code) on the GitHub-hosted runners; master's last green run (13 November) installed 3.25. The extra (`pyproject.toml`, `kahip<3.27`) and the CI step that installs it are held below 3.27. Check what 3.27 was built for (a `-march=native` build would fail for users on older CPUs too), report it upstream, and lift the bound once a release imports on the runners. **S**

### 3.23 clice beside clang-tidy {#clice}

Weigh [clice](https://github.com/clice-io/clice) as a second way to run the checks of 3.5 and as the tool that understands C++20 modules, without making CI depend on it. **S–M, a spike first; verify first**

- **What it is, from its README (November 2026, nothing run here yet).** An Apache-2.0 C/C++ language server built on LLVM/Clang, whose 1.0 is its first stable release, with binaries for Linux, macOS and Windows on x64 and arm64. It reads `compile_commands.json`, builds C++20 named modules on demand and caches PCHs and PCMs on disk, compiles a header that is not self-contained in the context of a file that includes it, and resolves dependent names in templates without concrete arguments. Its command line works without the server: `clice lint` runs clang-tidy over the project's units in a process pool (`--index` builds the index in the same parse), `clice index` and `clice query` answer references and call hierarchies in JSON from an on-disk index, and `format`, `refactor`, `analyze` and `modularize` are further subcommands.
- **State today.** `.clang-tidy` is `Checks: '-*,misc-include-cleaner'` and `tools/performance_tidy.py` drives clang-tidy itself, per translation unit with `--jobs` (3.5). No language-server configuration is committed (`.vscode/` holds `tasks.json` only, there is no `.clangd`). Module scanning is switched off for the tidy audit (`CMAKE_CXX_SCAN_FOR_MODULES=OFF`), and 4.14's named module does not exist yet. Nothing answers "what refers to this" mechanically: 3.4's cleaning items and `tools/dup_report.py` work from text.
- **Steps.**
  - **3.23.1 The spike.** Pin one clice release and record the Clang it embeds. Run `clice lint` on the compile database of `build/performance-tidy-MESHIO` with the `performance-*` set and compare with `tools/performance_tidy.py`: the findings must be the same set (file, check, message; the counts are those of [benchmarks](./benchmarks.md#clang-tidy-performance-audit)), and the wall time is the reason to go on or stop. A table in [static analysis](./static_analysis.md).
  - **3.23.2 An optional engine.** If the findings agree and the run is faster, 3.5.1's `tools/tidy_report.py` takes `--engine clang-tidy|clice`. clang-tidy stays the default and the reference of 3.5.5's gate, so a required check never rests on one young tool; clice is the fast local run and the `master` report.
  - **3.23.3 The index as a cleaning aid.** `clice index` once, then `clice query` for the reference counts and call hierarchies 3.4's items need (an unused helper, the callers of a `detail/` function through the templates of `mesh_api.hpp`), as a documented recipe in [static analysis](./static_analysis.md). Advisory: a zero-reference answer is a candidate to check, since the bindings reach the core by name.
  - **3.23.4 Editor setup.** A short section in [static analysis](./static_analysis.md): how to get a compile database (`CMAKE_EXPORT_COMPILE_COMMANDS=ON`, as `tools/performance_tidy.py` configures its tree), and header contexts for the `detail/*.hpp` headers that are not self-contained. No editor-specific file is committed.
  - **3.23.5 Modules.** `clice modularize` as a probe for [4.14](#414-a-c20-named-module)'s decisions: which of the 283 installed headers do not survive being a module's global fragment, and what the export list would be. A report only, no source change here; once 4.14 lands, clice is the language server that reads `meshioplusplus.cppm`.
- **Risks.** A first stable release: pin the version, keep every use advisory, and close the item with the measured reason under [non-goals](#non-goals-and-decisions-taken) if 3.23.1 shows different findings or no gain.
- **Done when.** The spike's table is in [static analysis](./static_analysis.md), and 3.23.2 has either landed or been declined with its numbers.

### 3.24 Structural decomposition of high-cognitive-complexity translation units {#cognitive-complexity}

Live SonarCloud (`cognitive_complexity` = 160,753 across 467,191 lines, November 2026) concentrates complexity and compile latency in a handful of multi-thousand-line translation units that are also the slowest files in 3.17.3. Splitting them along internal seams improves incremental rebuilds, static-analyzer turnaround and reviewability without changing any ABI or digest. **M–L**

- **State today.** Top files by SonarCloud `cognitive_complexity`: `src/cpp/src/operations/mesh_cad.cpp` (4,179 across 5,840 ncloc), `bindings/c/c_api.cpp` (3,485 across 11,688 ncloc), `src/cpp/src/formats/mfem.cpp` (2,728 / 4,779), `src/cpp/src/formats/nastran_op2.cpp` (2,506 / 4,537), `src/cpp/providers/occt/fit_cad_occt.cpp` (2,491 / 4,080), `src/cpp/src/formats/nastran_h5.cpp` (1,849 / 3,549), `src/cpp/src/formats/openfoam.cpp` (1,822 / 3,591), `src/cpp/src/operations/pipeline.cpp` (1,341 / 4,211) and `src/cpp/cli/main.cpp` (10,860 ncloc). Sources under `src/cpp/src/` and `bindings/c/` are globbed or easily grouped in `CMakeLists.txt`, so internal splits need no public header or ABI change (remembering the amalgamation's unique anonymous-namespace prefix rule).
- **Steps.**
  - **3.24.1 Bindings and CLI monoliths** (paired with 3.17.3): split `bindings/c/c_api.cpp` into `c_api_mesh.cpp`, `c_api_io.cpp`, `c_api_ops_geo.cpp`, `c_api_ops_mesh.cpp`, `c_api_ops_field.cpp` sharing one private `c_api_internal.hpp`; split `src/cpp/cli/main.cpp` into per-family verb units (`cli_io.cpp`, `cli_ops_geo.cpp`, `cli_ops_mesh.cpp`, `cli_data.cpp`, `cli_main.cpp`). **M**
  - **3.24.2 Core operations and formats:** split `mesh_cad.cpp` (wire/trimming, face triangulation, quad recombination, curving), `pipeline.cpp` (JSON schema/parsing, step dispatch, v2 multi-mesh), `nastran_op2.cpp` (record framing, table decoders, writer), `mfem.cpp` (mesh, NURBS, parallel/grid-function) and `openfoam.cpp` (lexer/dict, mesh/fields, decomposed/writer). Keep every split under the determinism check (`tools/bench_ops.sh --hash`), the instruction-count gate (`tools/bench_instructions.py --check`) and `tools/amalgamate.sh --smoke`. **M–L**
- **Done when.** No source file outside generated files exceeds 2,000 ncloc or 1,500 cognitive complexity in SonarCloud, with `tools/bench_ops.sh --hash` and `tools/amalgamate.sh --smoke` unchanged.
