## 4. Ecosystem reach {#ecosystem-reach}

*Admission: getting what exists to the people who would use it.*

### 4.1 Registration and distribution

Mostly logistics, and all blocking real adoption; the effort is small per target but the calendar time is set by each registry's review, so start early. Two rows are not logistics, and say so. **S each, calendar-bound**, except 4.1.2 and 4.1.3.

| Item | Target | Status today |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 4.1.1 | conda-forge | No recipe exists. |
| 4.1.2 | CRAN | Not submittable as it stands: the package needs a C library CRAN's builders do not have. **M** |
| 4.1.3 | Julia General, with a JLL | Not registered; no binary package, so `Pkg.add` would give a package that cannot load its library. **M** |
| 4.1.4 | Blender Extensions Platform | Zips built and attached to every release; only the listing remains. |
| 4.1.5 | ConanCenter, the vcpkg registry | Recipes are CI-validated but in self-hosted shape, not upstream shape. |
| 4.1.6 | A ParaView plugin index | Bundles ship on every release and load in Kitware's binaries; only a listing remains. |
| 4.1.7 | Homebrew, Nix, winget, Scoop | Not started. |
| 4.1.8 | A container image | Not started. |
| 4.1.9 | Octave Packages index, MATLAB File Exchange | Not started. |
| 4.1.10 | MCP registries and hosted directories (Glama, the official MCP Registry, Smithery, Docker) | Not started; [4.18](#hosted-mcp). |

Spack is upstream already (`meshioplusplus` and `py-meshioplusplus` in `spack/spack-packages`), and its bump is part of the release checklist in `AGENTS.md`.

- **4.1.1 conda-forge.** A `staged-recipes` pull request with one recipe, two outputs (`libmeshioplusplus`: the C and C++ libraries and the native CLI; `meshioplusplus`: the Python package). Source: the PyPI sdist (a GitHub tarball lacks the Eigen, JSON and Polyscope submodules). Host dependencies as the `paraview-plugin` job already installs them from conda-forge (`ci.yml` near lines 1900–1915): `hdf5`, `libnetcdf`, `zlib`, `zstd`, `lz4-c`, `bzip2`, `cgns`, `kahip`, `pybind11`, `scikit-build-core`, `numpy`, a C++20 compiler. CMake: `-DMESHIOPLUSPLUS_PREFER_STATIC_DEPS=OFF`, the `WITH_*` options on for what conda-forge has. OpenCASCADE is on by default (`CMakeLists.txt` line 435) and conda-forge has `occt`: decide between an MIT base output with `-DMESHIOPLUSPLUS_WITH_OCCT=OFF` plus a `meshioplusplus-occt` output (the PyPI split, recommended) and one LGPL-linked package. macOS deployment target 13.3. Probe: `conda build` locally, then `conda create -n t meshioplusplus && python -c "import meshioplusplus"`.
- **4.1.2 CRAN.** `bindings/r/meshioplusplus/DESCRIPTION` says `SystemRequirements: meshio++ C library (libmeshioplusplus), C99`, and `configure` finds an installed library (`MESHIOPLUSPLUS_CFLAGS`/`LIBS`, `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_HOME`, then `pkg-config`) or exits; `R CMD check --as-cran` passes in CI only because the `r` job builds the C API first (`ci.yml` lines 2640–2663). CRAN's machines have no `libmeshioplusplus`, so the package would fail there. Options, to decide first: (a) vendor the core's sources into the package tarball (a `tools/make_cran_tarball.sh` copying `src/cpp/include`, `src/cpp/src` and `bindings/c` under `src/`, a `Makevars` that compiles them with `CXX_STD = CXX20`, HDF5 and netCDF off unless found), within CRAN's 5 MB tarball limit and its compile-time limit, which the core's size may exceed: measure both before anything else; (b) a `configure` that downloads a prebuilt static library per platform from the GitHub release, which CRAN tolerates for Windows and macOS binaries but not as the only path on Linux; (c) R-universe instead of CRAN, which builds from the repository with system requirements installable, and needs only a `packages.json`. Recommended: (c) now, (a) if the measurements allow. Also missing: `cran-comments.md`, a `cleanup` script, `inst/COPYRIGHTS` for vendored code. Probe: `R CMD check --as-cran` in a container with no meshio++ installed.
- **4.1.3 Julia.** `bindings/julia/MeshioPlusPlus/Project.toml` has `[compat] julia = "1.9"` only, and `src/libmio.jl` loads `ENV["MESHIOPLUSPLUS_LIB"]` or whatever `Libdl.find_library` finds (lines 35–53); [Julia](./julia.md) says "deliberately no JLL". Steps: (1) a `build_tarballs.jl` for Yggdrasil building the C library (`-DMESHIOPLUSPLUS_BUILD_C_API=ON`, Python off, HDF5 and netCDF from `HDF5_jll` and `NetCDF_jll`) for the platforms BinaryBuilder supports with C++20, giving `meshioplusplus_jll`; (2) `libmio.jl` prefers the JLL's `libmeshioplusplus` and keeps the environment override, with `meshioplusplus_jll` in `[deps]` and `[compat]`; (3) register through Registrator with `subdir=bindings/julia/MeshioPlusPlus` and TagBot for tags. The binding is GPL-3.0 (`bindings/julia/LICENSE`) while the library is MIT: confirm that is intended before the registration makes it permanent. Probe: `Pkg.add("MeshioPlusPlus"); using MeshioPlusPlus; MeshioPlusPlus.version()` in a clean depot.
- **4.1.4 Blender.** `src/blender/blender_manifest.toml.in` (`id meshioplusplus`, `license ["SPDX:MIT"]`, `blender_version_min 4.2.0`), built per platform by `wheels.yml`'s `blender_extension` job with `cp311` wheels. Submit at extensions.blender.org; the review asks for the bundled wheels' licences (the base wheel is MIT with static permissive libraries: point to `LICENSE-BINARIES.md`). Add the listing to [Blender](./blender.md).
- **4.1.5 ConanCenter and vcpkg.** Conan: the real recipe is the root `conanfile.py`, which uses `exports_sources` with local paths; `packages/conan/` is the test package. ConanCenter needs `recipes/meshioplusplus/all/{conanfile.py,conandata.yml,test_package/}` with `source()` calling `get(**self.conan_data["sources"][version])` on the release tarball and its `sha256`, and dependencies as ConanCenter requirements rather than vendored. vcpkg: `packages/vcpkg/meshioplusplus/portfile.cmake` has `SHA512 0` and `HEAD_REF main`, and a private overlay port `packages/vcpkg/libaec/`; upstream needs the real hash, a `versions/` entry (`vcpkg x-add-version`), and either the upstream `libaec` or a pull request fixing it there first. Both recipes force Eigen and JSON off, which is right for a registry build. Generate both upstream forms from the self-hosted ones in the release job, so they cannot drift.
- **4.1.6 ParaView.** `tools/paraview-meshioplusplus-plugin.py` with the bundles of `src/paraview/build_bundle.py`. There is no central index; the listing is a page on the ParaView Discourse "Plugins" category and an entry in Kitware's community-plugin list, with the install lines of [ParaView plugin](./paraview_plugin.md#installation).
- **4.1.7 Homebrew, Nix.** A formula in a tap first (`loumalouomega/homebrew-meshioplusplus`: the native CLI and the C and C++ libraries, `depends_on "hdf5"`, `"netcdf"`, `"cmake" => :build`), homebrew-core once it has users; a `flake.nix` at the repository root exposing the CLI and the Python package, then nixpkgs. The release binaries cover the CLI meanwhile. Add **winget and Scoop** manifests for the Windows CLI archive in the same step (a `winget-pkgs` pull request and a Scoop bucket; the `cli.yml` Windows leg is the source), and state in [CLI](./cli.md) which verbs match upstream meshio's CLI (`convert`, `info`, `binary`, `ascii`, `compress`, `decompress`, `increase-order`, `decrease-order`) so a script of its calls runs unchanged: *verify first* by running each.
- **4.1.8 A container image.** `ghcr.io/loumalouomega/meshioplusplus:<version>`: a slim Python base with `pip install "meshioplusplus[all,addons]"` and the native CLI archive, built and pushed by a job in `wheels.yml` after publishing, with 3.14.5's attestation; for CI pipelines and HPC (Apptainer pulls it). Only the OSS-Fuzz Dockerfile exists today. The MCP server's image, with its stdio and HTTP entry points, is [4.18.1](#hosted-mcp), which shares this item's job in `wheels.yml`.
- **4.1.9 Octave and MATLAB.** `pkg install` takes a tarball built by hand ([Octave / MATLAB](./octave.md)). Attach `meshioplusplus-octave-<version>.tar.gz` to the release (a step in `wheels.yml`: `tar -C bindings/octave -czf …`), then a pull request to `gnu-octave/packages` with the package's YAML. MATLAB itself has never been run in CI: add a `matlab-actions/setup-matlab` leg to the `octave` job running the same test directory before listing on File Exchange.

### 4.2 Rust bindings

Over the C API: the next language by scientific adoption after Julia and R. **M**

- **State today.** No `bindings/rust`. The header `bindings/c/include/meshioplusplus/meshioplusplus.h` (9,181 lines; 639 functions, about 163 structs, about 30 enums) is C99, includes only `stddef.h` and `stdint.h`, and CI compiles a `-std=c99` consumer. Conventions (header lines 25–40): a status or `NULL`/`-1` return with the thread-local `mio_last_error()`; setters copy; getters borrow until the next mutation; row-major, 0-based; string getters follow `snprintf`. Discovery: `meshioplusplus.pc` and `find_package(meshioplusplus)`.
- **Design.**
  - `meshioplusplus-sys`: `bindgen` at build time from the installed header (`pkg-config` crate; `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_DIR` override), with care for: `MIO_API` needing `MIO_SHARED` on Windows, which the `.pc` does not set; the `MIO_CELL_TYPES(X)` X-macro behind `enum mio_cell_type` (lines 178–208), which bindgen expands; the function-like `MIO_VERSION_AT_LEAST`, which it skips (re-express as a `const fn`); enums as `newtype` constants, since the C side may add values. A `vendored` feature that builds the C library with the `cmake` crate.
  - `meshioplusplus`: safe wrappers. `Mesh` owns a `*mut mio_mesh` and frees it in `Drop`; the borrow window is the lifetime system's job: getters return `&[T]` or `ndarray::ArrayView` tied to `&self`, and every mutating method takes `&mut self`, so "borrows expire on mutation" is checked at compile time. Option structs are built by `*_opts_init` then field assignment behind builder types, never by literal (the reserved tails must stay zero). Errors: a `Result<T, Error>` carrying the status and `mio_last_error()`.
- **Steps.** (1) `bindings/rust/{meshioplusplus-sys,meshioplusplus}` as a workspace, version in step with the others (a row in `AGENTS.md`'s version table). (2) Tests ported from the Julia suite's sections (`bindings/julia/MeshioPlusPlus/test/runtests.jl`: introspection, shape identity, the borrow window, file round trip, regions, each operation, errors carrying `mio_last_error()`, the XDMF series). (3) A parity guard, which no binding but Octave has today: `tests/python/test_binding_parity.py` parses the C header's function names and checks each is wrapped or listed as exempt, for Julia (`ccall(_sym(:mio_…))` in `src/*.jl`), R and Rust alike. (4) A `rust` CI job in the pattern of `julia` (`ci.yml` lines 2488–2584: build the C API, run the tests against it, run the doc example, check that a missing library fails by name). (5) `doc/rust.md` on the template of `doc/julia.md` (building, array layout, the borrow window, memory, errors, documented gaps, tests), in the *Language surfaces* sidebar group (`doc/.vitepress/config.mts` line 253), and the binding lists in `AGENTS.md`. (6) crates.io publication from the release workflow.
- **Done when.** `cargo test` passes against an installed C library on the three platforms, and the parity test covers every `mio_` function.

### 4.3 Interop phase 2: Open3D and DOLFINx

- **State today.** `src/python/meshioplusplus/_interop.py`: `to_open3d` (lines 1662–1668) and `to_dolfinx` (1671–1678) call `_phase_two`, which raises `NotImplementedError`; neither is in `__all__`; `has_open3d` and `has_dolfinx` (1025–1032) return a hard-coded `False`. The working bridges share a pure payload layer (`_to_vtk_payload` line 469, `_to_triangles_payload` 698, `_to_table_payload` 882; dataclasses `VtkPayload`, `TrianglesPayload`), `_require(module, extra, op)` (1035) for the install hint, and `_copy_note`/`_emit` for the copy report. [Interop](./interop.md) records the intended `_to_tetra_payload` and `_to_dolfinx_payload` with `cell_perm_gmsh`.
- **Steps.**
  - **4.3.1 Open3D.** `to_open3d(mesh, kind="auto")` from `_to_triangles_payload` to `o3d.geometry.TriangleMesh` (or `PointCloud` for a vertex-only mesh, `TetraMesh` through a new `_to_tetra_payload`), using the tensor API (`o3d.t.geometry`, which takes numpy without the `Vector3dVector` copy the stub's message cites) where present; `from_open3d`; colours and normals as point data. `has_open3d` becomes an import probe (`_importable`). An `open3d` extra. **S**
  - **4.3.2 DOLFINx.** `to_dolfinx(mesh, comm=MPI.COMM_SELF)`: one cell type only (refuse a mixed mesh by name), the coordinate element from `basix.ufl.element("Lagrange", cell, degree, shape=(gdim,))`, connectivity permuted from VTK to DOLFINx order with `dolfinx.io.utils.cell_perm_vtk`, `dolfinx.mesh.create_mesh`; cell regions to `meshtags`. `from_dolfinx` through `mesh.geometry.x` and `mesh.geometry.dofmap`. DOLFINx is conda- or apt-only, so no extra: the hint names both. Verify first against the current DOLFINx release's API, which has moved between minors. **M**
  - Both: export in `__all__` with the lazy map (`__init__.py` lines 501–518), `_NOT_TOOLS` entries in `tests/python/test_mcp.py` (lines 2491–2511 hold the interop ones), gated tests in `tests/python/test_interop.py` in the pattern of the trimesh block (line 646), Open3D in the `interop` job's install line (`ci.yml` lines 1813–1847) and a DOLFINx leg in the conda-based `paraview-plugin` job's pattern, the *Phase 2* section of the page, a notebook cell.
- **Done when.** `test_phase_two_targets_are_not_implemented` (line 481) is deleted because both are.

### 4.4 A CLI chatbot

`meshioplusplus chat`, a natural-language entry point from the terminal. The MCP server already exposes the whole Python surface to an agent, but only to a host that speaks MCP; a user with an API key cannot ask the CLI "why does this file fail to convert" or "clean this mesh and tell me what changed". A thin client over existing work, not new mesh functionality. **S–M**

- **State today.** `TOOL_REGISTRY` (`src/python/meshioplusplus/mcp/_tools.py` line 7665) maps 203 names to `{"fn", "wraps", "gated"}` with no schemas; `call_tool(name, kwargs)` (8517) dispatches and `guard` (8507) turns exceptions into `{"error", "error_type"}`; the sandbox is `_resolve` (325–349) under `_ROOT` (`set_root`, 269); results pass `_sanitize`/`_json_safe` (352–391). `mcp/_http.py` already dispatches `/api/tools/{name}` through `call_tool` (lines 200–220). Schemas exist only as the typed wrappers of `mcp/_server.py`, which need the `mcp` SDK to list.
- **4.4.1 Reuse, not reimplementation.** The loop calls `_tools.call_tool` in-process: never a second dispatch, sandbox or sanitizer. Every tool added to the registry reaches the chatbot, under the parity `test_every_operation_has_a_tool` gives.
- **4.4.2 Tool schemas without the MCP SDK.** The provider needs a JSON schema per tool, and the `chat` extra must not require `mcp`. Generate them from `_server.py`'s wrapper signatures by introspection (`inspect.signature` and the annotations, the same data the SDK reads) in a small `mcp/_schemas.py`, with a test that it agrees with `server.list_tools()` when the SDK is installed (`test_server_lists_every_registered_tool`, `tests/python/test_mcp.py` line 3405, shows the listing). This is also the committed schema source 4.9.1 asks for.
- **4.4.3 v1 scope.** A stateless-per-turn loop (history and tool schemas out, tool calls executed, results fed back until a plain-text reply) over files under the working directory or `--root` (`set_root`); context lives in the terminal session only; a tool allow-list flag and a `--read-only` mode that withholds every tool whose `wraps` writes; each tool call echoed before it runs.
- **4.4.4 Packaging.** A `chat` extra carrying the provider's SDK, kept out of `[all]`; a new `_cli/_chat.py` with `chat_cmd` and one `_Verb("chat", "_chat", "chat_cmd", …)` row in `_cli/_main.py`'s `_VERBS`; `test_one_verb_imports_its_module_alone` (`tests/python/test_cli.py` line 300) guards that nothing else imports it. Without the extra or a key the verb fails by name, as `_require_server` does in `mcp/__init__.py`. Tests drive the loop with a scripted fake provider. Provider-agnostic wiring and persisted history are follow-ups.
- **4.4.5 Docs.** `doc/chat.md` on the section layout of [MCP](./mcp.md) (installation with the named error, running, the sandbox, what it can call), the sidebar, `README.md`, and the verb table of [CLI](./cli.md).
- **Done when.** `meshioplusplus chat --root examples "what cell types does bracket.vtu hold?"` answers by calling `info`, with a fake provider in CI and a real one by hand.

### 4.6 The dataset manager shows the stored thumbnail

- **State today.** A manifest entry's `Thumbnail` is a PNG path the Python API writes and reads (`src/python/meshioplusplus/_dataset.py`: key at line 49, `thumbnail_path` 278, `set_thumbnail` 715, `render_thumbnail` 721). The browser manager (`src/viewer/src/dataset/`) parses and saves the key (`manifest.ts` lines 47 and 178–190) but shows no stored picture: entries are list chips (`main.ts` `renderEntryList`, lines 312–344), and the only image is the per-manifest overview card (`overview.ts` lines 229–233), fed by a session capture (`thumbs.ts` `captureThumbnail`, called from `main.ts` lines 598–607). A sandboxed file route already exists (`GET /api/files?path=&token=`, `mcp/_http.py` lines 222–239; `ApiClient.fileUrl`, `api.ts` lines 126–132), and a picked directory can serve bytes (`WorkspaceFile.bytes()`, `fs.ts` lines 25–31).
- **Steps.** (1) A `thumbnailUrl(entry)` resolver: the companion's `fileUrl` when connected, else a Blob URL from the workspace file, else none. (2) Show it in the detail pane (`renderDetail`, `main.ts` 352–378) and as a small image on each chip, lazily (`loading="lazy"`, revoke Blob URLs on re-render). (3) The overview card prefers the first entry's stored thumbnail over a session capture. (4) "Save preview as thumbnail": write the capture as PNG into the workspace and set the key (the File System Access path is `readwrite` already). (5) Tests: `tests/viewer/dataset.spec.mjs` (a manifest with a thumbnail shows an `<img>` with a non-empty `naturalWidth`) and `tests/viewer/unit/manifest.test.mjs`; update [datasets](./datasets.md#thumbnails), whose line 101 says the manager does not display it yet; rebuild the embedded page (7.3).
- **Done when.** Opening a dataset written by `render_thumbnail` shows its pictures without opening any case. **S**

### 4.7 Split Python packages: Windows

v18.0.0 landed the provider registry, the shared core in the MIT base wheel, the `meshioplusplus-{occt,netgen,mmg}` add-ons, the permissive libraries linked static into the Linux and macOS base wheels, and plugin loading for the native CLI and the C API ([plan](./python_packages_plan.md)). What is left is Windows. **M**

- **4.7.1 The permissive libraries on Windows.** `build/build-permissive-libs.sh` refuses MSYS and Cygwin (lines 30–36) and builds with `-fPIC -fvisibility=hidden` and `make`, so the Windows wheel sets `WITH_HDF5=OFF WITH_NETCDF=OFF` (`wheels.yml` lines 166–167) and Python falls back to h5py and netCDF4 (the `__has_hdf5__`-style flags, `bindings/python/_core.cpp` lines 2005–2057). Steps: a `build/build-permissive-libs.ps1` (or a CMake `ExternalProject` superbuild that serves all three platforms from `build/deps-pins.sh`'s versions and hashes) building zstd, LZ4, bzip2, HDF5, netCDF, CGNS and KaHIP static with `/MD` (the wheel's runtime; `build-occt.sh --msvc-static-runtime` and `build-cutcells.sh` are the MSVC precedents) and `CMAKE_POLICY_DEFAULT_CMP0091=NEW`; HDF5's symbols stay out of the core DLL's export table because `cmake/gen_core_def.py` exports the `meshioplusplus` namespace only; cache the prefix in the Windows leg as the other two do (`wheels.yml` lines 34–41); turn the options on in `CIBW_ENVIRONMENT_WINDOWS`. Probe: `python -c "import meshioplusplus._core as c; assert c.__has_hdf5__"` in the Windows wheel test, and `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_STRICT_CORE=1` reading an XDMF file.
- **4.7.2 Plugins on Windows.** A DLL binds its imports at link time against a named import library, so a plugin cannot borrow its host's symbols as an ELF or Mach-O one does (`ENABLE_EXPORTS`, `CMakeLists.txt` line 2254; `cmake/plugins.cmake` stops with a fatal error on `WIN32`, lines 13–15). The wheel already solves it for Python: a shared `meshioplusplus_core_meshio.dll` with a generated export table (`gen_core_def.py`, wired at `CMakeLists.txt` lines 1572–1589) that the add-ons link. For the native CLI and the C API the same core DLL would be the host both link, which conflicts with the CLI's static runtime (`/MT`, lines 2279–2284; `cli.yml` builds OpenCASCADE with `--msvc-static-runtime`): exceptions and allocations must not cross a runtime boundary. Decision to take: a second Windows CLI flavour built `/MD` against the core DLL, shipped with it and able to load plugins, beside the single-file `/MT` executable that cannot. Then lift the `WIN32` guard, export through `src/cpp/providers/plugin_export.hpp`, and enable `Plugins.*` (`tests/cpp/test_plugins.cpp`; today `NotBuiltOnThisPlatform`, line 123) on Windows.

### 4.8 Provider plugins beside the native CLI release

v18.0.0 lets the native CLI and the C API load providers from shared libraries (`MESHIOPLUSPLUS_PLUGIN_PATH`, [providers and plugins](./cpp_api.md#providers-and-plugins)), but no release ships one: `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_BUILD_PLUGINS` is off (`CMakeLists.txt` lines 1803–1807), no workflow sets it, and `cli.yml` packages the executable and its licence files only. Reopened from the MMG and Polyscope [non-goals](#non-goals-and-decisions-taken), which keep both out of the executable itself. **M each**

- **4.8.1 MMG and Netgen plugins.**
  - **4.8.1.2 Measure the risk.** The executable links `-static-libgcc -static-libstdc++` (`CMakeLists.txt` line 2278) with `ENABLE_EXPORTS`, a plugin needs the host's C++ runtime (`providers.hpp`), and no test loads a plugin into that configuration. In the manylinux container of `cli.yml`: build the fake plugin (`tests/cpp/plugin/fake_plugin.cpp`) and load it into the static executable; throw an exception across the boundary both ways. If it fails, the plugin-capable CLI is a second, dynamically linked flavour, as on Windows (4.7.2).
  - **4.8.1.3 Build and ship.** `-DMESHIOPLUSPLUS_BUILD_PLUGINS=ON` with Netgen and MMG prefixes in the Linux and macOS legs of `cli.yml`, in the same container and toolchain; `meshioplusplus_plugin_mmg` and `meshioplusplus_plugin_netgen` (`cmake/plugins.cmake` lines 21–43) into `meshioplusplus-cli-plugins-<version>-<os>.tar.gz` with the add-on wheels' `COPYING.LESSER` and `RELINKING.md` (they ship in the wheels now); MMG built without ELAS, as the wheel. Document in [CLI](./cli.md) and [MMG backend](./mmg.md).
  - **Probe.** In the release smoke step: `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_PLUGIN_PATH=plugins ./meshioplusplus --version | grep -E "Netgen yes, MMG yes"` (the line `main.cpp` prints at 10788–10805), and `adapt` on a cube exits 0. Windows waits on 4.7.2.
- **4.8.2 Polyscope as a viewer plugin.** The executable stays free of OpenGL, GLFW and X11.
  - **State today.** `src/cpp/cli/polyscope_view.cpp` (`view_mesh` line 252, `screenshot_mesh` 263) is linked `PRIVATE` when `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_WITH_POLYSCOPE` is on (`CMakeLists.txt` lines 2264–2275). Without it: `view --backend polyscope` reports the build flag (`view_not_built`, `view_payload.cpp` lines 638–644), `view --backend auto` falls back to the terminal viewer on a tty, and `screenshot` falls back to the software rasterizer and exits 0 (`main.cpp` lines 8536–8551).
  - **Steps.** A `ViewerProvider` in `providers.hpp` beside the four kinds there (`mName`, `mView(const Mesh&, const ViewOptions&)`, `mScreenshot(const Mesh&, const ViewOptions&, const std::string& path)`; named registration like `add_tet_mesher`: `add_viewer`, `viewer(name)`, `viewer_names()`); a pure ABI addition (an [ABI review](./abi_reviews.md) row, as v18.0.0's were). `cmd_view` and `cmd_screenshot` ask the registry before their fallbacks. `polyscope_view.cpp` built as `meshioplusplus_plugin_polyscope` (MIT) through `meshioplusplus_add_plugin`, added to 4.8.1.3's archive; `--version` lists viewers.
  - **Probe.** A C++ test registers a fake viewer and `view` reaches it (it does not compile today); the `cli-polyscope` job (`ci.yml` lines 2391–2479) loads the plugin instead of linking it. After 4.8.1.

### 4.9 An MCP server for programs that embed meshio++

The [MCP server](./mcp.md) is a Python process. A program that links meshio++ as a C++ library, through the C API or the single header, or that loads the WebAssembly package, has the operations but no way to offer them to an agent, and the Python-free native CLI has no `mcp` verb. **L, design pass first**

- **State today.** Native pieces that exist: `detail/json_value.hpp` (core-private: `JsonValue`, `parse_json`, `write_json`, non-finite numbers written as `null`), which the single header and WASM can carry, unlike the pipeline's JSON front end and `pipeline_report_json`, which need the optional nlohmann; `src/cpp/cli/json_out.hpp` (`JsonOut`, a streaming writer with no truncation and no non-finite count); the registry; `apply_pipeline_step` and `run_pipeline_steps` (`include/meshioplusplus/operations/pipeline.hpp` lines 191 and 198) over `PipelineStep {mOp, mParams}`, with the step table generated from `tools/pipeline_steps.json` (111 steps with each key's kind and default). The Python side: sandbox, read cache (`_CACHE`, `_cache_limit`, `_cache_key`; `_tools.py` lines 450–477), sanitizer (`_MAX_ARRAY = 1000`), 203 tools of which about 100 are named after a pipeline step and about 104 are not (generators, inspection, multi-mesh operations, datasets, training, rendering). No native MCP code exists.
- **4.9.1 Design pass.** Decide and write down in a `doc/mcp_native_plan.md`:
  - *The native tool set*: `read`/`info`/`convert`/`formats`/`sniff` through the registry; one tool per pipeline step type, dispatched through `apply_pipeline_step` and described from `pipeline_steps.json` (never a second dispatch); the generators and the two-mesh operations (`merge`, `interpolate`, `diff`, `hausdorff`), which the pipeline's v2 steps partly cover. Python-only tools (training, datasets, Arrow and Parquet export, the viewers) are recorded exemptions.
  - *One source of truth for schemas*: 4.4.2's generated schema file, committed, with the Python registration and the native server both checked against it; `pipeline_steps.json` gains the per-key descriptions it lacks.
  - *The JSON layer*: promote `detail/json_value.hpp` to what the server uses for parsing and writing, with the truncation wrapper and the non-finite count added there; the pipeline's JSON front end moves onto it too, which removes the nlohmann requirement from `pipeline_report_json` (a prerequisite, and a gain on its own for the single header and WASM).
  - *Whether the Python server delegates* its sandbox and sanitizer to the core once they exist there, or keeps a twin held equal by tests. Recommended: keep the twin for one release, then delegate.
- **4.9.2 The core server.** An installed header `meshioplusplus/mcp.hpp`: `McpServer` with `HandleMessage(std::string_view json) -> std::string` (JSON-RPC 2.0: `initialize`, `tools/list`, `tools/call`, `ping`), so the host owns its transport; `ServeStdio()` for the common case; `McpOptions` (sandbox root, read-cache budget, tool allow-list, host name and version); `AddTool(name, schema, callback)` for host tools. The sandbox, cache and reports keep the Python server's contract ([path sandbox](./mcp.md#path-sandbox), [read cache](./mcp.md#read-cache), [strict JSON](./mcp.md#reports-are-strict-json)): realpath confinement, LRU eviction within a byte budget keyed on the file's identity, `NaN` as `null` with a count, arrays longer than 1,000 elements truncated. HTTP and the dashboard stay Python's.
- **4.9.3 Surfaces.** C: an opaque `mio_mcp_server` with `mio_mcp_server_create(const mio_mcp_opts*)`, `_handle(server, json, out, out_len)` in the `snprintf` convention, `_serve_stdio`, `_free`, and `mio_mcp_server_add_tool` taking a callback with a user pointer: the header's first callback typedef, so state its threading and re-entrancy rules there. The native CLI: a `mcp` row in `kCliVerbs` (`src/cpp/cli/main.cpp` from line 10042). The installable C++ API and the single header. WASM: `mcpHandle(json)` and an integer handle in the module table, the convention `js_bindings.cpp` lines 10441–10462 explain. Thin Fortran, Julia, R and Octave wrappers over the C entry points.
- **4.9.4 Tests and docs.** A parity guard beside `test_every_operation_has_a_tool`: every native tool is a Python tool of the same name and schema, every Python tool is native or a recorded exemption; a scripted JSON-RPC session against the native CLI in the `mcp` job (`ci.yml` lines 1972–2031, which already runs both SDK majors); a fuzz target on `HandleMessage` (3.12.5); an *Embedding the server* section in [MCP](./mcp.md) and the C, WASM and C++ pages; an [ABI review](./abi_reviews.md) row for the new header.
- **Probe.** `meshioplusplus mcp` is an unknown verb in the native CLI, and `mio_mcp_server_handle` does not exist.

### 4.10 Interop phase 3: scikit-fem and xarray

Bridges in the [interop](./interop.md) pattern of 4.3. No module imports either today; verify first each package's current data model. The gmsh Python API is not one of them: it is GPL and in-process, which the decision on Gmsh's code excludes ([non-goals](#non-goals-and-decisions-taken)). **S each**

- **4.10.1 scikit-fem.** `to_skfem(mesh)`: one cell type, to `MeshTri`/`MeshQuad`/`MeshTet`/`MeshHex` and their second-order variants, `p` as points transposed and `t` as connectivity transposed with skfem's local ordering (the hexahedron differs from VTK's: a permutation in the payload layer, tested against `skfem.io.meshio.from_meshio`, which is the reference implementation of the same mapping for meshio); cell regions to `subdomains`, side regions to `boundaries` through `(cell, local facet)` to skfem facet indices. `from_skfem`. Extra `skfem`.
- **4.10.2 xarray.** `to_xarray(mesh, location="point")`: a `Dataset` with a `point` (or `cell`) dimension, a `component` dimension for vectors and tensors, the coordinates as a coordinate variable; for a sequence, `sequence_to_xarray(path)` adds a `time` dimension lazily through `dask.delayed` over the [sequence reader](./sequences.md), which is what makes it more than `to_pandas`. With 2.4.3.2, UGRID attributes on the dataset so `xugrid` and `uxarray` accept it. Extra `xarray`.
- Exports, `_NOT_TOOLS`, gated tests, CI install line and page section as in 4.3.

### 4.11 A .NET binding

Over the C API, after Rust (4.2). Desktop pre- and post-processors and their plugin APIs are largely .NET (Rhino and Grasshopper, the Ansys ACT and SpaceClaim APIs, Femap's and Simcenter's automation). **M**

- **Design.** `bindings/dotnet/MeshioPlusPlus/`: `LibraryImport` source-generated P/Invoke declarations over `meshioplusplus` for `net8.0`; handles as `SafeHandle` subclasses calling the `_free` functions; option structs as `[StructLayout(LayoutKind.Sequential)]` mirrors initialised by the `*_opts_init` calls, with a generated layout test against the sizes the C header documents (the check `c_api.cpp`'s `static_assert`s make on the C side); getters as `ReadOnlySpan<T>` over the borrowed pointer, valid until the next mutation (document it; a debug build tracks a version counter to throw on stale spans); errors as `MeshioException` from the status and `mio_last_error()`. Generate the declarations from the header with ClangSharp, committed and freshness-checked, since 639 functions are too many to keep by hand.
- **Steps.** The project and tests (xUnit, ported from the Julia sections as in 4.2); the parity guard of 4.2 extended; a `dotnet` CI job on the three platforms against the built C API; a NuGet package with `runtimes/<rid>/native/` libraries from the C API build of `cli.yml`'s toolchains; `doc/dotnet.md`, sidebar, `AGENTS.md`'s lists and version table.
- **Not queued.** Go, Java and Swift: no consumer has asked, and each would follow the same pattern. A native Node addon: the WebAssembly package runs in Node.

### 4.12 A Jupyter widget

- **State today.** `view()` in a notebook embeds a self-contained page per cell: `build_page` (`src/python/meshioplusplus/_viewer_browser.py` line 170) fills three payload slots of `_viewer_assets/viewer.html` (a base64 VTP, stats, the view configuration) and `view_browser` (255) shows it as a `data:` URL iframe (303–310). It talks to nothing: no picked cell comes back to Python, and a changed mesh re-sends the 1.5 MB page. The viewer's state is `window.__viewerState` (`src/viewer/src/main.ts`, with `picked` and `selection`).
- **Steps.** An anywidget `MeshWidget` (`src/python/meshioplusplus/_widget.py`, a `widget` extra): the front end is an ES module built from `src/viewer` by a third Vite target (`build:widget`, beside `build:web` and `build:embed`) exporting `render({model, el})`; traitlets for `color_by`, `time_step`, `clip`, `camera` (Python to JS) and `picked`, `selection` (JS to Python, wired where `__viewerState` is written); the mesh crosses as a binary buffer (the VTP bytes, not base64 in JSON), re-sent only when it changes. `view(backend="widget")` returns it; the iframe stays the default and the fallback. Tests: a Playwright test in `tests/viewer/` loading the module with a fake model; a Python test of the traitlets without a front end. A notebook, [viewer](./viewer.md), `_NOT_TOOLS`.
- **Done when.** Picking a cell in JupyterLab sets `widget.picked` in the kernel. **S–M**

### 4.13 A VS Code mesh preview

A custom read-only editor that opens a mesh file in the browser viewer through the WebAssembly package, with the inspection report beside it. It is what [fuzzing](./fuzzing.md) already says exists (3.16.4). **S–M**

- **Steps.** `src/vscode/`: a `package.json` contributing `customEditors` for the extensions the lean artifact reads (generated from the registry's extension table), an `extension.ts` whose `CustomReadonlyEditorProvider` posts the file's bytes to a webview that loads `src/viewer`'s `build:web` output and the lean `@meshioplusplus/wasm` artifact (no native code, no Python; `loadMeshioPlusPlus`, `src/wasm/src/index.mjs` line 471), and shows `readMetadata` and `checkConformity` in a side panel. Bundled with the viewer's Vite build; a strict webview content-security policy (the readers parse untrusted files: the page gets no network and no file access beyond the posted bytes). Tests with `@vscode/test-electron` in a `vscode` CI job; packaging with `vsce` in the release workflow; publication to the Marketplace and Open VSX.
- **Done when.** Opening `bracket.vtu` in VS Code shows it with its cell counts.

### 4.14 A C++20 named module

`import meshioplusplus;` beside the headers. **M, design pass first**

- **State today.** 283 installed headers under `src/cpp/include/meshioplusplus/` with no umbrella header; the only single entry point is the amalgamation (`src/single_include/meshioplusplus/meshioplusplus.hpp`, 308,816 lines). CMake's floor is 3.15, and module scanning is switched off for the tidy audit (`CMAKE_CXX_SCAN_FOR_MODULES=OFF`). Targets are exported from `CMakeLists.txt` lines 1610–1966 with components `C` and `CXX`.
- **Decisions to take first.** [3.23.5](#clice)'s `clice modularize` probe informs all three. (1) The module's shape: a module interface unit `meshioplusplus.cppm` with the headers in the global module fragment and `export using` declarations for the public names (no source change, and the ABI is the headers'), against re-declaring; recommended the first. (2) What is exported: everything outside `detail::`, generated from the headers by a script so it cannot drift, with a freshness check. (3) Whether the single header can be the global fragment, which would make a header-only module possible; measure its compile time first.
- **Steps.** An umbrella header `meshioplusplus/meshioplusplus_all.hpp` (generated; useful on its own); `option(MESHIOPLUSPLUS_BUILD_MODULE)` guarded by `CMAKE_VERSION >= 3.28` and a scanning-capable compiler, adding a `FILE_SET CXX_MODULES` to the installable core targets and exporting it; a second consumer project `tests/consumer_module/` built in `cpp-install` on GCC 14, Clang 17 and MSVC; [C++ API](./cpp_api.md).
- **ABI.** Unchanged: the module wraps the same declarations. An [ABI review](./abi_reviews.md) row for the new umbrella header.

### 4.15 The training-set stores in the core

Reopened from the [non-goal](#non-goals-and-decisions-taken) that keeps `pmsh`, `zarr`, `cae` and `usd` in Python. For `usd` and `cae` its reason holds. For `zarr` and `pmsh` it now costs something: a dataset meshio++ itself wrote cannot be opened from the browser viewer, the dataset manager, Julia, R or the native CLI, and the parity decision's "a Python-only path has no twin to check it against" applies to them as to any format. **M, design pass first**

- **State today.** `zarr/_zarr.py` writes the PhysicsNeMo layout with zarr-python 3 only (Zarr v3): root attributes (`physicsnemo_mesh_type`, `__tensordict__`, provenance), arrays `points` and `cells`, groups `point_data`, `cell_data`, `global_data`, chunks of at most 200,000 rows, Zstandard level 3 or none (lines 81–166). `write_dataset`'s store (`_ml.py` near lines 617–650) writes one group per entry with whichever zarr major is installed and its default codecs (Blosc under v2). `pmsh/_pmsh.py` writes a tensordict memory-map tree: `meta.json` files and raw little-endian blobs. In the core: the JSON DOM (`detail/json_value.hpp`), `vtk_codec_decompress_block` for zlib, LZ4, Zstandard and LZMA (`detail/vtu_binary.hpp` lines 107–157), but Zstandard and LZ4 are off by default (`CMakeLists.txt` lines 264–265; on in the wheels and the full WASM artifact) and there is no Blosc.
- **Decisions to take first.** (1) Scope: `pmsh` first (JSON and raw blobs: no codec, days of work) and Zarr v3 with the two codecs the `zarr` writer itself uses; Zarr v2 and Blosc stores are refused by name, with `write_dataset` changed to write v3 with Zstandard so that everything meshio++ writes is readable. (2) Local directories only: no object-store client in the core; the browser reads a picked directory through the existing workspace layer. (3) A build without Zstandard reads uncompressed stores and refuses compressed ones naming the option, the TecIO pattern.
- **Steps.** By [checklist F](#landing-checklists): `formats/pmsh.{hpp,cpp}` and `formats/zarr.{hpp,cpp}` (directory formats: the registry's directory sniffing, as OpenFOAM and Elmer); the Python packages become shims with the current code as the twin (`plain_shims`); writers too, by the parity decision; conformance declarations; remove the Python-only notes on the binding pages.
- **Sharding and codecs.** After reading `zarr/_zarr.py`: say which Zarr v3 codecs and whether sharding (`sharding_indexed`) are in the native reader's scope, so a store written with default zarr-python 3 chunking and one written with sharding both read, and a codec the reader lacks is refused by name. Specification†: Zarr v3 core.
- **Probe.** `meshioplusplus info store.zarr` in the native CLI, and the dataset manager opening a `.pmsh` entry.

### 4.16 Solver adapters beyond DOLFINx {#solver-adapters}

Zero-copy or one-copy bridges to the solver libraries whose meshes a user already holds, after 4.3's DOLFINx and in its pattern ([interop](./interop.md)). **L, design pass first**

- **State today.** Open3D and DOLFINx are 4.3; scikit-fem and xarray are 4.10; deal.II is a [non-goal](#format-reach) (it reads and writes formats meshio++ reads). No item names PETSc DMPlex, MFEM's in-memory `Mesh`, Kratos' `ModelPart`, libMesh or preCICE (**verify first**: the Kratos mesh *backend* exists, `--mesh-backend KRATOS`, which is not an adapter to a `ModelPart` of a running Kratos).
- **Decisions to take first.** Per library: the data model (DMPlex's cone/support and `DMPlexCreateFromCellListPetsc`; MFEM's `Mesh` from vertices and elements; a `ModelPart`'s nodes, elements and conditions; preCICE's `setMeshVertices` and `setMeshTriangles`†), whether the bridge is Python (a gated test and an extra, as 4.3) or a provider, and which of them has a pip or conda package a CI job can install.
- **Done when.** Each adapter that lands solves a Poisson problem on a mesh meshio++ read, in a job using that library's package or container; the rest stay listed here. References†: the PETSc DMPlex manual, MFEM's mesh format page, preCICE's API reference.

### 4.17 A nanobind spike {#nanobind-spike}

*Spike.* Nothing in this file or in the docs names nanobind; pybind11 is the binding layer (`bindings/python/_core.cpp`, `np_conversions.hpp`). Measure before deciding: import time of `meshioplusplus._core`, wheel size, and the per-call overhead of a small getter, on a branch that ports one translation unit's worth of definitions (`core_def_*` helpers) to nanobind†; the decision, with the numbers, goes to [spikes](./spikes.md) and either closes this item or opens a migration. Free-threaded builds (3.15.1) are the other reason to look: nanobind's support for them is a documented feature†. **M, spike**

### 4.18 Hosted MCP: registries, a container and a public endpoint {#hosted-mcp}

The [MCP server](./mcp.md) runs only where meshio++ is installed: `meshioplusplus-mcp` over stdio, or `--http` over streamable HTTP at `/mcp` with one bearer token printed at start (`src/python/meshioplusplus/mcp/_http.py`). Nothing in the tree lets an agent reach it without a local install: there is no `Dockerfile` for it, no `glama.json`, `server.json`, `smithery.yaml` or `.mcpb` manifest, and no `mcp-name:` marker in the package description. Listing it needs little (a registry starts the image and reads the tool list); serving it to strangers needs more, because every tool takes file paths a remote caller does not have. The first six items are logistics and small; 4.18.7 is a design pass. **S each, calendar-bound for the listings; 4.18.7 L, design first**

- **State today.** `pyproject.toml` has `mcp = ["mcp>=1.2,<3"]` and `dashboard` (adds Starlette and uvicorn), neither in `[all]`; `_server.py` is the only module importing the SDK and supports both majors. `--root` confines every path ([path sandbox](./mcp.md#path-sandbox)), and the read cache and the root are process-global (`_CACHE`, `_ROOT` in `_tools.py`). `--http` has no `PORT` or `HOST` environment handling, no stateless mode, prints its token, and `--no-token` exists. The training tools (`_jobs.py`) spawn subprocesses. `wheels.yml` publishes to PyPI by OIDC and builds no image; the only `Dockerfile` is the OSS-Fuzz one. Tool annotations (`readOnlyHint`, `destructiveHint`) are not verified in `_server.py`, and directories display them.
- **4.18.1 A container image of the server.** `Dockerfile.mcp`: a slim Python 3.12 base, `pip install "meshioplusplus[mcp,dashboard]"` plus the HDF5 and netCDF Python packages, a non-root user, `WORKDIR /data` with `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_MCP_ROOT=/data`, `ENTRYPOINT ["meshioplusplus-mcp"]` (stdio, which is what registries introspect) and a documented `--http --host 0.0.0.0 --token "$MESHIOPLUSPLUS_MCP_TOKEN"` form with a `HEALTHCHECK` on `/api/health`. Small server changes it needs: `PORT` and `HOST` honoured, the token read from the environment and never printed when it came from there, `--no-token` refused on a non-loopback host. Built and pushed as `ghcr.io/loumalouomega/meshioplusplus-mcp:<version>` by a job in `wheels.yml` after the PyPI publish, with the attestation of 3.14.5; shares its base with 4.1.8's image. Test: a CI step that runs `initialize` and `tools/list` over the container's stdio and asserts the tool count equals `len(TOOL_REGISTRY)`, with no environment variables and an empty `/data`. **Done when** `docker run -i ghcr.io/loumalouomega/meshioplusplus-mcp:<version>` answers a scripted session. **S**
- **4.18.2 Glama.** [glama.ai/mcp/servers](https://glama.ai/mcp/servers) indexes GitHub repositories, builds the repository's `Dockerfile` and introspects the server. Add `glama.json` at the repository root (`$schema` and `maintainers` with the GitHub login) and claim the listing; point Glama's build at `Dockerfile.mcp` (a root `Dockerfile` that is a one-line copy, if it only reads that name). The server must start with no arguments and list every tool without the optional extras: the gated tools already degrade by name ([MCP](./mcp.md#installation)), so check that `tools/list` does not fail on a missing `polyscope` or `torch`. Glama scores the licence, the tool descriptions and a passing inspection: the 200-odd tools already carry long docstrings. Put the badge in `README.md` and [MCP](./mcp.md). **Done when** the Glama page lists the tools and shows a successful build. **S**
- **4.18.3 The official MCP Registry.** `registry.modelcontextprotocol.io` is what Smithery-like aggregators, PulseMCP and clients ingest. A `server.json` at the root: `name` `io.github.loumalouomega/meshioplusplus`, `packages` with a PyPI entry (`meshioplusplus`, run as `meshioplusplus-mcp`, the `mcp` extra) and an OCI entry for 4.18.1's image, later a `remotes` entry for 4.18.7. PyPI ownership is proved by `mcp-name: io.github.loumalouomega/meshioplusplus` in the README that is the package description. Publish with `mcp-publisher` from a release job using GitHub OIDC. Add `server.json` to the version-bump table in `AGENTS.md` (its `version` and the package `version`). **Done when** the registry's API returns the entry for the released version. **S**
- **4.18.4 Directories fed by the above or by a form.** One row each, status "not started":
  - Smithery: a `smithery.yaml` for the container (stdio, and the HTTP form once 4.18.7 exists); the single static token does not fit its per-user configuration.
  - PulseMCP and mcp.so: both ingest from the official registry and GitHub; submit by form if the entry has not appeared a week after 4.18.3.
  - Docker MCP Catalog: a pull request to `docker/mcp-registry` with a `server.yaml`, from 4.18.1's image.
  - Cursor's directory and VS Code's MCP gallery: install deep links and a `.vscode/mcp.json` example in [MCP](./mcp.md), which today has Claude Desktop's configuration only.
  - Claude Code: `claude mcp add meshioplusplus -- uvx --from "meshioplusplus[mcp]" meshioplusplus-mcp` (4.18.6) in the docs.
  - Anthropic's connectors directory: needs a remote endpoint, a privacy policy, an icon and a stable URL, so after 4.18.7.
- **4.18.5 A desktop extension.** A `manifest.json` for an `.mcpb` bundle (stdio, launching through `uv`, with a `user_config` field for the workspace root passed as `--root`), built and attached to each release in `wheels.yml`: Claude Desktop installs it by opening the file, with no JSON editing and no Python on the user's path. **S**
- **4.18.6 A zero-install launcher.** Confirm `uvx --from "meshioplusplus[mcp]" meshioplusplus-mcp --root .` starts on a machine with only `uv` (CI step in the `mcp` job, `ci.yml` lines 1972–2031), and document it in [MCP](./mcp.md) and in `server.json`. An `npx` wrapper is added only if a registry requires an npm package. **S**
- **4.18.7 A hosted, public endpoint.** The design pass writes `doc/mcp_hosting_plan.md` and decides:
  - *Bytes in and out.* The tools take paths. Hosted mode adds an `upload` tool (base64 for small files, a presigned URL for large ones), a `fetch_url` tool (allow-listed hosts, a size cap, no redirects to private addresses) and a `download` tool, with each output also offered as an MCP resource, all inside a per-session root with a time-to-live and a byte quota. The existing sandbox resolves every path, so no tool changes; `_ROOT` and `_CACHE` become per-session, which is a small refactor of `_tools.py`.
  - *Limits.* Request size, wall-clock and memory per call, a concurrency cap per client, and a tool subset that excludes the training and job tools, webhooks, the viewers (`view`, `screenshot`), the dataset scans and anything that loads a plugin (`dynlib_open`), recorded as an allow-list the way 4.9.2 plans one for the native server.
  - *Authentication and abuse.* OAuth 2.1 per the MCP authorization specification where a directory demands it, a bearer token otherwise; rate limiting in front (the platform or a proxy); an optional anonymous tier with the read-only tools only.
  - *Where it runs.* A container host that runs the compiled wheel: Fly.io, Cloud Run, Render, Railway, or a Hugging Face Space for a demo (Docker SDK, port 7860). A Cloudflare Worker cannot run the native module; only 4.9's server compiled to WebAssembly could, and that is recorded as the later option. Smithery and Glama can also host the container themselves.
  - *Privacy and licence.* Uploaded meshes are user data, deleted at the time-to-live, with a notice in [MCP](./mcp.md); the OpenCASCADE, Netgen and MMG add-ons stay out of the hosted image unless their licences ([`LICENSE-BINARIES.md`](https://github.com/loumalouomega/meshioplusplus/blob/main/LICENSE-BINARIES.md)) allow offering them as a service.
  - *Cost.* Who pays for the compute, and the cap at which the endpoint stops answering.

  Steps after the decisions: the three tools in `mcp/_tools.py` and `_server.py` (the checklist of `AGENTS.md`: `tests/python/test_mcp.py`, [MCP](./mcp.md), a `_NOT_TOOLS` exemption only if a tool cannot be a path-based one), a `--hosted` mode in `_http.py` that turns the limits on and refuses `--no-token`, a deploy workflow, and the `remotes` entry of 4.18.3. **Probe.** An agent with no local install fetches a mesh by URL, calls `quality`, converts it and downloads the result. **L**
- **4.18.8 The server's tool count and annotations.** [MCP](./mcp.md) and `README.md` say 87 tools while the registry holds about 200 (`TOOL_REGISTRY` in `_tools.py`); every listing shows the number. Generate it from the registry in `tools/` (a line the docs build checks, as 3.16 plans for other counts), and verify that `_server.py` sets `readOnlyHint` on the inspection tools and `destructiveHint` on the writers (`output_path` tools overwrite), since directories display them. **S**

### 4.19 A C-ABI plugin contract for providers {#c-abi-plugins}

Today a runtime plugin must match the host's C++ compiler, standard library, CRT (`/MT` vs `/MD`) and exact meshio++ release because `providers.hpp` passes `std::string`, `std::vector`, `std::function` and C++ exceptions across the shared-library boundary — the exact wall 4.7.2 (Windows CLI plugins) and 4.8.1.2 (Linux `-static-libstdc++` CLI plugins) hit. A pure C99 plugin boundary removes that coupling and lets third-party plugins be written in C, Rust or Fortran as well as C++. **M–L, design pass first**

- **State today.** `src/cpp/include/meshioplusplus/providers.hpp` (lines 65–167) defines `CadKernelProvider`, `TetMesherProvider`, `QuadRemesherProvider` and `RemesherProvider` with C++ STL members; `src/cpp/src/providers.cpp` loads shared libraries via `dynlib_open` and invokes `meshioplusplus_register_plugin` (`src/cpp/providers/plugin_export.hpp`), expecting exceptions to cross the boundary (`AGENTS.md` notes `--exclude-libs` must stay off).
- **Decisions to take first.** (1) Scope: start with a versioned `mio_plugin_api_v1` C99 header (`bindings/c/include/meshioplusplus/plugin_api.h`) covering the flat-data provider kinds first (`TetMesherProvider`, `QuadRemesherProvider`, 9.1's `BooleanProvider`, 9.7's `GraphPartitionerProvider`, 4.8.2's `ViewerProvider`) using caller-owned buffers/callbacks, `mio_mesh*` handles and integer status codes + UTF-8 error buffer — never throwing across the boundary. (2) Keep the existing C++ provider registration for monolithic builds and Python add-on wheels; wrap C-ABI plugins into the same internal `providers.hpp` structs on load. (3) Decide whether `RemesherProvider` (MMG) and `CadKernelProvider` (OpenCASCADE) adopt the C-ABI entry point so one static Windows `/MT` or Linux `-static-libstdc++` CLI binary can load every plugin archive (4.8.1) without a second dynamically linked CLI flavour (4.7.2).
- **Steps.**
  - **4.19.1 Header and host loader.** Define `mio_plugin_api_v1` (struct sizes + `abi_version` handshake), extend `providers.cpp` to probe `mio_plugin_init_v1` before falling back to the C++ symbol, and wrap errors into `std::runtime_error` on the host side. **S–M**
  - **4.19.2 Reference C plugin and cross-runtime test.** Port `tests/cpp/plugin/fake_plugin.cpp` to a pure-C99 `fake_c_plugin.c` compiled with a different runtime/compiler flag (including `/MT` host + `/MD` plugin on Windows and `-static-libstdc++` host on Linux) to prove zero C++ ABI coupling. **S**
  - **4.19.3 Migrate CLI plugin archives (4.8.1, 4.8.2, 9.7.2)** onto the C-ABI boundary so release plugins survive patch releases and static-runtime CLIs on all three platforms. **M**
- **Done when.** A pure-C99 plugin compiled independently loads into the statically linked Linux and Windows CLI executables and returns structured errors without crossing C++ exceptions.

### 4.20 Zero-copy Apache Arrow C Data Interface in the core {#arrow-c-data}

Data-science, columnar analytics and physics-ML engines (DuckDB, Polars, PyArrow, Arrow-rs, Arrow.jl, nanoarrow in R) all speak the dependency-free Arrow C Data Interface (`ArrowSchema` and `ArrowArray`, two small C structs†). Exposing it from the C++ core and C API lets any language binding hand mesh points, connectivity and point/cell arrays straight into columnar engines with zero copies and zero external dependencies. **S–M**

- **State today.** `to_arrow` and `from_arrow` exist in Python only (`src/python/meshioplusplus/_interop.py` lines 1302–1450) and require `pyarrow`. Nothing in `src/cpp/` or `bindings/c/` exports `ArrowArray` / `ArrowSchema`.
- **Reuse.** `NDArray`'s shared buffer ownership (`include/meshioplusplus/ndarray.hpp`), `detail::DTypeUtils` (`src/cpp/src/detail/dtype_table.hpp`), and the Python `_to_table_payload` schema convention (`_interop.py` line 882).
- **Steps.**
  - **4.20.1 `NDArray` ↔ Arrow C Data Interface.** A small header `include/meshioplusplus/arrow_c_data.hpp` (using the standard ABI-stable `ArrowSchema`/`ArrowArray` structs guarded by `ARROW_C_DATA_INTERFACE`†) with `export_ndarray_to_arrow(const NDArray&, ArrowSchema*, ArrowArray*)` (holding a shared ownership ref in `private_data` released by the `release` callback, zero copy for contiguous 1-D/fixed-size-list arrays) and `import_ndarray_from_arrow(ArrowSchema*, ArrowArray*)` (owning the imported buffer via `NDArray`'s custom deleter). **S**
  - **4.20.2 C API and flat bindings.** `mio_ndarray_export_arrow` / `mio_mesh_export_point_data_arrow` in `bindings/c/include/meshioplusplus/meshioplusplus.h` and `c_api.cpp`; wire Python's `to_arrow` / `from_arrow` to use `__arrow_c_array__` when available, and add zero-copy smoke tests in R (`nanoarrow`†), Julia (`Arrow.jl`†) and Python (`pyarrow` / `duckdb`†). **S–M**
- **Done when.** A C++ or C caller exports a 10⁶-point coordinate and field array via `ArrowArray` into PyArrow or DuckDB with zero buffer copies verified by pointer equality, under ASan with zero leaks on release.

---

## 5. Performance {#performance}

*Admission: a slowdown in a path a user hits, measured or verified in the code, with the shape of the fix named. Every item lands through [landing a performance change](./benchmarks.md#landing-a-performance-change): output byte-identical across backends, thread counts and the baseline digests, SEQ not slower, the ABI tier stated. The section closed in October 2026 with its decisions recorded in the [non-goals](#non-goals-and-decisions-taken); the November 2026 audit reopened it with what those decisions do not cover, and the review later that month reopened two decisions with a narrower shape (an in-place `transform` and vector kernels behind run-time dispatch), both measured and closed again: see the non-goals. The impact estimates come from reading the code and from the tier rows of [benchmarks](./benchmarks.md) and the [XL spike](./spikes.md), not from new measurements: each item's first step is the measurement its probe names. A second audit that month (5.1.5 and 5.17 to 5.22) read the instruction baseline, `benchmark/instructions_S.csv`, against the code of its heaviest rows and against the operations the harness has no row for; nothing was profiled for it, so its items say what to measure before they say what to change. Its rows for the operations the harness lacked (5.1.5) landed the same month, 46 of them, with the operations that have none and why listed in [benchmarks](./benchmarks.md#rows-for-every-operation); no row's digest differed between SEQ, OpenMP and THREADS at 1, 4 and 8 threads (tiers S and M; TBB was not installed). Items 5.23 to 5.28 (`smooth_volume` tet fast path, sparse solver workspace/IC(0)/Nested-Dissection LDLᵀ upgrades, `check_conformity` stack clipping and O(F) boundary-facet extraction, `untangle` analytic gradients and coloured parallel sweeps, `estimate_error` zero-clone recovery, and `--allocs` / `benchmark/allocs_S.csv` CI tracking) closed in November 2026: see [benchmarks](./benchmarks.md#performance-tier-pass-roadmap-523-to-528).*

**The tools every item uses.** Rows are defined in `src/cpp/benchmark/bench_ops.cpp` through `row(name, lambda)` (line 521), `read_row(op, format, ext, mesh)` (774), `file_row` (801) and `write_row` (921); tiers are `S` 12, `M` 30, `L` 50, `XL` 120 cells a side (line 378). `tools/bench_ops.sh [OUT.csv] [BACKENDS] [THREADS] -- --hash --tier S` sweeps backends and threads and compares digests against `BASELINE=<csv>`. `python3 tools/bench_instructions.py --check benchmark/instructions_S.csv` is the pull-request gate (1% above the baseline fails; `--record` re-records it, on CI's `ubuntu-24.04` toolchain, saying why), and `python3 tools/bench_allocs.py --check benchmark/allocs_S.csv --advisory` tracks heap allocation counts and peak live bytes beside it. `benchmark/bench.py --sizes S,M,L --formats a,b` times formats from Python.

### 5.2 The wheels' parallel runtimes

The Linux wheels build the THREADS backend since v19.0.0 (`wheels.yml` lines 143–147; [installation](./installation.md)), a `std::thread` pool in the core that needs no runtime library. **S**

- **5.2.1 The measurement.** One manylinux wheel from a `wheels.yml` dispatch against the last SEQ wheel (v18): `python benchmark/bench.py --sizes L --formats vtu,xdmf` and `tools/bench_ops.sh` at 1, 4 and 8 threads on the same machine; numbers into the table of [benchmarks](./benchmarks.md#a-threads-backend-for-the-linux-wheels), whose last sentence names this probe.
- **5.2.2 macOS.** The base wheel installs Homebrew's libomp (`CIBW_BEFORE_ALL_MACOS`, line 112), sets no backend (so `AUTO` picks OpenMP) and no repair command, leaving cibuildwheel's default `delocate`. Add `delocate-listdeps --all` on the built wheel to the job's test step and assert `libomp.dylib` is bundled; if it is not, switch macOS to THREADS too. Then replace "to be confirmed in a wheel listing" in [installation](./installation.md) (line 53) with the result.

### 5.8 HDF5

- **5.8.2 The library lock around library calls only.**
  - **State today.** `src/cpp/src/detail/library_lock.hpp`: `LibraryLock` (RAII over a recursive mutex) and `LibraryUnlock`, which gives the lock up for a scope and retakes it; used by the parallel deflate (`detail/hdf5_util.cpp` lines 231 and 321) and the VTKHDF reader's assembly (`formats/vtkhdf.cpp` lines 1100, 1120, 1654). The writers hold the lock for their whole body: `write_vtkhdf` (line 1577), the series writer's methods (2048–2169), `write_xdmf` (`formats/xdmf.cpp` lines 829–831) and `xdmf_time_series.cpp` (eight sites). Giving it up around `vtkhdf_collect` (line 1231) was tried and measured no change on four concurrent writes of a 125,000-hexahedron box (0.63 s either way), the deflate being the bulk ([benchmarks](./benchmarks.md#compression-outside-the-library-lock)).
  - **Steps.** Reopen only on a profile that shows preparation dominating: an uncompressed write of a mesh with many small arrays, or XDMF's light-data assembly. Then split each writer into *prepare* (no library call, no lock) and *write* (locked), which 3.10.3's annotations can enforce. The invariant, no two threads inside the libraries, is unchanged.
  - **Done when.** Either a profile is recorded showing nothing to gain, and the item closes into the non-goals, or four concurrent uncompressed writes scale. **S–M**

### 5.13 Browser memory: a 64-bit build

Several decisions rest on the browser's heap: the viewer's applies are not cached because a second copy of the mesh shares it with MEMFS. The 32-bit heap now reaches 4 GB (`-sMAXIMUM_MEMORY=4GB`, [WebAssembly](./wasm.md#the-heap-ceiling): about 13 to 16 million tetrahedra became 25 to 35 million through a write and a read-back); what is left is the step past 4 GB. **M, verify first**

- **State today.** Lengths cross embind as `std::size_t` (`arr["length"].as<std::size_t>()`, `js_bindings.cpp`) and `-sWASM_BIGINT` is set. The loader picks sequential or threaded by `crossOriginIsolated` (`src/wasm/src/index.mjs`) and the full artifact only on request. `tests/wasm/memory.mjs` fails with `std::bad_alloc` on a 2 GB heap and passes on the 4 GB one; `tests/wasm/bench.mjs` times the JS boundary.
- **Steps.**
  - **5.13.2 Memory64.** Verified in part (November 2026, emsdk 6.0.3: `-sMEMORY64=1` on the core, with no HDF5, netCDF or zlib). It builds and links, with Emscripten warning that `-sMEMORY64` is deprecated in favour of `-m64` or `--target=wasm64` (use those). Node 22's V8 (12.4) will not load it (`invalid table elements limits flags`: the 64-bit table), Node 24 (V8 13.6) instantiates it. The first call into JS then fails, `new Float64Array(n)` with `n` a BigInt (`TypeError: Cannot convert a BigInt value to a number`): embind passes a 64-bit `size_t` as a BigInt, so every `size_t` that crosses must be converted at the boundary, 9 typed-array constructors and 18 length reads in `js_bindings.cpp` at least, and every exported signature that takes or returns one, with `index.d.ts` unchanged. Not yet checked: the slowdown of 64-bit pointers (nothing runs before the boundary is fixed), the dependencies of `build/build-wasm-deps.sh` rebuilt for `wasm64`, and Safari's support. If it holds: a fifth artifact (`meshioplusplus_wasm_64.{mjs,wasm}`) built by `build/configure-wasm.sh --memory64`, chosen by the loader only on request (`{ memory64: true }`), as full is.
  - **5.13.3 Measure the caching decision again** on the 4 GB heap: the [non-goal](#non-goals-and-decisions-taken) on caching between the viewer's applies cites the shared 32-bit heap, which is now twice the size; the viewer's own largest mesh before and after is the number to record (the Node measurement above is the core's, not the viewer's).

### 5.16 Streaming writers for one large mesh {#streaming-writers}

The [sequence driver](./sequences.md) already keeps one mesh alive at a time (two with its opt-in [`Prefetch`](./sequences.md#prefetch)); a *single* mesh of 10⁸ cells is held whole before any writer sees it. **L, design pass first**

- **State today.** `write` takes a whole `Mesh`; the VTU `appended` mode (since v16.21.0) streams arrays into one section but from memory ([VTU](./formats/vtu.md)). *Verify first* which writers could emit a block at a time without a second pass (VTU appended, VTKHDF, XDMF with heavy data in HDF5, Exodus).
- **Steps.** A `BlockWriter` interface (`Begin(header)`, `AddCellBlock`, `AddPointChunk`, `End`) beside `SeqSink`'s writers (`operations/sequence.cpp`), implemented first for VTKHDF and VTU-appended; the in-memory `write` becomes its one-chunk special case, with output byte-identical to today's (`tools/bench_ops.sh … --hash`).
- **Done when.** A 10⁸-cell write peaks at no more than twice one chunk plus the mesh the caller holds, and is byte-identical to the in-memory writer's. An [ABI](./abi.md) addition: a review row.

## 6. Code duplication {#code-duplication}

*Admission: copies the duplication measure finds, or an audit by role finds where the measure cannot (the same job under different names and token shapes), listed by place so each can be taken alone, without a feature waiting on it. An item names the files and line ranges, what is repeated, and the shape a shared version would take; it closes when the copies call one implementation or when the copy is shown to be a table that only looks repeated. Items whose shared home is one of [§3.4](#quality-of-implementation)'s kernels say so and are counted there, not here. Line numbers are those of `master` at `db32c73` (November 2026) and drift as the files change: rerun the report before starting an item.*

Measured with `tools/dup_report.py --lang all --json` ([static analysis](./static_analysis.md#local-duplication-report)), which works like SonarCloud's detector: C++ 22,294 of 244,439 code lines duplicated in 730 files (9.1%), Python 12,468 of 136,064 in 469 files (9.2%). SonarCloud's first analysis ([§7](#sonarcloud-findings)) counts less, 4,974 duplicated lines in 314 blocks across 110 files (0.9%), because its C++ detector needs longer runs; its ranking of files agrees with the one below at the top (`cli/main.cpp`, the Nastran pair, `mcp/_server.py`, `c_api.cpp`, `_core.cpp`), and it found the copies of 8.10 the local report did not scan until it took the R binding's C sources. The report lists regions shared by two files; the copies within one file, which dominate the four worst files, were found with the same tokenizer and window over each file alone. Probe for every item: the file's duplicated lines drop in the report, `tools/bench_ops.sh --hash` against a `BASELINE` reports identical digests, and the [instruction-count gate](./benchmarks.md#instruction-count-gate) passes. A change to an installed header follows the [ABI policy](./abi.md).

After the shared-utilities pass (November 2026: the report's [table filter](./static_analysis.md#local-duplication-report), the private `MathUtils`, `GeometryUtils`, `BoundingBoxUtils`, `FacetSlotUtils`, `DTypeUtils` and `NDArrayUtils` classes of [shared geometry and FEM kernels](./geometry_kernels.md), and the Python helpers it closed items into) the report, which now skips literal tables and reads the R binding's C sources, counts C++ 21,699 of 251,295 code lines duplicated (8.6%; 19,725 of 243,917 on the earlier roots, 8.1%) and Python 8,962 of 135,752 (6.6%). The numbering below is that pass's: what was 8.2 to 8.11 is now 8.1 to 8.10.

After the binding pass (November 2026: the helpers listed in [shared geometry and FEM kernels](./geometry_kernels.md#what-the-binding-pass-shared), from `_forward` in the MCP server to `mio_r_report_list` in the R binding) the report counts C++ 20,554 of 250,625 code lines duplicated (8.2%) and Python 7,214 of 133,787 (5.4%). It closed 8.1.2, 8.2.3, 8.3.1, 8.6.1, 8.8.1 and 8.8.2, the server side of 8.4.1 and the C++ side of 8.4.2; it closed 8.1.3 and 8.1.4 and narrowed 8.3.2 by the decisions below, and narrowed the rest to what it found. Two of its closures raise their file's count, because the detector normalises names and literals and so matches the rows of a table against each other: `registry.cpp`'s one row per format (263 duplicated lines before, 424 after) and `cli/main.cpp`'s verb table (2,270 before, 2,348 after). Each lists every format or verb once where there were four and two lists; they are tables that only look repeated, the closing condition above. The decisions it took are not open work: `_core.cpp`'s feature flags are one `#if` each with nothing a table would remove; the Python render keywords and the pipeline's `Snapshot` options are not one list spelt twice (15 keys exist only in Python, and the colours and encodings differ: tuples against hex strings); and a verb's text and `--json` reports are not one-to-one (column widths, conditional lines, keys only in JSON and their order against `write_mesh_cli`), so a report helper is per verb, as `cli_sweep_report` is, not one generic field list.

After the third pass (November 2026, the second half of the binding pass: the C API's result moves, lattice extents and enum checks; the native CLI's option, warning and lattice helpers and one decimate verb body; the MCP tools' `_run_op`; one factory for 20 Python format shims; the Nastran writers' model building; forced inlining for two of 3.4.11's shares; one spec for the pipeline step tables; the R and Octave bindings' report and getter helpers) the report counts C++ 19,411 of 249,708 code lines duplicated (7.8%) and Python 6,500 of 132,440 (4.9%). Every item of the binding pass's list closed or became a decision except the three below. The decisions, which are not open work: `_core.cpp`'s remaining option fills each fill one struct once, with string-to-enum conversions that carry their own messages, so a generic `(keyword, member, default)` table would need a converter per entry to keep them; the C API's remaining inline conversions (`mio_select`, `mio_mesh_cad_surface2`, `mio_fit_cad3`, `mio_boundary_layer`) convert their struct once; of the native CLI's verbs only `check-conformity` prints the same fields as text and as JSON, so the per-verb report helper has nothing to share, and the four MMG verbs share their options, parameters and summary already, the rest being each verb's own fill; the MCP tools that transform an argument before the call (a path resolved, a list built) keep their bodies, since `_run_op` passes only call-free overrides; the primitives' checks are one shared call each, which a combined prologue would only re-wrap; the pipeline's per-step option parsing stays hand-written in each engine, with `tests/python/test_pipeline_spec.py` holding both to the spec's kinds and defaults; the time-series, `svg`/`tikz` and near-miss shims differ in their signatures or core arguments; the Nastran card tables and unique-id rules (per card in HDF5, global in OP2) and the readers' per-block policies (insertion against sorted order, last- against first-value-wins) differ by design; the R builders left (`data_info`, `data_integrate`, `diff`) build nested lists in loops. The viewer page's repeats are vtk.js's own WebGPU shader templates inside the single-file bundle, which `sonar.cpd.exclusions` leaves out of the duplication measure. The numbering below is this pass's: 8.7.1 became 8.1, what was left of 8.8.1 8.2, and 8.9 8.3.

After the installed-header pass (November 2026, ABI 42: the last three items, with the OpenCASCADE build no earlier pass had) the report counts C++ 18,744 of 249,629 code lines duplicated (7.5%; 19,411 of 249,784, 7.8%, before it) and Python 6,500 of 132,442 (4.9%), and this section has no open item. It closed 8.1 (the provider's pole and knot builds, node welding, and its copies of the native `fit_cad`'s triangulation, adaptive options, re-segmentation rung, refinement verdicts and STEP chain walks, now `fit_cad_native.hpp`'s and `step_brep.hpp`'s; the `fit_cad` goldens unchanged), 8.2 (one gather body in `detail/subset_gather.hpp`, forced inline into `build_cell_subset` and `clean`: `split`, `crop`, `partition`, `clean` and `clean_weld` within the gate; a plain inline function, or an out-of-line entry beside the body in `subset.cpp`, cost `build_cell_subset` its inlining of `read_int`, `split` +10%) and 8.3 at ABI 42 (the grid walks, the link sentinels, the series writers' mode, raw array and special members, the drawing writers' options, the B-rep face counts and the distance names; see [shared geometry and FEM kernels](./geometry_kernels.md#what-the-binding-pass-shared) and [common utilities](./common_utilities.md)). The decisions it took, which are not open work: the XDMF and VTKHDF writers keep their own declarations of `WritePointsCells`, `WriteData`, `Flush` and the rest, whose contracts and documentation differ (buffered light data against per-step HDF5 writes), and the MESHIO and NATIVE backends keep their own `CellView` and region and property-set accessors: every shared form measured (a `CellView` template over member pointers or a traits class, a common base, a CRTP base for the region accessors) moved GCC's inlining in the operations that read cells past the [instruction-count gate](./benchmarks.md#instruction-count-gate) (`linearize_ragged` +3.7%, `gradient_surface` +1.0% to +1.04%), and their point and data accessors sit over different storage (`unordered_map` against `NamedArrays`); `mPadding`/`mPaddingRelative` are two fields of five option structs with different defaults (0 or 0.1), not one struct spelt five times; the `*_from_name` parsers of `octree_mesh` and `sdf` each name their own aliases and messages; the format headers' `read_<fmt>_metadata` and `<fmt>_time_values` are one documented declaration per format, a table that only looks repeated. What the report still finds across the files the pass touched is outside this section: the hand-written cross products of `step_brep.cpp` and `fit_cad.cpp` (`fcn_facet_area_fraction`, the patch areas) are [3.4.1](#quality-of-implementation)'s. A copy the report finds from here on is listed in this section as a new item, numbered from 6.1.

**The audit by role (November 2026).** The report and SonarCloud both match runs of tokens, so neither sees two helpers that do one job when their names, parameter types or loop shapes differ: forty trimmers of four lines each are forty different token runs. This audit grepped for the job instead (a definition named `*_trim`, a `parent` vector with `std::iota`, the golden-ratio constant of a hash, `std::nth_element`), in `src/cpp/src`, `src/cpp/include`, `src/cpp/cli` and `src/python/meshioplusplus`. Every item below moves its copies to a shared utility as the earlier passes did ([adding or extending a utility](./common_utilities.md#adding-or-extending-a-utility)): core-private under `src/cpp/src/detail/`, a Kratos-style class of static members for a family, a `tests/cpp/test_*_utils.cpp`, and its row in [common utilities](./common_utilities.md) in the same change. The probe is this section's, with one difference: the report's count does not fall for copies it never matched, so each item's own count, given by the grep it names, is what drops. The copies of an item often differ in an edge case (a whitespace set, a tie rule, a median of an even count): the item says which, and a share keeps each caller's behaviour, by a parameter or a named variant, unless the difference is shown to be an accident and is fixed with a test. Line numbers are those of `master` in November 2026.

**The audit by role closed (November 2026), and this section has no open item.** `detail/union_find.hpp`, `detail/edge_use_table.hpp`, `detail/text_utils.hpp`, `detail/splitmix.hpp`, `detail/hash_utils.hpp`, `detail/fortran_number.hpp`, `MathUtils`' order statistics and the Python `_common.unit_vector` and `fortran_exponent` replaced the copies its eight items listed, and `external_mesher`'s shells (the union gave the root to the later face, but shells are numbered by their lowest triangle, so only the partition is read) and `param_boundary_loops` (a directed count, kept in directed-key order by sorting the boundary edges) moved last. The decisions it took, which are not open work: `fit_cad`'s crease graph over sparse `uint64` vertex ids keeps its own find, since a dense `UnionFind` would need an id-to-index map first; `param_disc`'s weight map and `render`'s point map are not edge counts; the trims that strip NUL (`fil_trim`, `slf_trim`, `ensight_trim`), take their set as an argument (`lsd_strip`), read a `const char*` (`vtu_strip`, `vtu_strip_view`) or are asymmetric (`frd_strip`) keep their bodies; `ensight_starts_with` and `ensight_has_suffix` keep their `rfind` form, since `std::string_view::starts_with` cost `read_ragged_ensight` 3.0% more instructions; `lm_ends_with` refuses a name equal to its suffix; `gid_line_starts_with` is a hot per-row test of two prefixes and `gid_split` keeps quoted strings; the installed `trim` of `detail/drawing.hpp` and the CLI's `refine_trim` wait on an ABI bump and on the CLI reaching a core-private header; `bucket_table.hpp`'s home-slot mix and `tet_remesh`'s triangle hash use other shifts than splitmix, and `TsKeyHash` is FNV-shaped. A copy the report finds from here on is listed in this section as a new item, numbered from 6.1.

**Fresh copies surfaced by `tools/dup_report.py` and live SonarCloud (November 2026, after PR #252):** the follow-up pass closed 6.1 (`MathUtils::Invert3x3` / `PackTensor3x3`), 6.3 (`series_shims` / `series_read_shim` / `write_shim` / `series_writer_class` / `dispatch_write` in `_shims.py`, also closing 3.4.12's `is_buffer` drift), 6.4 (`GeometryUtils::TetDihedralAnglesDeg`, `GeometryUtils::TriangleVertexRings`, `detail/append_series.hpp`; `exodus`/`permas` are literal lookup tables, `decimate`/`decimate_volume` already call shared helpers) and the `svg`/`tikz` `__init__.py` shims of 6.2, dropping `tools/dup_report.py` from 21,717 to 21,595 duplicated C++ lines (7.5%) and from 6,811 to 6,370 Python lines (4.3%). What remains:

- **6.2 Drawing writer internals (`svg` and `tikz`, C++ and Python).** The `svg/__init__.py` and `tikz/__init__.py` shims are now shared through `dispatch_write` and `drawing_read_shim` in `_shims.py`. Remaining: `svg/_svg.py:123-157` vs `tikz/_tikz.py:103-137` (163 tokens, shared projection/colormap setup into `_drawing_common.py`) and `svg.cpp` vs `tikz.cpp` option unpacking before `detail::drawing_write_projected`. **S**
