## 7. SonarCloud findings {#sonarcloud-findings}

*Admission: what SonarCloud's rules report beyond duplication, grouped by rule so a rule can be closed in one pass. A finding is either fixed, or shown to be a false positive and the rule narrowed for that pattern ([static analysis](./static_analysis.md#false-positives)), so the next scan does not report it again; a finding confirmed as a defect that loses data or crashes opens a *Correctness debts* section at the top, as [how this file works](#how-this-file-works) says. The live list is on [sonarcloud.io](https://sonarcloud.io/project/issues?id=loumalouomega_meshioplusplus).*

The first analysis ran on the default *Sonar way* profile, with no coverage: 416,477 lines; 110 code smells (maintainability A); 226 bugs (reliability E, 121 of them blocker); 46 vulnerabilities (security E). The SonarCloud pass (November 2026, ABI 41) worked every item of the list it made, from the 376 issues the scan of `60825af` held, and closed them in code where the behaviour stays the same:

- *Misread patterns.* NaN tests are `std::isnan`/`math.isnan`; `NDArray`'s default heap is out of line, where the analyzer had followed its shape loop taking no dimension and reported every write past an array's first element (68 blockers); guards and locals the analyzer can follow replace its divisions and shifts by zero; the preprocessor branches that were alike are one `constexpr` test each. The Python floating-point equalities (88) were each read and are exact sentinels the reference must match the core on, recorded once for the rule.
- *Smells.* The MESHIO data names are sorted on each call rather than memoized from a `const` accessor, so a `const Mesh` may be read from several threads ([mesh data model](./mesh_data_model.md#threads)); atomics take the default order but one idempotent mark; the recursive and the deliberately leaked mutexes say why; parsing with side effects in `&&` is split; string lengths are bounded or `std::string_view`; CLI handlers that only report return nothing; NumPy and Python idioms; the nine backtracking regular expressions are rewritten without overlapping quantifiers; the viewer's controls are labelled and its splitter follows the WAI-ARIA separator pattern.
- *Bugs to verify.* None was a defect that loses data. The triangle intersection's slot count, the curve evaluator's dimension, the tensor and stiffness preconditions, the null connectivity and edge-table paths are explicit; destructors guard their logging; the C API's octree entry points keep their defaults' address in a local.
- *Vulnerabilities.* Real, and fixed: the TUI music player wrote its loop to a predictable name under the temporary directory (now a `mkdtemp` directory), `make_temp_dir` and the decompressed copies were not private (now `mkdtemp`, mode 0700), the subprocess log was world-readable (0600), the MCP server's `resolve_checkpoint` followed the best checkpoint a job file recorded without confining it, and log lines carried a file's control characters unescaped (`log::escape`). By design, and recorded once per rule: the library's top-level modules and the trainer open what their caller names; `dynlib_open` loads the configured plugin; seeded sampling, a SHA-1 content key, the companion's plain-HTTP loopback URL.

The live `master` scan (25 November 2026, after PR #252; 467,191 lines; previously 42 issues at `f39f9a8`) reported 45 open issues (11 bugs, 2 vulnerabilities, 32 code smells) and red `new_reliability_rating` / `new_security_rating` (E). All 45 issues and the `sonar-project.properties` exclusions (binding tests excluded from `sonar.sources`, `bindings/r/meshioplusplus/R/**` from coverage, Python `3.14` added, `cpp:S5018` in `src/cpp/providers/occt/**` recorded) have been addressed in code: `iga.cpp` zero-span validation (formerly 1.2), `adapt_volume.cpp`/`tensor_invariants.cpp` array initialization, `lsdyna.cpp` optional check, `cgns.cpp` null `tmpl` guard, `openfoam.cpp` `~FoamOut` catch-all, `_data_statistics.py` `math.isnan`, `fit_cad_kpi.cpp` private `mkdtemp` and validated/escaped `backend`/`tag`, the 21 `_cli/` handlers returning `None`, `gmsh.cpp`/`ugrid_nc.cpp` `string_view`, and `sequence.cpp` `std::thread` + `std::promise`.

What the pass could not settle here, because only a scan shows it. Configuration: `sonar-project.properties` (sources, exclusions, thirteen `sonar.issue.ignore.multicriteria` entries each with its reason) and `.github/workflows/sonarcloud.yml`.

- **7.1 Confirm the 45 fixes on the next `master` SonarCloud scan.** Query `https://sonarcloud.io/api/issues/search?componentKeys=loumalouomega_meshioplusplus&resolved=false&ps=500` and `api/qualitygates/project_status?projectKey=loumalouomega_meshioplusplus` after the next `master` scan and verify that `new_reliability_rating` and `new_security_rating` read A and 0 unrecorded issues remain (unblocking 7.4). **S**
- **7.2 Keep the coverage condition measured.** `sonarcloud.yml` now fails (rather than warning) when no `coverage-reports` artifact is found on a `master` push; confirm on the next `master` run. **S**
- **7.3 The viewer's built page is stale.** `src/viewer/src/features/shell.ts` line 39 sets the splitter's `aria-valuenow` when it moves, but the committed `src/python/meshioplusplus/_viewer_assets/viewer.html` holds the attribute once, the static one: its minified `setWidth` predates the change. Rebuilding needs the WebAssembly package (`npm run build:embed` in `src/viewer`, then copy `dist-embed/index.html` over the asset, `src/viewer/README.md` lines 44–108). CI's only check is a size drift of at most 10% (`ci.yml` lines 2171–2190), which cannot see this: add to the `viewer-web` job, which builds the package anyway, a step that runs `build:embed` and fails when the result differs from the committed asset (or commits it from a scheduled job), so the embedded page cannot fall behind again. 4.6 and 4.12 need the rebuild too. **S**
- **7.4 Make the gates required.** *Verify first* in the repository settings (not readable from the tree): the quality gate of `sonarcloud.yml` and the `tools/dup_report.py` ratchet are advisory until named as required status checks on `master`; 3.5, 3.9 and 3.10 already say "required" for their own gates. List the gates that are required today in [static analysis](./static_analysis.md) and make the Sonar gate and the duplication ratchet required once 7.1 confirms the two failing ratings are green (the other conditions already pass). **S**

## 8. Data model {#data-model}

*Admission: something files hold and users ask for that `Mesh` has no place for, so that every format carrying it drops it, splits it or hides it in a naming convention. Each item may change `NDArray` or `Mesh`, and then it reaches every mesh backend (MESHIO, NATIVE, KRATOS), every binding and the [ABI](./abi.md): each starts with a design pass in the manner of the [long-run spikes](./spikes.md), which decided the last two questions of this kind (NURBS as a side channel; the isogeometric layer beside `Mesh`), and the outcome may again be "a convention, documented and tested" rather than a new type. Each item lists the questions its pass must answer and the option recommended from what the code shows today. The order is by how many shipped formats lose data for want of each.*

**What a new dtype touches**, for 8.1 and 8.2: `enum class DType` and `dtype_size`/`dtype_numpy_str` (`src/cpp/include/meshioplusplus/ndarray.hpp` lines 225–270); `detail::DTypeUtils` (`src/cpp/src/detail/dtype_table.hpp`, `kNumDTypes = 10`) and `dispatch_dtype` (`detail/value_io.hpp` line 180); `dtype_from_numpy` (`bindings/python/np_conversions.hpp` line 103, which today throws on kinds `b` and `c`, sending the call to the Python twin); `mio_dtype` (`meshioplusplus.h` line 156, values 0 to 9); Fortran's four constants (`meshioplusplus.f90` near line 2281); Julia's `_DTYPES` (`libmio.jl` line 93); R's `mio_r_fill_real`, which widens everything to double; Octave's `dtype_of_class` (`util.c` lines 421–433); WASM's `js_typed_array_ctor` and `dtype_of_js_array` (`js_bindings.cpp` lines 303 and 559); every writer's dtype switch. A new enumerator appended is ABI Tier C for C++ and additive for C, but every `switch` over `DType` in the 86 format sources must handle it, which is the real cost.

### 8.2 Strings and booleans

- **State today.** `field_data` is numeric arrays only in the core (`unordered_map<string, NDArray>` in MESHIO, `NamedArrays` in NATIVE, `native_mesh.hpp` line 450); Python's `Mesh.field_data` accepts any dict (`_mesh.py` line 200), and the conversion throws on a non-numeric entry unless `lenient_field_data` skips it (`np_conversions.hpp` lines 487–575), so a string in `field_data` silently selects the Python twin or is dropped. Strings a format holds live in side structs (`ExodusInfo::mInfoRecords`, `MedInfo::mMedNom`). Booleans widen to `uint8` (Octave maps logical to `MIO_UINT8`) and come back as integers.
- **Decisions to take first.** (1) Strings in `field_data`: recommended, a documented encoding rather than a dtype: a `uint8` array of UTF-8 bytes with a companion `<name>:offsets` `int64` array for a list of strings (the ragged layout `NDArray` already carries for polyhedra), written by `np_conversions` from a Python `str` or list of `str` and decoded back, so the core carries them opaquely and every format writer that has a string slot (Exodus info and QA records, CGNS `Descriptor_t`, MED descriptions, VTK `vtkStringArray`) can use it. (2) Booleans: recommended, a `meshio:bool:<array>` marker in `field_data` naming `uint8` arrays that are masks, honoured by the numpy conversion (returns `bool`) and by the formats with a boolean type; no dtype.
- **Steps.** The encoding in `detail/string_field.hpp` with tests; `np_conversions.hpp` both ways; the C API needs nothing new (arrays of bytes) but gets `mio_mesh_get_field_string` as a convenience; the four formats above; drop the silent fallback (a `str` in `field_data` no longer declines the core).
- **Done when.** `Mesh(..., field_data={"title": "wing"})` writes through the core (`MESHIOPLUSPLUS_STRICT_CORE=1`) to Exodus and reads back equal. **M, design pass first**

### 8.3 Integration-point data

Gauss-point results are the primary output of a structural solver, and no operation knows an array is per integration point.

- **State today.** Three per-format conventions, each a cell array of shape `(cells, points × width)` with its layout in `field_data`: Abaqus `.fil` (`abaqus:layout:<name>` = `[points, width]`, point-major, NaN-padded; names with `@sp<k>` section points; `formats/abaqus_fil.cpp` lines 1060–1148); d3plot (`lsdyna_d3plot:layout:<name>`, `formats/lsdyna_d3plot.cpp` lines 1426 and 1992); GiD (`gid:gauss_points:<name>` and `gid:gauss_coords:<name>`, `formats/gid.hpp` lines 275 and 303, the only one that records where the points are). MED's ELGA fields are read by the Python twin as a 3-D `(cells, NGA, components)` array, not written, and declined by the core (`med/_med.py` lines 695–719 and 1104). `.rst` element results and Gmsh's `$ElementNodeData` (2.3.6.1) have the same shape. `interpolate`, `data_average` and `tessellate` treat the columns as components.
- **Decisions to take first.** (1) Recommended: one convention replacing the three, not a third data location: `field_data["meshio:ip:<name>"] = [points, width, rule_id]` on a 2-D cell array, with `rule_id` naming a rule of 3.4.3's quadrature table (point positions and weights in the reference element), or 0 for "nodes of the cell" (element-nodal data) and -1 for "unknown rule" (the order is the file's); the readers write both their old key and the new one for one release. A third location (`ip_data`) would cost every backend, binding and writer for no operation the convention cannot serve. (2) Each format's rule and point order mapped to the table: Abaqus and Ansys element libraries, LS-DYNA's through-thickness points, MED's `GAUSS` localisations (read from the file: MED stores coordinates and weights), GiD's internal and given rules. Where a format's order is undocumented, -1. (3) The operations: `ip_to_points` (extrapolate with the element's shape functions, then average at nodes: the least-squares form for rules with fewer points than nodes), `ip_to_cells` (weighted average), and the reverse `points_to_ip` for writing.
- **Steps.** `detail/integration_points.hpp` (layout read and write, rule lookup); the readers and writers of the five formats on it, with MED ELGA written and read in the core; the two operations by [checklist O](#landing-checklists); `data_info` reporting integration-point arrays as such; conversion MED to GiD and `.fil` to MED as the round-trip tests.
- **Done when.** A `.fil` stress field converts to MED ELGA and to a nodal field whose values match Abaqus's own extrapolation (`.dat` printout) on the truss and a C3D8 run. **L, design pass first**

### 8.4 Data on facets and edges

- **State today.** A `Region` holds a name, kind, dimension, tag and entries (`include/meshioplusplus/region.hpp` lines 123–148; `Side` entries are `(global cell, local facet)` rows, numbered by `detail/cell_faces.hpp` and `cell_edges.hpp`) and no values; `Region` is 120 bytes in `test_abi_layout.cpp`. A value per boundary face today means a duplicated block of surface cells with cell data, which is what the VTK family and OpenFOAM's boundary fields use (the writer maps boundary-block rows to `boundaryField`).
- **Decisions to take first.** (1) Recommended: no change to `Region`; side data is cell data on the lower-dimensional block, and a side region can be turned into such a block and back: `sides_to_cells(mesh, region)` (the facets as a new block with a `meshio:side_of` `(cell, local facet)` cell array) and its inverse, so the formats that store values on sides (CGNS `FaceCenter` solutions and BC data, Exodus side-set variables, UGRID `location = "face"` on volumes, MED face families) read into that form and write from it. This makes the convention explicit and testable instead of per format. (2) Edge data likewise on `line` blocks with `meshio:edge_of`. (3) A data member on `Region` only if a format needs values on sides with no cells to hang them on.
- **Steps.** The two helpers by checklist O (they are `extract_skin`'s neighbours and reuse its facet table); the readers of 2.3.1.4, 2.3.2.3 and 2.4.3.2; a section in [regions](./regions.md).
- **Done when.** An Exodus side-set variable reads as cell data on a quad block that `sides_to_cells` would have produced, and writes back. **M, design pass first**

### 8.5 Hierarchy

- **State today.** Regions are flat; nesting is `/` in the name ([regions](./regions.md), lines 286–298), and the KRATOS backend is the one consumer that interprets it (`BuildSubModelPartsFromRegions`, `kratos_mesh.hpp` line 610; `RestoreRegions`, 995), losing only names that contain `.` and the `mDim`/`mTag` members. VTKHDF assemblies and `.vtm` flatten to it on read.
- **Decisions to take first.** Whether `/` names plus one `field_data` entry are enough to round-trip the trees files hold. Recommended: yes, with `field_data["meshio:tree"]` as a string list (8.2) of the internal nodes that own no entity (an assembly that only groups), since leaves are regions already. Check against: Abaqus parts and instances (instances repeat a part with a transform: the tree names them, the transform goes to `field_data`), VTKHDF `Assembly`, `.vtm` nesting, CGNS families of families, Exodus assemblies (2.3.2.4), USD prims.
- **Steps.** `detail/region_tree.hpp` (parse, build, parent and children queries) with the KRATOS interpreter moved onto it; the five formats' writers rebuild their nesting from it; `regions` reports print the tree.
- **Done when.** A nested `.vtm` and a VTKHDF file with an `Assembly` round-trip with the same nesting. **M, design pass first; after 8.2**

### 8.6 Structured grids that stay structured

- **State today.** `vti`, `vtr`, `vts` and legacy structured VTK are expanded to explicit cells by `detail::vtk_structured_cells` (`src/cpp/src/detail/vtk_xml_pieces.cpp` line 315): eight indices per cell for what three integers describe. Their writers accept only a mesh that `detail::lattice_from_mesh` (`detail/grid_lattice.hpp` line 205) recognises as a lattice, which is why they fail [conformance](./conformance.md) on the canonical mesh. CGNS structured zones are refused (2.3.1.3) and PLOT3D is not read (2.4.2). The physics-ML [grids](./grids.md) are the opposite case: `GridSpec`/`GridArray` in Python with no `Mesh`.
- **Decisions to take first.** (1) Recommended first step: an annotation, not a block kind: `field_data["meshio:structured:<block>"] = [ni, nj, nk]` (point counts) set by every structured reader on the block it expands, checked by `lattice_from_mesh` instead of re-deriving, honoured by the structured writers (which then also accept curvilinear lattices for `vts`, CGNS and PLOT3D without geometric detection), and removed by any operation that changes the block's points or cells (the pipeline clears it unless the step is data-only or a rigid transform; an explicit list, tested). This closes the three conformance failures by declaring what the formats hold, and costs no ABI. (2) An implicit-connectivity block (`CellView` generating corners from indices) only for 10⁸-cell voxel data: every operation's hot loop reads connectivity through `CellView`, and the measured cost of changing it is what kept the backends' `CellView`s apart ([§6](#code-duplication)). XL, and only with a workload.
- **Steps (annotation).** The key and its invalidation rule in [mesh data model](./mesh_data_model.md); readers (`vti`, `vtr`, `vts`, legacy VTK, VTKHDF image data, CGNS, PLOT3D, EnSight `block`) set it; writers use it; `conformance_spec.py`'s three `error` declarations become lattice round-trips; `to_grid(mesh)`/`from_grid` join `Mesh` and the Python `GridArray`.
- **Done when.** `read("a.vts")` then `write("b.vts")` is exact, and the same mesh after `smooth` is refused by name. **M** (annotation); **XL** (block kind), design pass first

---

## 9. Operations {#operations}

*Admission: an operation a mesh workflow reaches for that the core's own kernels nearly give, found by reading each operation's page for what it refuses and by comparing the surface with what users of comparable libraries call. Not the algorithms the [non-goals](#non-goals-and-decisions-taken) leave to backends. Each lands on every surface by [checklist O](#landing-checklists); the anchors below are the kernels to build on, located in the code in November 2026.*

### 9.1 Surface booleans through a backend

Reopened from the non-goals, which record why a native `mesh_boolean` was not queued: the [spike](./spikes.md#surface-booleans-formerly-roadmap-7-8) was exact in topology and failed on rounding its output and on coplanar contact, the two things a dedicated library spends its complexity on. The same non-goal says the answer to such an algorithm is an optional backend, and that route was not measured. **M, measure first**

- **State today.** The prototype: `mesh_boolean_spike(A, B, BooleanOp)` returning `BooleanResult {mOk, mFailure, mMesh, mReport}` with `boolean:source` cell data (`src/cpp/src/detail/mesh_boolean.hpp` line 104). The harness `src/cpp/benchmark/boolean_spike.cpp` (target `meshioplusplus_boolean_spike`) calls it directly at line 234 and prints one JSON line per run (`ok`, `watertight`, `self_intersection_free`, `rounding_inverted`, the volume identity errors, times). `tools/boolean_corpus.py` has `synthetic`, `fetch`, `run`, `summary`; the weekly `boolean_corpus` workflow runs it. Provider kinds are structs of `std::function`s in `include/meshioplusplus/providers.hpp` with named registration (`add_tet_mesher`, `tet_mesher(name)`, lines 145–166); add-on wheels follow `packages/python/meshioplusplus-mmg` and `cmake/addon_*.cmake`; plugins `cmake/plugins.cmake`.
- **Steps.**
  - **9.1.1 A backend switch in the harness.** `--backend spike|manifold` in `boolean_spike.cpp`, the Manifold path compiled only when `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_SPIKE_MANIFOLD` finds the library; no public API yet.
  - **9.1.2 Measure.** [Manifold](https://github.com/elalish/manifold) (Apache-2.0†; verify its dependencies' licences, Clipper2's Boost licence among them†; guaranteed-manifold output from manifold input, coplanar faces by symbolic perturbation, its own WebAssembly build). Run the synthetic corpus (`--synthetic 78 200`) and the Thingi10K pairs on the spike's own criteria: every result watertight (`surface_watertight_check`), free of self-intersections (`find_intersections` on the output), the two volume identities, and the coplanar classes that the spike could not handle (boxes sharing a face, identical spheres, a vertex on a face). Also what it does with inputs that are not manifold (it refuses: count how many Thingi10K models that excludes, and whether `repair` first changes the count). Record the table in the spike's page beside its own.
  - **9.1.3 If it passes: the operation.** A `BooleanProvider {mName, mVersion, mBoolean(const Mesh&, const Mesh&, BooleanOp) -> BooleanResult}` kind with `add_boolean_provider`, `boolean_provider(name)`, `boolean_provider_names()`; `mesh_boolean(a, b, op, options)` in `operations/mesh_boolean.hpp`, refusing by name with no provider; point and cell data carried through Manifold's per-triangle provenance (`boolean:source`, and a parent cell id per output triangle so `cell_data` maps); a `meshioplusplus-manifold` add-on wheel and plugin; the full WASM artifact links Manifold's own wasm build if its size is acceptable (measure). By checklist O, with a pipeline step.
  - **9.1.4 What it unblocks.** CSG of the [primitives](./primitives.md#not-generated) (`box - cylinder`) and the scan cut by a fitted solid (the non-goal *Booleans of a scan with fitted solids*): remove both entries when it lands. OpenCASCADE's B-rep booleans stay the route for fitted solids that must remain analytic.
- **Done when.** The corpus table is recorded; then either `meshioplusplus boolean a.stl b.stl --op union` works with the add-on installed, or the measured reason joins the spike's and the non-goal stands.

### 9.2 Smooth subdivision

- **State today.** [`refine`](./refine.md) splits at midpoints (`RefineOptions`, `operations/refine.hpp` line 356; `refine_once`, `refine.cpp` line 292) and [`subdivide`](./subdivide.md) is polyhedral; neither moves a point. No Loop or Catmull–Clark code exists anywhere (`fit_cad`'s patch network fits B-spline patches by other constructions). Crease and boundary classification exists: `crease_edges`, `crease_edges_triangles` and `CreaseEdge {IsBoundary, IsNonManifold, IsCrease}` (`src/cpp/src/detail/crease_edges.hpp`), already behind `feature_edges`, `decimate` and `smooth`.
- **Design.** A new operation `subdivide_surface(mesh, SubdivideSurfaceOptions {mScheme: Loop|CatmullClark, mLevels, mFeatureAngleDeg, mPreserveBoundary, mRecordParentIds})`, not an option of `refine`: `refine`'s contract (points of the input are points of the output, bit for bit; green closure; hierarchy arrays) does not hold when points move. Loop for triangle blocks (Warren's weights; boundary and crease edges by the cubic B-spline curve rule; corners fixed), Catmull–Clark for quads and polygons (output all quads); creases from `crease_edges` at the feature angle, semi-sharp not offered. Point data is subdivided with the same masks, cell data inherited by children, regions remapped through `detail/region_remap` with `FirstChild`. Topology by one pass over an edge table (`detail/edge_table` as `refine` uses), `parallel_for` over points for the masks, serial numbering for determinism. A numpy twin with the same arithmetic order.
- **Tests.** Limit positions on a regular patch against the closed form; a cube converging to its Catmull–Clark limit volume; a creased cube staying a cube at feature angle 30; determinism across backends (`thread_sweep.hpp`).
- **Done when.** `meshioplusplus subdivide-surface coarse.obj out.obj --scheme loop --levels 2` on every surface. **M**

### 9.3 Ray and nearest-point queries: remaining

`cast_rays` and `locate_points` landed on every surface ([ray casting and point location](./ray_queries.md)): the nearest hit of each ray, and the containing cell with its barycentric weights, in the core, Python, both CLIs, the MCP server and the flat bindings. What remains is the two things that page lists as not offered. **S–M**

- **9.3.1 Every crossing along a ray.** `cast_rays` reports the nearest hit only. `tri_bvh_segment_hits` (`detail/tri_bvh.hpp`) returns every crossing of a segment, de-duplicated across shared edges and vertices, but names no triangle, so an all-hits mode needs a traversal that keeps the triangle id (`tri_bvh_ray_filtered` is the nearest-hit analogue). Result shape: a CSR pair (`mRayOffset`, per-hit `mDistance`, `mCell`, `mPoint`), which every flat binding can return as arrays.
- **9.3.2 A query handle.** Only if a caller needs many batches on one mesh: a `SurfaceQuery` that owns the triangle soup and its hierarchy, following the existing handle conventions (C `mio_surface_query_create`/`_free` as `mio_sequence`; Julia a mutable struct with a finalizer; R an external pointer; WASM an integer handle in the module table; pybind a `py::class_`).
- **Done when.** `cast_rays(..., all_hits=True)` returns every crossing on every surface; the handle only on request.

### 9.5 Elevation beyond order 2

- **State today.** `ccells_elevate` (`src/cpp/src/operations/convert_cells.cpp` line 835) refuses the full-Lagrange targets `quad9`, `hexahedron27`, `wedge18` and `pyramid14` (lines 845–849, `ccells_is_full_lagrange`, 191–194) and has no entry above order 2 (`ccells_elevate_table`, 168; twin `_ELEVATE`, `_convert_cells.py` line 99). The pieces exist: arbitrary-order Lagrange bases and `interpolation_matrix(Shape, P, nodes, targets)` (`src/cpp/src/formats/lagrange_common.hpp` line 721; twin `_lagrange.py` line 454), `fixed_lagrange` naming the fixed types to order 10, and the node-ordering registry (`detail/node_order.hpp`). `tessellate` handles five quadratic types only.
- **Design.** `convert_cells(mesh, "elevate", order=q)` and `"reduce", order=q` for any supported shape: the new nodes are the geometry interpolated at the target lattice's nodes with `interpolation_matrix` (order p to q), deduplicated across cells by their owning edge, face or interior (an edge table and a face table keyed by sorted corner ids, as the order-2 path does for mid-edge nodes); point data interpolated by the same matrix; fixed types up to the registry's limit, VTK Lagrange ragged cells above. Elevating a linear mesh gives straight-sided high-order cells; curving them is `project_to_cad`'s job afterwards. After 3.4.3's first step (the header moved out of `formats/`), which is a rename.
- **Tests.** Elevate then reduce is the identity on points and data; a polynomial field of degree q is reproduced exactly at the new nodes; node counts against the closed form per shape; VTK reads a written order-4 mesh with positive Jacobians (the `pyvista` gated test).
- **Done when.** `convert_cells(elevate, order=3)` on a tetrahedral mesh gives `tetra20` on every surface, and `order=2, full=True` gives `hexahedron27`. **M**

### 9.6 Repeated and mixed green refinement

- **State today.** [`refine`](./refine.md#what-is-not-implemented) names two limits. A later selective pass refines the green (transitional) cells directly, so repeated passes over one region degrade quality without bound. And mixed-type green (a quadrilateral closed into triangles, a hexahedron into pyramids) is not offered, because one input block would emit several output blocks, which the `FirstChild` cell-map contract cannot express (`RefineResult::mCellMaps`, `operations/refine.hpp` line 427; `CellMapKind {Direct, FirstChild, Global}`, `include/meshioplusplus/detail/region_remap.hpp` line 100). `undo_green(coarse, fine)` exists (`operations/undo_green.hpp` line 134) but needs the coarse mesh and undoes a single-pass hierarchy only; the hierarchy reader is `detail/refine_hierarchy.hpp`.
- **Steps.**
  - **9.6.1 Undo before refining.** A `RefineOptions` flag `mUndoGreenFirst` (from the reserved space: `mio_refine_opts` has `reserved[5]` free; the C++ struct is pinned at 120 bytes, so a new member is Tier A): before a selective pass, cells whose `refine:entity` marks them green are merged back to their parent using `refine:parent_id` and `refine:cell_id` alone (no coarse mesh: rebuild the parent's corners from the children's, which the templates of `detail/refine_templates.cpp` make possible), the parent is marked for red refinement if any child was selected, and closure runs again. Requires `record_hierarchy`, and raises by name without it. Extends `undo_green` to multi-level hierarchies as a by-product. **M**
  - **9.6.2 Mixed-type green.** Decide the map shape first: `CellMapKind::Global` (one global child-range per input cell) exists internally; expose it for `refine` when `mClosure = MixedGreen`, with every binding's map accessor returning the global form. Then the templates: quad with one or three marked edges into triangles, hexahedron into pyramids and tetrahedra around a refined face. **M–L, design pass first**
- **Done when.** Ten selective passes over the same region keep the minimum scaled Jacobian above a stated bound (a test that fails today), driven by `error_size` in a notebook.

### 9.7 Partitioning

- **State today.** `PartitionMethod {SFC, KaHIP, Auto}` (`operations/partition.hpp` line 85); KaHIP is a compile-time option (`MESHIOPLUSPLUS_WITH_KAHIP`, `CMakeLists.txt` line 319), not a provider, called in `partition_kahip_parts` (`partition.cpp` lines 197–267) on the facet dual graph of `detail::build_facet_dual_graph` (`detail/dual_graph.hpp` line 66) with weights from `mWeightsKey`; without it the method throws by name (lines 274–284). METIS and Scotch are graph files only (`write_graph`, `operations/adjacency.hpp` line 133). The C API passes the method as a string (`mio_partition`, `meshioplusplus.h` line 3082), so a new method needs no C change.
- **Steps.**
  - **9.7.1 A partitioner provider kind.** `GraphPartitionerProvider {mName, mVersion, mPartition(const DualGraphCsr&, nparts, imbalance, seed, weights) -> std::vector<int64_t>}` with `add_graph_partitioner`, `graph_partitioner(name)`, `graph_partitioner_names()`; `partition`'s method becomes a name looked up there after `sfc`; KaHIP moves onto it as a compiled-in provider with its behaviour and digests unchanged. `PartitionMethod` gains no enumerator: add `PartitionOptions::mMethodName` (Tier A) or keep the enum for the built-ins and add the name beside it.
  - **9.7.2 METIS and Scotch providers.** METIS (`METIS_PartGraphKway`, Apache-2.0 since 5.1.1†) and Scotch (`SCOTCH_graphPart`, CeCILL-C†, LGPL-compatible) as optional compiled-in providers and plugins, in the pattern of the Netgen provider (`src/cpp/providers/netgen/`), each with a CI job like `kahip` (`ci.yml` line 591). Seeds fixed, so output is reproducible per library version; state that it is not across versions.
  - **9.7.3 Connected parts.** KaHIP's `--connected_blocks` is not reachable through the `kaffpa` C interface ([partition](./partition.md) records it). A method-independent post-pass `mConnectedParts`: label components per part with `cell_components` (`detail/cell_components.hpp` lines 55–60), keep each part's largest, and reassign every other component to the neighbouring part it shares most facets with, repeating until none remains; report the balance after it.
- **Done when.** `partition(mesh, 8, method="metis")` works with the provider present and refuses by name without, and `connected_parts=True` gives one component per part on the space-filling curve's output. **S–M**

### 9.8 The isogeometric layer's open list: remaining

[IGA](./iga.md) shipped second derivatives (`iga_evaluate(..., derivatives=2)`), `iga_bezier_extract`, `iga_remove_knots` and `iga_closest_point` for NURBS patches. What is left of the list is the cases those refuse. **S–M**

- **State today.** The three new operations take a `NurbsInfo` patch; a `BrepInfo` model is refused by name; knot removal refuses a model whose patch topology joins several patches (they share knot vectors); Bézier extraction produces cells, not operators; the closest point takes curves and surfaces.
- **Steps.**
  - **9.8.5 Extraction operators.** `iga_bezier_extract` could also return the extraction operator per element (the matrix taking the B-spline basis of the element to the Bernstein basis, what an IGA code assembles with), beside the cells it returns now. **S**
  - **9.8.6 Closest point on a volume.** A volume needs a point-in-solid test first (the closest point of a solid is the query point itself unless it is outside): the parameters by Newton inversion, then the boundary patches for a point outside. **S–M**
  - **9.8.7 B-rep faces and joined patches.** `iga_bezier_extract`, `iga_remove_knots` and `iga_closest_point` on the faces of a `BrepInfo` (the trims would follow the parametrisation, which removal only preserves within the tolerance), and knot removal on a model whose patch topology joins several patches (a knot goes only if it goes from every patch sharing the knot vector). **M**
- **Done when.** The *Not yet* line of the page holds only refinement of one patch of an MFEM model.

### 9.9 Point-cloud conditioning: remaining

`filter_points` landed on every surface ([point-cloud conditioning](./filter_points.md)): statistical and radius outlier removal, voxel and Poisson-disk thinning, with the pipeline step `FilterPoints`. What remains is the two things that page lists as not offered. **S**

- **9.9.1 An averaged voxel point.** The voxel step keeps the real point nearest each cube's mean, so the result never invents a point and `point_map` is exact. A second representative, the mean of each cube's coordinates and float arrays (an integer array taken from the member nearest the mean), needs a point map that says "new point" (-1) and an array-reducing step in the gather; add it as `mVoxelMode` beside `mVoxelSize`.
- **9.9.2 `subsample_points` in the core.** `select_points` and `subsample_points` (`src/python/meshioplusplus/_point_budget.py`) keep their count-driven `grid` and `farthest` methods in Python over `farthest_point_order` (`detail/farthest_points.hpp`, bound to Python alone). Moving them behind the same core operation gives the flat bindings the count-driven forms too, and needs the Python function's drop-and-remap of cells, regions and arrays reproduced on the C++ side (`filter_points` already does the gather and the Point regions); the Python functions stay as shims with unchanged output.
- **Done when.** `FilterPoints` reads `VoxelMode` with the averaged point, and `subsample_points(..., method="farthest")` runs through the core on every surface with its output byte-identical to today's.

### 9.10 Units and coordinate systems

#### 9.10.1 Units of fields

- **State today.** Length only: `field_data["meshio:length_unit"]` with `mesh_length_unit` and `rescale_mesh_length_unit` (`include/meshioplusplus/detail/length_unit.hpp`) and Python's `_units.py` (`length_unit`, `set_length_unit`, `unit_name`); used by CGNS (`DimensionalUnits_t` on the base, `cgns.cpp` lines 647–690), DXF, UNV, MED, Radioss, STEP. MED's per-field units are kept in a side channel (`MedInfo::mFieldUnits`, `formats/med.hpp` line 162; `field_data["med:field_units"]` in Python). A field's unit travels nowhere else.
- **Decisions to take first.** (1) Representation: recommended, the seven SI exponents plus a scale: `field_data["meshio:unit:<array>"] = [scale, m, kg, s, K, A, mol, cd]` (8 doubles), so a conversion is arithmetic with no unit database in the core; names (`"MPa"`) are parsed and printed in Python and the CLIs from a small table. (2) Which formats carry them: MED (per field), CGNS (`DimensionalUnits_t`/`DimensionalExponents_t` per array), Exodus (none: skip), EnSight (`# units` lines in the case file), UNV (the 164 dataset's unit system: derive per data type), Selafin (variable-name units, 2.4.3.4), Nastran and Abaqus decks (none: a user-declared system). (3) `convert_units(mesh, system)`: scales points, the length unit and every array with a known dimension to a target system (`SI`, `mm-t-s`, `mm-kg-ms`, …), refusing arrays with no declared unit by name unless told to leave them.
- **Done when.** A MED file in millimetres and megapascals converts to SI and back to the same bytes. **M, design pass first**

#### 9.10.2 Georeferencing

Now actionable: 2.4.3 (UGRID NetCDF, ADCIRC, Selafin) and 2.4.6.1 (LAS, whose VLRs carry the CRS verbatim in `las:vlrs`) have landed, so georeferenced meshes are read. `field_data["meshio:crs"]` as a WKT2 or `EPSG:<code>` string (8.2's encoding), set by the UGRID, LAS and ADCIRC readers and written back; reprojection as a mode of [`convert_coordinates`](./coordinates.md) (`CoordinateOptions`, `operations/coordinates.hpp` line 119) through PROJ, an optional dependency in the TecIO pattern (2.4.6.2) with a Python twin through `pyproj`; without it the operation refuses by name. glTF and 3D Tiles get the ECEF convention only on request. **S–M**

*(9.11 FVM face-pair quality, 9.12 bilateral/guided surface denoising, 9.13 MCF curve-skeleton extraction, 9.14 shortest geodesic paths and surface Voronoi, and 9.15 parametric FFD and RBF morphing have landed.)*

### 9.16 Cancellation and progress reporting for long-running operations {#progress-cancel}

Heavy operations (`fit_cad`, `octree_mesh`, `adapt_volume`, `optimize_volume`, `tetrahedralize`, `sequence`) run for seconds to minutes with the Python GIL released (`doc/architecture.md#threads-and-the-gil`), so `KeyboardInterrupt` (`SIGINT`) cannot interrupt them, and interactive callers (CLI, Jupyter widget 4.12, browser Web Workers, hosted MCP 4.18.7) receive no progress updates and cannot enforce timeouts. **M, design pass first**

- **State today.** No operation header under `src/cpp/include/meshioplusplus/operations/` accepts a cancellation token or progress callback.
- **Decisions to take first.** (1) Zero-overhead hot-loop rule: a lightweight `ProgressContext {std::atomic<bool>* mCancel; std::function<bool(double, const char*)> mCallback;}` (or a C-compatible function pointer + `void*` user data so it crosses the C API unchanged), passed via an optional parameter or a scoped thread-local guard (`ScopedProgressContext`) so existing option-struct layouts do not change (no ABI break on every operation struct!). Checked only at outer-pass boundaries (never inside per-cell inner loops), so tier S instruction counts stay within the 1% gate. (2) Semantics on cancel: throw a dedicated `CancelledError` (mapped to `MIO_ERR_CANCELLED` in C and `KeyboardInterrupt` / `CancelledError` in Python). (3) Python wiring: periodically call `PyErr_CheckSignals()` (briefly acquiring the GIL every ~100 ms or outer pass) so Ctrl-C interrupts C++ loops cleanly, plus an optional `progress=` callable. (4) WASM and MCP: forward progress to Web Worker `postMessage` in the browser viewer and wire wall-clock deadlines in hosted MCP (4.18.7) onto `mCancel`.
- **Steps.** `include/meshioplusplus/progress.hpp` + `CancelledError`; instrument the six heaviest operations (`fit_cad`, `octree_mesh`, `adapt_volume`, `optimize_volume`, `tetrahedralize`, `sequence`) at outer-loop boundaries; expose `mio_cancel_token` and `mio_set_progress_callback` in the C API and Python GIL-safe signal check in `bindings/python/_core.cpp`.
- **Done when.** Setting the cancel flag stops `adapt_volume` and `octree_mesh` within one outer pass under ASan/TSan, Ctrl-C interrupts a long Python call cleanly, and `tools/bench_instructions.py --check benchmark/instructions_S.csv` passes with default (null) progress context.

---

## 10. In-situ and coupling interchange {#in-situ-interchange}

*Admission: a data description a simulation or a coupling library hands over in memory, which meshio++ can convert to and from without reading a file. Added in November 2026 from a proposed revision of this file; every item here was checked against the tree for a prior implementation (none found) and is marked **verify first** for the library's own current API. MPI inside the library stays a [non-goal](#non-goals-and-decisions-taken): these adapters take the data a rank holds, as [`partition`](./partition.md)'s ghost layers already do.*

### 10.1 A Conduit Mesh Blueprint adapter: remaining {#conduit-blueprint}

10.1.1 has landed: `to_conduit`/`from_conduit` in Python ([interop](./interop.md#conduit-mesh-blueprint)), on a `conduit.Node` or a nested dict, with exact round trips for every linear shape, mixed, polygonal and polyhedral topologies and grids expanded on read. Left: the gated `conduit.blueprint.mesh.verify` test has not yet run against a real Conduit (its Python module is not on PyPI; a CI leg on conda-forge's `conduit` would run it), and 10.1.2, a native provider, only if a consumer turns out unable to use Python. **S**

### 10.2 Catalyst and Ascent as consumers: remaining {#catalyst-ascent}

`contrib/catalyst/blueprint_series.py` ([route](./routes/catalyst.md)) writes the Blueprint Catalyst 2's `catalyst_execute` or an Ascent Python extract receives as a `.vtkhdf` series or a file per step, tested against stand-in nodes. Left: the recorded run inside ParaView Catalyst and Ascent, item 2.2.19. **S**

### 10.3 ADIOS2 BP5 and Fides {#adios2-fides}

Checked (November 2026): [`vtx`](./formats/vtx.md) reads DOLFINx's VTX schema (BP4 and BP5) and writes it as BP5; a `.bp` with Fides's `Fides_Data_Model` attribute and no `vtk.xml` is a `ReadError` that names Fides. Fides is not read or written, and no reader that could check a Fides file (ParaView's `vtkFidesReader`, VTK-m's Fides) is in the VTK wheel or installable in the development sandbox, so it is held until one is. **M**

- **Steps.** Add Fides as a second schema of the same format (a `schema=` read and write option, the `unstructured_single`, `unstructured`, `uniform` and `rectilinear` data models) under the same `ADIOS2` build gate, in both engines; check each file both ways against ParaView's Fides reader, as `tools/gen_vtx_reference.py` drives `ADIOS2VTXReader` for the first schema, and record it beside item 2.2.18.
- **Done when.** A Fides file ParaView wrote reads as the mesh ParaView shows, and a file meshio++ writes with `schema="fides"` opens in ParaView with the same points, cells and fields.

### 10.4 Ghost, owner and global-id conventions: remaining {#ghost-conventions}

The names are fixed in [ghosts, owners and global ids](./ghosts.md) (`detail/ghost_names.hpp`, `_ghosts.py`), the formats that hold the data use them, and Exodus reads and writes its number maps as `global:node_id`/`global:cell_id`. A ghosted partition written as `.pvtu`, read with `ghosts="drop"` and ordered by `partition:original_*` gives the serial mesh back exactly. Left: `partition:owner` has no producer yet (the Nemesis reader of 2.3.8.2 and a `partition` option would be the first), and the reassembly is a test, not an operation: 2.3.8.1's `merge_by_global_id` takes these names as its inputs. **S**

## 11. A modern C++ API surface {#modern-cpp-api}

*Admission: the installable C++ API ([C++ API](./cpp_api.md)) as a library user meets it, as opposed to its formats or operations. Each item here changes an installed header, so each is a pure addition or an [ABI](./abi.md) bump named in its steps. Link 4.14 (the module) and 3.13 (the ABI diff) rather than repeating them.*

### 11.1 Errors as values {#errors-as-values}

Every reader throws `ReadError` (`exceptions.hpp`) and the C API turns it into a status; a C++ caller who wants to branch on "this file is not this format" pays for an exception per probe. **M, design pass first**

- **Decisions to take first.** (1) The type: `std::expected<Mesh, Error>` where the compiler's library has it (C++23) and a small header-only twin otherwise, against a `Result<T>` of the project's own that needs no feature test. (2) `Error` carries the format name, the byte offset or line where the reader knows one (it mostly does not: say so on the page and fill it where `detail/parse_guard.hpp` and the line readers do) and the C API's status code, one to one. (3) Whether `try_read`/`try_write` are new entry points beside the throwing ones (a pure addition, recommended) or replace them (an ABI bump).
- **Steps.** `try_read`, `try_read_as`, `try_write` and `try_sniff` as thin wrappers over the registry (`registry_read` and the `catch` converting `ReadError`, `WriteError`, `Unsupported`); a test that every registered reader's refusal of a junk file arrives as an `Error` naming its format; an ABI review row.
- **Done when.** `try_read` on 64 random bytes returns an `Error` for each registered format with no exception escaping, under ASan and UBSan.

### 11.2 Views, not copies {#mesh-views}

*Verify first* how the uniform API hands back arrays today (`mesh_api.hpp`, `ndarray.hpp`): a caller who wants the points of a mesh to feed a solver copies them or reaches for a backend member. **M**

- **Steps.** A non-owning `MeshView` (points as `std::span<const double>` with stride 3, one `CellBlockView` per block with its `CellType` and a two-dimensional view of the connectivity as `std::mdspan` where available and a hand-written `Strided2D` otherwise), built from any backend through `AssignPoints`' read-side twin and `CellView`; lifetime rules as the C API's borrowed getters (valid until the mesh is mutated); the single header carries it.
- **Reuse.** The C API's getters already borrow; the WASM and Python bindings already expose the same buffers (`np_conversions.hpp`): the view is their native shape.
- **Done when.** A solver-shaped loop (a `std::span` pass over `points` and `cells`) runs with no copy and no backend member, on MESHIO, NATIVE and KRATOS.

### 11.3 Concepts for user meshes {#mesh-concepts}

After 11.2. A `MeshLike` concept (what `registry_write` needs from a type) so an application's own mesh class can be written without being converted first. **M**

- **Steps.** `template <MeshLike M> void write(const std::string&, const M&)` building a `MeshView`, so no format code changes; a test with a deliberately minimal user type; the page records which backends satisfy it.

## 12. Provenance, remote data and dataset interop {#provenance-remote}

*Admission: where a mesh and its data come from and where they are kept, beyond the formats themselves. Added in November 2026 from the same proposed revision; the parts that already exist (fsspec URLs in Python, dataset manifests, the PyG `Dataset`, ghost layers, Zarr v3 in Python) are not re-listed.*

### 12.1 Provenance as W3C PROV and RO-Crate {#prov-rocrate}

[Provenance](./provenance.md) records what produced a mesh in its own schema. A reviewer or a data repository wants the standard ones. **S**

- **Steps.** `provenance_to_prov(mesh)` (PROV-JSON†: an entity per input file with its digest, an activity per recorded operation with its options, the software agent with the version) and `write_ro_crate(directory, mesh, outputs)` (`ro-crate-metadata.json`† listing the files with their formats); both Python-only, MCP exemptions recorded in `_NOT_TOOLS` unless they are expressible through file paths (the crate writer is: add it as a tool).
- **Done when.** The PROV document validates against PROV-JSON's schema†, and the crate against `rocrate-validator`†.

### 12.2 Remote reads beyond Python {#remote-reads}

*Verify first.* [Formats](./formats.md#remote-files) reads URLs through the optional `fsspec` in Python only; the native CLI and WASM do not (the [CLI page](./cli.md) says so). **M, design pass first**

- **Decisions to take first.** Whether the native CLI gains an HTTP(S) range reader with a vendored client (a dependency and a supply-chain item for 3.14.6's SBOM), or stays Python-only by decision and the page says why; for HDF5 formats, the ROS3 virtual file driver† where the bundled HDF5 is built with it.
- **Done when.** The decision is a line under [non-goals](#non-goals-and-decisions-taken) or the item is split into steps.

### 12.3 Statistics and resumption for `dataset add` {#dataset-build}

[`meshioplusplus dataset add`](./datasets.md) registers files by glob into a manifest. *Verify first* whether an entry records per-file statistics (cells, bounds, field ranges) and whether re-running skips files already registered. **S**

- **Steps.** A `--stats` option on `add` filling each entry from `read_metadata` (no full read; `_cli/_dataset.py`), and `--resume`, which skips an entry whose file digest is already in the manifest.
- **Done when.** Interrupting and re-running a 100-file `add --resume` reads no file twice.

### 12.4 A resource-limit audit of every reader {#resource-limits}

`max_cells`-style caps exist (the parse guards of `detail/parse_guard.hpp`, the windowed-sparse bound of `ansys_rst.cpp`, the per-reader limits [fuzzing](./fuzzing.md) lists); what is not stated anywhere is whether every reader has all of them. **M**

- **Steps.** A table in [fuzzing](./fuzzing.md) per reader: cap on points and cells declared in a header, on any single allocation, on a decompression ratio (gzip, zlib, zstd, lzma, lz4 inside VTU, HDF5 filters), on XML include or entity depth (`pugixml` options of the VTK XML family: external entities refused), and on HDF5 external links and virtual datasets (refused unless an option allows them); a reader missing a cap gets it through `detail/parse_guard.hpp`, with its minimized input under `tests/fuzz/regressions/<format>/` and the same refusal in the Python twin.
- **MCP.** Cross-check [MCP](./mcp.md)'s sandbox against 4.9: output and temporary files created with restrictive modes and inside the allowed directory only (*verify first* in `mcp/_tools.py`'s path handling).
- **Done when.** The table has no empty cell, and a `tests/fuzz/` decompression-bomb seed is refused by every codec-bearing reader without allocating more than the cap.

## 13. Educational animation with manim {#manim}

*Admission: explaining meshes, and what the library does to them, in rendered video: a bridge to [ManimCommunity](https://github.com/ManimCommunity/manim), the helpers that draw a mesh with it, and a library of scenes. No item here adds mesh functionality; a scene that needs an operation the library lacks names the item of §9 it waits on. Added in November 2026, and rewritten the same month against ManimCommunity (`pip install manim`, MIT, [docs.manim.community](https://docs.manim.community)) instead of the ManimGL fork it was first read against: the fork only served to hand the code over, so nothing here patches manim, and every class named below is either meshio++'s own or ManimCommunity's public API. Claims about ManimCommunity's API that no one has run against a released version are marked †, *verify first*, with the probe that settles them (13.6.2 fixes the version).*

**State today, for the whole section.** Nothing in meshio++ names manim: no bridge in `src/python/meshioplusplus/_interop.py`, no extra in `pyproject.toml`, no page, no `example/manim/`. What ManimCommunity gives for a mesh is not a mesh: `Polyhedron` is a `VGroup` of one `Polygon` per face†, `Surface` a parametric grid, and a `ThreeDScene` draws with the Cairo renderer, a CPU vector renderer that sorts the faces of the scene by depth each frame (`set_camera_orientation`, `begin_ambient_camera_rotation`, `add_fixed_in_frame_mobjects`, `Line3D`, `Dot3D`†). So a mesh is one `Polygon` per face, flat-coloured, with its stroke as its edges: a quad stays a quad, but there is no per-vertex colour, no smooth shading and no clip plane, and the cost is per face (a Python object and a Cairo path each), which bounds a scene to a few thousand faces (13.2.5, measured in 13.6.4). Cairo needs no GPU and no display, so it renders on a plain CI runner. ManimCommunity's own OpenGL renderer† and ManimGL (`pip install manimgl`) are the routes to larger meshes, queued behind the measurements (13.3.6).

### 13.1 Decisions to take first {#manim-decisions}

Written down as the first section of `doc/manim.md` before any code, each with the option recommended here. **S, design pass first**

- **13.1.1 Which manim.** ManimCommunity, because it is what `pip install manim` installs, it is documented, and its Cairo renderer works headless. The supported version range is whatever 13.6.2's matrix shows to work (its 0.x API moves between releases†). ManimGL on moderngl is a second backend, queued as 13.3.6 and not before, and the fork is not used anywhere.
- **13.1.2 Where each piece lives.** The payload and `to_manim` in meshio++ (`_interop.py`, Python-only, lazy, in the pattern of 4.3), because they are mesh code and the payload tests with no manim installed; `to_manim` returns a plain `manim.VGroup` of `Polygon`s, so the library defines no manim subclass and pins no private manim API. The helpers (highlight, fade, explode, field playback, annotations) are functions and `Animation` subclasses in `example/manim/meshio_scenes/`, importable by a scene and not part of the library's API, because they are documentation of the library and follow manim's releases, not ours.
- **13.1.3 Installation.** `pip install "meshioplusplus[manim]"`, a new extra left out of `all` as the other interop-sized extras are (the rule is in the comments of `pyproject.toml` lines 67 to 140), and the hint of `_require` (`_interop.py` line 1035) names it. System requirements the page must state: FFmpeg, Cairo and Pango (wheels for `pycairo` and `manimpango` exist for the common platforms†), LaTeX only for scenes that use `Tex` or `MathTex`. The Python floor is the higher of this project's (`pyproject.toml` line 10) and ManimCommunity's†.
- **13.1.4 Where videos live.** Never in git, and not in LFS (a re-render replaces every byte). Recommended: rendered in CI or by hand from a tag, attached to the GitHub release as `meshioplusplus-videos-<version>.zip`, the docs site embedding them with `<video>` from the release URL and a poster frame committed as PNG; a hosting platform (a channel) is a mirror, decided by the maintainers. Licence: CC BY 4.0 for the videos and their scripts' narration text, MIT for the scene code; third-party fixtures only where their licence allows redistribution in a video (the Stanford bunny's terms†: *verify first*, or use a generated primitive).
- **13.1.5 Sound and language.** Recommended for v1: no voice, on-screen captions in English drawn by the scene and a WebVTT file generated from the same strings, so a translation is a string table and not a re-render of the picture. Voice-over (`Scene.add_sound`†) is a follow-up that needs a narrator, not code.
- **13.1.6 What a video may claim.** Every number shown on screen (a cell count, a quality value, a timing) is computed by the scene from the mesh at render time, never typed, the rule 14.5.3 applies to the paper; a scene that shows a comparison with another tool runs that tool or does not show it.
- **Done when.** The six decisions are on the page with their dates, and 13.2 to 13.6 are edited to match any that went the other way.

### 13.2 The payload layer {#manim-payload}

What a renderer that draws one flat polygon per face needs from a mesh, as numpy arrays and nothing else: testable with no manim installed, and the same data for ManimCommunity's Cairo renderer, for its OpenGL renderer and for the ManimGL route of 13.3.6. **S–M**

- **State today.** `_to_triangles_payload(mesh, zero_copy_only=False)` (`_interop.py` line 698) returns a `TrianglesPayload` (line 645: `vertices` `(V, 3)` float64, `faces` `(F, 3)` int64, `vertex_data`, `face_data`, region masks, `ops`, `notes`), composing `extract_surface`, `convert_cells("linearize")` and `convert_cells("simplexify")` in `_triangulate` (line 665), never a reimplementation. It is triangles only, double precision, and carries no colour, no edges and no map from a face back to the cell it came from: `_triangulate` calls `extract_surface(mesh)` without `record_parent_ids=True` (`_surface.py` line 176), the option that gives `"surface:parent_cell"`. `colorize(mesh, field, cmap=…, vmin=…, vmax=…, symmetric=…, steps=…, component=…, return_report=True)` (`_colorize.py` line 18) bakes a field into `red`/`green`/`blue` UInt8 point or cell data and reports `{"vmin", "vmax", "location"}`; it is core-only, with no numpy twin.
- **Steps.**
  - **13.2.1 The dataclass.** `ManimPayload` beside the three payloads there, frozen: `vertices` `(V, 3)` float64 (ManimCommunity takes double-precision arrays, so there is no float32 cast); the faces of the skin as `face_start` `(F + 1,)` and `face_nodes`, a ragged list in which a triangle, a quad or a polygon stays one face; `face_rgba` `(F, 4)` float64 in `[0, 1]`, one colour per face (a cell's own colour, or the mean of its corners' point colours, since a Cairo polygon has one fill); `face_cell` `(F,)` int64, the global block-major index of the input cell each face came from (the region convention of `AGENTS.md`), `-1` where there is none, and `face_block` `(F,)` int32; `segments` `(E, 2, 3)` for line cells and feature edges that no face outlines; `points` `(N, 3)` for a mesh with no cells; `scalar_range` `(vmin, vmax)` and `scalar_name` for a legend; `fit` (`center` and `scale`, the similarity that brings the mesh into scene units, kept so labels and a second mesh of the same scene can use it and so it can be undone); `ops`, `notes`, `derived` as the other payloads.
  - **13.2.2 The builder.** `_to_manim_payload(mesh, *, color_by=None, cmap="viridis", vmin=None, vmax=None, symmetric=False, steps=0, component=None, color="#808080", opacity=1.0, edges="cell", feature_angle=30.0, fit_height=6.0, fit=None, max_faces=None, shrink=None, triangulate=False)`. Order matters and each stage is an existing operation: `colorize` first when `color_by` is given (on the input mesh, so the range is the field's, not the skin's); then `extract_surface` with parent ids, which means giving `_triangulate` a `record_parent_ids` argument and keeping the skin's own polygons unless `triangulate=True` (then through `linearize` and `simplexify`, which keep cell data: *verify first*; if `simplexify` does not repeat a parent's value on its children, that is a defect to fix there, not here); then the fit in float64 (13.2.6). `fit_height` is in scene units, against the default 3D frame height of ManimCommunity†.
  - **13.2.3 Edges.** `edges="cell"`: the outline of each face, which is the outline of the *input* cell's face and not of the triangles a quad was split into, and which a Cairo polygon draws with its stroke, so no separate edge list exists for a skin of polygons (a hexahedral mesh shows quads without any work). `"feature"` through `feature_edges` ([feature edges](./feature_edges.md)) with `feature_angle`, drawn in a heavier stroke over the faces; `"triangle"` sets `triangulate=True`; `"none"` sets the stroke width to zero. A curved (order 2 and more) edge is sampled, through [`tessellate`](./tessellation.md): *verify first* that its `Tessellation` helpers return the edge polylines, and state the sample count.
  - **13.2.4 High order and volume interiors.** A higher-order skin is tessellated rather than linearized when `curved=True` (default for meshes that hold such cells), so a `triangle6` shows its curvature. A volume mesh shows its skin; the interior is shown by cutting first ([`clip`](./clip.md), [`slice`](./slice.md), [`crop`](./crop.md)), which is the scene's job, or by `shrink=<factor>` in the builder, which shrinks every cell about its centroid so that the cells of a volume mesh are seen apart (each cell's own faces are then drawn, not the skin: bound it with `max_faces`).
  - **13.2.5 Size.** The cost is per face, not per byte: each face becomes a `Polygon`, a Python object and a Cairo path, and the faces of a 3D scene are depth-sorted on the CPU every frame†. `max_faces` therefore refuses by name above a bound taken from 13.6.4 (a few thousand is the figure to confirm, not a measurement), naming [`decimate`](./decimate.md) and `extract_surface` as the reduction and the raster path of 13.3.5 for a mesh that must stay whole, never decimating silently; the frame time at 10², 10³, 5·10³ and 10⁴ faces goes on the page.
  - **13.2.6 Precision.** Subtract the centre and scale in float64, the arrays staying float64, so a mesh in UTM coordinates does not lose its shape. A degenerate bounding box (a point, a line) fits to a unit scale with a note.
- **Tests.** In `tests/python/test_interop.py`, ungated (no manim needed), beside the payload tests the trimesh block starts at (line 471 lists `has_trimesh` among the probes): shapes and dtypes for a tetrahedral cube, a hexahedral one (quad faces, not triangles), a `triangle6` patch, a point cloud and an empty mesh; `face_cell` points at the right input cell on a two-block mesh; `face_rgba` equals `colorize`'s channels; the fit round-trips; `max_faces` refuses by name.
- **Done when.** `python -c "from meshioplusplus._interop import _to_manim_payload"` builds a payload for `example/data/hook.mdpa` whose `face_cell` has one entry per face, with no manim importable.

### 13.3 The bridge and the helpers {#manim-mobjects}

`to_manim` in meshio++, the rest in `example/manim/meshio_scenes/`; everything takes ManimCommunity as it is, so it is reviewable and testable here with `pip install manim` and nothing else. **M**

- **State today.** As the section's first paragraph. Mobject calls this section relies on and that are to be probed against the pinned release†: `Polygon(*points)` for a planar face, `VGroup`, `set_fill(color, opacity)` and `set_stroke(color, width, opacity)`, `set_shade_in_3d(True)` (which darkens a face by its normal and the camera's light source†), `Line3D`, `Dot3D`, and in a `ThreeDScene` `set_camera_orientation(phi, theta)`, `begin_ambient_camera_rotation`, `add_fixed_in_frame_mobjects` and `ValueTracker`. No clip plane exists in the Cairo renderer, so anything that needs one is rebuilt from per-face visibility (13.4.3).
- **Steps.**
  - **13.3.1 `to_manim` in meshio++.** `to_manim(mesh, **payload_options)` in `_interop.py`: `_require("manim", …)` with the extra's hint of 13.1.3, then a `VGroup` with one `Polygon` per face of `_to_manim_payload` (`set_fill` from `face_rgba`, `set_stroke` from the edge style, `set_shade_in_3d(True)`), the submobject order equal to the face order so an index is a face, the payload attached as `group.meshio_payload` so a scene reaches `fit`, `scalar_range` and the maps. `has_manim()` through `_importable("manim")` plus a probe that `Polygon` and `ThreeDScene` import, so a broken install answers `False` with a message naming the extra. Exports: `__all__`, the lazy map (`_LAZY_ATTRS`, where `"to_trimesh": "_interop"` is, `__init__.py` near line 533) and the `TYPE_CHECKING` imports; `_NOT_TOOLS` entries (`tests/python/test_mcp.py` line 2535), since an in-memory mobject cannot cross MCP. There is no `from_manim`: a mobject is a picture, not a mesh, the reason [interop](./interop.md#no-from-pandas-from-polars) gives for the data frames.
  - **13.3.2 Helpers on that group.** In `meshio_scenes/mesh.py`, functions of a group and its payload, addressing faces through `face_cell`: `highlight_cells(group, cell_ids, color)` and `fade_cells(group, cell_ids, opacity)`, each returning the animation that does it; `cell_submobject(group, cell_ids)` lifting a cell's faces out into a new `VGroup`; `explode(group, by, factor)` shifting each block's or each region's faces away from the common centre, as an animation; `set_field(group, rgba)` recolouring every face (a Python loop over the submobjects, the per-frame cost 13.6.4 measures); `get_cell_center(group, cell_id)` for labels.
  - **13.3.3 Edges that no face outlines, and points.** Line cells and feature edges as one `Line3D` each, or as the subpaths of a single `VMobject` (`set_points_as_corners` per edge, or the subpath constructor the class has†): measure both at 10², 10³ and 10⁴ edges and keep the one that renders faster at equal quality. Nodes as `Dot3D`, or a point-cloud mobject†. State on the page the edge count where each stops being usable.
  - **13.3.4 Shading.** Flat, one colour and one lighting factor per face, and no smooth shading in the Cairo path: a sphere looks faceted. This is a stated limit, not a defect to fix here; smooth shading comes with 13.3.6 (a shader that reads a normal per vertex, fed by [`compute_normals`](./normals.md) with a crease angle so a hard edge stays hard) and with the raster path below.
  - **13.3.5 Large meshes: a raster path.** For a mesh above `max_faces` (the bunny, a CAD skin), draw it with meshio++'s own [`render`](./tui.md) and bring the frames in as pictures: a turntable as an image sequence, or a field playback as one frame per step, shown with `ImageMobject` and updated by an updater. Labels, captions and a colour bar are ordinary manim mobjects laid over it, positioned through `fit`. What is lost is depth interplay with 3D mobjects and any per-face animation. *Verify first* what `render` offers for the camera, the image size and a per-cell colour, and its time per frame, on the pilot's meshes.
  - **13.3.6 Beyond Cairo.** *Verify first, S–M.* Whether ManimCommunity's OpenGL renderer† (`--renderer=opengl`, with `OpenGLSurface` and custom shaders) can draw an indexed triangle list with a colour per vertex and a normal for smooth shading, and whether it is maintained enough to depend on; and whether a ManimGL (`manimgl`) target, a second set of helpers over the same payload, is worth the maintenance. Decided from 13.6.4's numbers and 13.6.3's result, not before.
- **Tests.** In `tests/python/test_interop.py`, a manim block in the pattern of the trimesh one, skipped by `has_manim()`: the group has one submobject per payload face, the fill of each equals `face_rgba`, `meshio_payload` is attached, the hint names the extra when `manim` is missing. The helpers' frame tests are 13.4's.
- **Done when.** `meshioplusplus.to_manim(meshioplusplus.read("example/data/hook.mdpa"))` returns a `VGroup` that a three-line `ThreeDScene` renders with `manim -ql -s`.

### 13.4 Animations {#manim-animations}

The moves an explanation of meshes needs, as `Animation` subclasses and helpers in `example/manim/meshio_scenes/` (they use the library to compute what they show). Each is a few dozen lines on 13.3. **M**

- **13.4.1 A field over time.** `FieldPlayback(group, series, field, run_time, *, cmap, vmin, vmax)`: an `Animation` whose `interpolate_mobject(alpha)` picks the step from a [`TimeSeries`](./sequences.md) (`_sequence.py` line 706: indexable, holds the plan and never a mesh) and recolours the faces through `set_field`. The range is fixed over the whole series before the first frame (one pass over the steps reading only that array, `read(..., arrays=[field])`: [selective reads](./selective_read.md)), or the colours would flicker; steps are cached by index with a bound, and frames between two steps hold the earlier one unless `blend=True` interpolates the scalar (not the colour) between them. A changing topology across steps rebuilds the group at that step and says so in a note; a series whose faces exceed `max_faces` goes through the raster path of 13.3.5.
- **13.4.2 Warping.** `WarpByVector(group, displacement, factor)`: vertices moved by a point vector field times an animatable factor (a `ValueTracker`), for mode shapes ([eigenmodes](./eigenmodes.md)) and deformations; with 13.4.1 for a transient. A `Polygon`'s points are set from the displaced vertices each frame, so it stays on the small-mesh path; the displacement is carried per vertex in the payload (`vertex_id`, the input point index of each face corner: add it to 13.2.1 when this item starts).
- **13.4.3 Before and after an operation.** Topology changes, so `Transform` between the two meshes is wrong by construction (it would align faces by index). Three honest forms: a cross-fade (`FadeOut` and `FadeIn`); a wipe, both meshes present and a moving plane that decides, face by face against its centroid, which of the two is shown (the Cairo renderer has no clip plane, so the opacity of each face is set, in an updater); and a true morph only where the operation gives a correspondence: `refine` and `subdivide` children know their parent (*verify first* which operations report a parent map: [refine](./refine.md), [subdivide](./subdivide.md), `extract_surface`'s `record_parent_ids`), so the coarse face can be drawn pre-split into its children and the new points moved from the parent's edge midpoints to their final places. `OperationWipe(before, after, direction)` and `RefineMorph(coarse, fine, parent)`.
- **13.4.4 Building and taking apart.** `GrowCells(group, order)`: faces revealed in a given cell order (by index, by distance from a seed through [`geodesic_distance`](./geodesic.md), by an advancing front through [adjacency](./adjacency.md)), by an opacity ramp over `face_cell`; its reverse; `explode` by block, by region ([regions](./regions.md)) or by part ([`partition`](./partition.md)) with ghost cells ([ghosts](./ghosts.md)) shown translucent on both sides.
- **13.4.5 Annotations.** `ColorBar(scalar_range, cmap, label, steps)` fixed in frame (`add_fixed_in_frame_mobjects` in a `ThreeDScene`†), its ticks computed, with the same colour table `colorize` used (take the table from the core through `colorize` on a ramp mesh, do not retype it); `NodeLabels(group, cell_id)` numbering a cell's nodes in the file's order from the tables `doc/diagrams/diaglib/tables.py` loads, for [cell types](./cell_types.md) and [node ordering](./node_ordering.md), hidden-edge dashing as `diaglib/cellart.py` draws it; `CellCallout(cell_id, text)`; `CodeCaption(text)` showing the one Python line a move corresponds to, set in ManimCommunity's `Code` mobject† (it needs no LaTeX).
- **13.4.6 Camera helpers.** `frame_mesh(scene, group, margin)` and an orbit through `begin_ambient_camera_rotation`, with a fixed default view (the 70°/110° of `diaglib/project.py`, as `set_camera_orientation` angles) so stills match the documentation's figures.
- **Tests.** Each class gets a case in `example/manim/tests/test_frames.py`: the scene is rendered at low quality to a PNG with `-s` (skip to the last frame) and compared with a committed reference by a tolerance, in the `manim` CI job of 13.6.2; the pure parts (the step picking of 13.4.1, the tick positions of 13.4.5, the parent maps of 13.4.3, the wipe's per-face side test) are ordinary unit tests that need no renderer.
- **Done when.** A scene of under 40 lines shows `hook.mdpa` coloured by a field over a series with a colour bar, then wipes to its refined version.

### 13.5 The scene library and the series {#manim-series}

The educational content itself. **L**

- **Layout.** `example/manim/`: `README.md` (installing `meshioplusplus[manim]`, rendering one episode, the conventions below), `meshio_scenes/` (the package of 13.3 and 13.4: `common.py` with the palette taken from `doc/diagrams/diaglib/palette.py` so videos and figures agree, title and end cards, the caption helper of 13.1.5, the default camera), `episodes/NN_<slug>.py` (one file per episode, one `Scene` class per shot so a shot can be re-rendered alone), `storyboards/NN_<slug>.md` (the script: per shot, what is on screen, the caption, the API line shown, the length in seconds), `render_all.sh` (every episode at a given quality into `example/manim/out/`, git-ignored; concatenation of an episode's shots with FFmpeg's concat demuxer; the WebVTT file), `fixtures.py` (every mesh built from [primitives](./primitives.md) or read from `example/data/`: `Bunny.stl`, `example.msh`, `example.stp`, `hook.mdpa`; nothing downloaded at render time; a fixture over `max_faces` is decimated there, or marked for the raster path of 13.3.5).
- **Conventions.** 1080p at 30 frames per second for release (`-qh`), `-ql` in CI; a shot is 5 to 20 seconds and an episode 2 to 5 minutes; one idea per episode; every episode ends on the three lines of Python that reproduce what was shown; no claim without the computation in the scene (13.1.6); determinism: a seeded random generator wherever one is used, so a re-render is the same video.
- **The episodes.** The order is the order to write them; 13.5.1 is the pilot that proves the pipeline end to end, so it is done before the rest is scripted.

| Item | Episode | What it shows | Library surface on screen | Needs |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 13.5.1 | What is a mesh? | points, cells, connectivity; one triangle becoming many; a field on points and on cells | `Mesh(points, cells)`, `point_data`, `cell_data` ([data model](./mesh_data_model.md)) | 13.3.1, 13.3.2 |
| 13.5.2 | Cell types and node ordering | the linear cells, then their quadratic and Lagrange variants; the same hexahedron numbered by VTK, Gmsh and Abaqus, and what goes wrong when the order is mistaken (a folded cell) | [cell types](./cell_types.md), [node ordering](./node_ordering.md), `convert_cells` | 13.4.5 |
| 13.5.3 | One mesh, many files | a mesh written to several formats, what each keeps and loses; text against binary; a conversion as read then write | `read`, `write`, `convert`, [formats](./formats.md), [conformance](./conformance.md) | 13.5.1 |
| 13.5.4 | Curved cells | why order matters on a curved boundary; elevation; tessellation for display | [`convert_cells`](./convert_cells.md), [`tessellate`](./tessellation.md) | 13.2.4 |
| 13.5.5 | Regions and sets | naming parts of a mesh; point, cell and side regions; exploding by region | [regions](./regions.md) | 13.4.4 |
| 13.5.6 | Refine, smooth, decimate | each operation as a morph or a wipe, with the quality histogram moving beside it | [`refine`](./refine.md), [`smooth`](./smooth.md), [`decimate`](./decimate.md) | 13.4.3 |
| 13.5.7 | What makes a good cell | aspect ratio, skewness, scaled Jacobian on one cell deformed live, then on a whole mesh coloured by quality | [mesh quality](./mesh_quality.md), `colorize` | 13.4.5 |
| 13.5.8 | Cutting and contouring | clip, slice, isosurface of a field in a volume | [`clip`](./clip.md), [`slice`](./slice.md), [`isosurface`](./isosurface.md) | 13.2.4 |
| 13.5.9 | Partitioning | splitting a mesh for parallel work; the cut edges; ghost layers | [`partition`](./partition.md), [ghosts](./ghosts.md) | 13.4.4 |
| 13.5.10 | From CAD to a volume mesh | a STEP model, its surface triangulation face by face, the boundary layer, the tetrahedra shrunk apart | [`mesh_cad_surface`](./mesh_cad.md), [`boundary_layer`](./boundary_layer.md), [`tetrahedralize`](./tetrahedralize.md) | 13.2.4, a build with OpenCASCADE |
| 13.5.11 | Results over time | a transient field played back; a mode shape warped | [sequences](./sequences.md), [eigenmodes](./eigenmodes.md) | 13.4.1, 13.4.2 |
| 13.5.12 | The same call in eight languages | one conversion in Python, C++, C, Fortran, Julia, R, Octave and JavaScript, the code morphing between them over the same mesh | the binding pages | 13.4.5 |

- **Per episode, the steps.** (1) The storyboard, reviewed before any scene code: it is cheap to change and the video is not. (2) The scenes, shot by shot, each checked with `manim -ql -s episodes/NN_<slug>.py <Shot>` (last frame only) before a full render. (3) A reference frame per shot added to the frame test of 13.4. (4) The episode's row in `doc/manim.md` with its poster frame, length and the notebook or page it accompanies. (5) A line in `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Done when.** `example/manim/render_all.sh --quality l` renders all twelve from a clean checkout with `pip install "meshioplusplus[manim]"`, each storyboard's shot list matches the scene classes of its episode (a test compares the two lists), and the release carries the archive of 13.1.4.

### 13.6 Tests, CI and documentation {#manim-ci}

**S–M**

- **13.6.1 Gated tests in meshio++.** The manim block of 13.3's tests, skipped by `has_manim()`, plus the payload tests of 13.2 that run everywhere.
- **13.6.2 A `manim` CI job.** In `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, in the pattern of `interop` (line 1814: `needs: [lint]`, Python 3.12, the submodule action): `apt-get install ffmpeg libcairo2-dev libpango1.0-dev pkg-config`, `pip install ".[interop,manim]"`, then the frame tests of 13.4 and one episode at `-ql`, uploading the video as an artifact. Cairo needs no GPU and no display, so the job needs neither a Vulkan driver nor `xvfb`. **Verify first**, before anything else in this section, because the rest depends on it: that a frame renders to the same pixels, or within the tolerance, on two runs and on two runners (fonts and Cairo versions can differ†), and which release range of `manim` works, by a matrix of the oldest that passes and the latest.
- **13.6.3 A ManimGL leg**, allowed to fail, recording whether the helpers of 13.3 and 13.4 port to `manimgl`; its result is an input to 13.3.6.
- **13.6.4 Measurements.** `example/manim/bench_frames.py`: time per frame against face count (10², 10³, 5·10³, 10⁴) for an opaque mesh, a transparent one, a field update per frame, with and without strokes, and for the raster path of 13.3.5; the table goes to `doc/manim.md` with the machine, and sets 13.2.5's default, 13.3.3's choice and whether 13.3.6 is worth starting.
- **13.6.5 Documentation.** `doc/manim.md` (the decisions; installation with the named hint; the payload and what forces a copy, on the layout of [interop](./interop.md); the helpers; the animations with a still each; the episode table; limits: face counts, flat shading, no clip planes, no picking, no `from_manim`), in the sidebar of `doc/.vitepress/config.mts`; a *manim* section in [interop](./interop.md) linking to it; the `interop_layers` figure (`doc/diagrams/figures/flows.py`) gains the fourth payload, regenerated; a cell in `example/python/04_interop.ipynb` that builds the payload and shows a matplotlib rendering of it (no manim in the notebook job); `README.md`; `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Done when.** The `manim` job is green on a pull request that touches `_interop.py`, and `doc/manim.md` embeds the pilot episode.

## 14. The paper {#paper}

*Admission: the written, citable account of what exists, in the form a journal and arXiv take. Nothing here adds a feature; an item that finds the library cannot support a sentence removes the sentence or names the item elsewhere in this file that would make it true. Added in November 2026. Every policy, limit and address of a third party below is quoted from memory and marked †, as in the [specification index](#specification-index): open it before relying on it, since submission rules change more often than file formats.*

**State today, for the whole section.** There is no manuscript and no `paper/` directory. The project is citable as software only: `CITATION.cff` (title "meshio++: Tools for mesh files", five authors, the Zenodo concept DOI `10.5281/zenodo.21384760`, MIT) and no `preferred-citation`. The material a paper would be made of exists as documentation: [architecture](./architecture.md), the [mesh data model](./mesh_data_model.md), [supported formats](./formats.md) and the generated [conformance matrix](./conformance.md), [benchmarks](./benchmarks.md) with their raw CSVs under `benchmark/` (and `benchmark/bench.py`, which already times meshio++ against upstream meshio when `MESHIO_LEGACY_SRC` or an installed `meshio` is present), the [ABI policy](./abi.md), [fuzzing](./fuzzing.md), [spikes](./spikes.md), the binding pages, and the diagrams `doc/diagrams/gen_diagrams.py` generates. `README.md` (near line 1705) credits Simvia's `meshlane` fork for some code and fixtures. None of it is written as an argument, compared with other tools on stated terms, or frozen at a version.
